# UX Shell, commands, collaboration experience, assistance ports, extensions, governance and host integration

**Status:** first draft, 2026-11-06, from specification draft 0.2 ([13 Experience, extensibility and host integration](../spec/13-experience-extensibility.md)), [ADR 0014](../adr/0014-experience-benchmark-and-extensions.md) and the review sections 3.6, 3.7, 5 and 6 ([review](../review/2026-09-18-pitfall-review.md)). Every number is a target to validate, not a measurement. The story format and field values are those of the [README](README.md).

**Scope.** The parity profile as a maintained artifact; the shell (header, toolbars, panes, status bar, modes); the command system (one registry for both toolbar layouts, context menus, the mini toolbar, command search, access keys, shortcuts, the embedding API and extensions); the collaboration experience (presence, comment threads, mentions, assigned tasks, reactions, suggestion review tools, the share button, output restrictions); the proofing and assistant ports; the extension model with trust tiers and custom content; built-in themes, localisation, the accessibility checker and the accessibility conformance report; the budget stories of 13 section 9; governance and administration; host integration (the integration guide, the reference host and its compose file, service calls on behalf of a user).

**Left to other epics (README section 2.3).** The embedding contract itself (PRO); the editing surface, suggestion mode, the region guard, the recovery journal and the theming mechanism (FE); catch-up and the history panel (HIS); range mapping of comments in the authority (SYN); who may comment, suggest or restrict (PRM); the fill-mode interface and field panes (FRW); the page-view panes (FE); the measurement harness and CI gates (FND); tagged PDF (EXP); retention mechanics (HIS). Where a story here shows or triggers one of those, it says so and depends on it.

**Design.** Visual and interaction design is left to a designer (13 section 13). Stories describe what a person can do, never how it looks. Word for the web is a feature checklist only.

## Parity profile

### UX-001 Maintain the parity profile as a published artifact

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §1, §2; ADR 0014; review §3.7, §5; 11 risk 12 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want the parity profile kept as a versioned, published artifact, so that scope questions are settled by one contract and parity is judged by what a user can do.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the repository, when the profile is opened, then it lists every capability of 13 section 2 in exactly one tier (built in, through ports and extensions, non-goal) with its phase and the identifiers of the stories that deliver it.
- [x] Given a non-goal of the profile (SmartArt, charts, shapes and text boxes, ink, mail merge, macros, table formulas, researcher-style lookup, transcription, offline-first editing), when a story or change proposes it, then it is refused, or the profile and the non-goals in PRODUCT.md are changed by an explicit owner decision in the same change set.
- [x] Given a command added to the built-in registry (UX-003), when the build runs, then it fails if no profile row lists the command's capability.
- [x] Given any row, when it is read, then it states what a user can do and prescribes no look, arrangement or name from another product.
- [x] Given a release, when the integration guide (UX-090) is published, then it links to the profile version that matches that release.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/parity/profile.ts is the source and packages/ui/src/parity/build.ts the checks; packages/ui/scripts/generate.ts writes spec/parity/parity-profile.json and spec/parity/parity-profile.md (phases derived from the stories); packages/ui/test/parity.test.ts: the artifacts equal a fresh generation, every item of 13 §2 is covered in its own tier only, a non-goal with a delivering story, a command delivering a non-goal and a change of the non-goals without an updated decision record are refused, a built-in command whose capability has no row fails, and rows naming another product's design or prescribing a look fail. Criterion 5 (lane w3, 2026-11-15): `apps/reference-host/docs/integration-guide.md`, bullet "Parity (UX-001)", links by a relative path to `spec/parity/parity-profile.md` and states its version and date; `apps/reference-host/test/integration-guide.profile-version.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js; `pnpm exec vitest run --project node apps/reference-host`: 155 passed, 1 skipped) fails when the link does not open the generated profile of the same checkout, when the stated version or date differs from `spec/parity/parity-profile.json` or from the heading of the linked file, or when the guide names a second profile version.

**Notes:** R14 requires a "published parity profile". Phase inferred (1a): it must exist before the registry so that risk 12 (scope creep) has its mitigation from the start. The build check is a proposed mechanism and may be relaxed to a report. Rows without a delivering story in any epic are gaps to report, for example symbols, watermark, table sorting, columns and breaks, captions, bookmarks, equations and templates, which the profile lists as built in but this backlog assigns to other epics or later phases. Built on 2026-11-06 (ADR 0041, section 8): `spec/parity/` lists four gaps (cropping of pictures; text prediction, translate and read aloud have no port or story; dictation has no test of its own). The non-goal lists of 13 §2 and PRODUCT.md differ: 13 §2 also names text boxes, ink, table formulas, researcher-style lookup and transcription, and PRODUCT.md also names product-level non-goals; the profile follows 13 §2 (owner question). Criterion 5 (2026-11-15): the guide and the profile are in one repository, so a release, being one commit, holds one version of each; the guide states the profile version in a relative link and a test keeps the two equal. No release has been cut and the guide is not hosted anywhere yet (versions are 0.0.0, no tags). If the guide is later published outside the repository, the relative link must be rewritten to the profile of the same tag by whatever publishes it; that step belongs to the release work and is an open question for the owner.

### UX-002 Report parity status with every release

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | S | should | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §2; 00 R14 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-001

As a maintainer, I want a parity report generated for every release, so that "same features as Word for the web" is measured capability by capability.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a release candidate, when the report is generated, then every profile row shows one of shipped, partial, planned (with phase) or not planned, derived from the state of its stories.
- [x] Given a row marked shipped, when the report is built, then it links to an automated test that exercises the capability as a user would (Playwright or unit), not to a screenshot.
- [x] Given a capability in the tier "through ports and extensions", when the report is built, then it names the port or the reference extension that demonstrates it, or states that none exists.
- [x] Given the report, when it is published with the release notes, then it is judged by capability only: no row is marked "partial" or "shipped" because of appearance.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/parity-status.test.ts (the four states of `rowStatus` in `packages/ui/src/parity/status.ts` from the stories' criteria; a planned row carries the earliest phase of its stories; non-goals and rows with no known story are not planned; a row with a gap is never shipped; property tests over the published profile rows and the monotone order of the states; `parseCriteria` reads the ticked and total criteria of each story from the epic files). Criterion 3 (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/src/parity/demonstrators.ts gives each row of the tier a demonstrator, which is `none` with a reason for all eight rows (no proofing port, reference assistant or reference extension ships; checked against packages/ and apps/); `parityReport` puts it on those rows and reports a row with no entry as a problem; packages/ui/test/parity-demonstrators.test.ts. Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/parity-demonstrators.test.ts packages/ui/test/parity-status.test.ts packages/ui/test/parity.test.ts`: 31 passed. Run of 2026-11-13: `pnpm vitest run packages/ui` (25 files, 275 tests passed). Lane w3 (2026-11-15), criteria 1 to 4: `packages/ui/src/parity/report.ts` (`buildParityReport`) builds `PARITY-REPORT.md` and `parity-report.json` with the state of every row, the tests of each shipped row from `packages/ui/src/parity/tests.ts`, the demonstrator of each "through ports and extensions" row, and the statement that states come from story criteria only; it refuses a shipped row with no test, a link to a missing file and a link that is not a `*.test.ts` or `*.spec.ts` file. `packages/ui/scripts/parity-report.ts` (`pnpm --filter @docedit/ui parity:report --release <version> --out <dir>`) writes the files, and `scripts/release/release.ts` (step 8b) runs it into the directory of the release notes and lists both files in the manifest. `packages/ui/test/parity-report.test.ts` (11 tests: the four states, the links, the three refusals, the report of this repository with every shipped row linked to existing test files, every ports-and-extensions row with its demonstrator text, and the script run into a temporary directory). Run: `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/ui/test/parity-report.test.ts packages/ui/test/parity-status.test.ts packages/ui/test/parity-demonstrators.test.ts scripts/release` (14 files, 92 tests passed). A dry run of the release on this machine (`node scripts/release/release.ts --dry-run --out <scratch>`, Windows 11, Node.js 22.18) wrote `PARITY-REPORT.md` and `parity-report.json` beside `RELEASE-NOTES.md` with the step "parity report" ok; the report had 68 rows: 2 shipped (`toolbar`, `sharing`), 50 partial, 3 planned, 13 not planned. The dry run as a whole failed on its pin check (`infra/webhook-egress-policy.ps1`, an image not pinned by digest), which is not part of this story.

**Notes:** Phase inferred (1b, first usable release). Round 7 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 ticked on the report function. Not wired: no release job calls `parityReport` yet and the report is not written to `spec/parity/` (release pipeline, FND-012; generated artifacts are outside `packages/ui`). Criterion 2 is open: a profile row names its stories but not the automated tests that exercise a shipped capability, so a shipped row cannot yet link to a test; the profile needs that field (owner or PRO/FND decision, not made here). Criterion 3 (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13) is ticked: `demonstrators.ts` states the demonstrator of each "through ports and extensions" row, and every one is `none` today. A row that gains a port or a reference extension needs its entry changed in that file. Criterion 4 is a rule for whoever publishes the report, not a check the code can make beyond the state rule above. Audit 2026-11-13: criteria 1 and 3 are unticked because `parityReport`, `parseCriteria` and `DEMONSTRATORS` are imported only by their own tests (no script, release job or export calls them, so no report can be generated); tick again once something produces the report. Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criteria 1 to 4 are ticked again; the audit's objection is met, as `packages/ui/scripts/parity-report.ts` and step 8b of `scripts/release/release.ts` now produce the report. The report is a release artefact written beside the release notes; it is not committed under `spec/parity/`, because it changes with every ticked criterion. Proposed, not decided by the owner: the tests of a shipped row are kept in `packages/ui/src/parity/tests.ts`, beside the profile as `demonstrators.ts` is, so the published profile's shape does not change; moving them into the profile row is still open. Criterion 4: the code can only derive states from story criteria and say so in the report; no release has been published yet, so the rule has been exercised on a dry run only. `scripts/release/release.ts` belongs to FND; the change there is one added step.

## Command registry and command surfaces

### UX-003 Command registry that drives every command surface

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | L | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §4; 02 §4, §5.1; ADR 0014 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** FE-026

As a maintainer, I want one command registry, so that the compact and full toolbars, context menus, the mini toolbar, command search, access keys, `exec()` and extensions never drift apart.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a command entry with `id`, `label`, `icon`, `group`, `keys`, `accessKey`, `enabled` and `active` (the shape in 13 section 4), when it is registered, then the compact toolbar, the full toolbar, context menus, the mini toolbar, command search, access keys, `exec()` and extensions all read that entry from the same registry instance.
- [x] Given an entry whose `enabled` or `active` names a query on the document API (for example `canEditSelection`, `isBold`), when the selection or document changes, then the state is evaluated once, through the document API, and no surface evaluates it by itself.
- [x] Given an entry with an unknown query name or an `id` that is already registered, when it is registered, then registration fails with an error that names the entry, and nothing is overwritten silently.
- [x] Given several state changes within one animation frame, when the registry publishes, then at most one interface update is produced for that frame (02 section 4) and `de-command-state` lists only the commands whose state changed.
- [x] Given a command registered after start-up (as extensions do), when it is registered, then every surface shows it without a reload.
- [x] Given a locale, when a label is shown, then it is resolved from the string tables (UX-064) and not taken from a literal in the entry.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/commands/registry.ts, builtin.ts, queries.ts; packages/ui/test/registry.test.ts (each query asked once per change through CoreApi.query, surfaces read only the snapshot; duplicate identifiers, aliases, unknown queries and literal labels refused with the entry named; registration and removal at run time; labels are keys of packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts); packages/ui/e2e/toolbar.spec.ts (a command registered and one removed at run time appear and disappear without a reload) and e2e/strings.spec.ts (labels follow the locale), in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Criterion 1 in part (lane V-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-003-registry-surfaces.spec.ts, one entry registered at run time is read by the full toolbar, the context menu, command search, the access-key mode and `exec`; passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Criterion 4 (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-003-command-state.spec.ts, 'a burst of selection changes in one frame fires one event that lists only what changed' (three selection changes in one task give one `de-command-state` event in one frame, listing `format.italic` and not `format.bold` or `edit.selectAll`), passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`, port 56251). The number of events is the measure of the interface update here; the drawn frame of the shell is covered by packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts (UX-072 criterion 4). Criterion 1 (lane w3, 2026-11-15): packages/ui/e2e/ux-003-w3-all-surfaces.spec.ts, run with Playwright against the built harness on a free local port (`DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`; 2 tests, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, together with ux-003-registry-surfaces.spec.ts: 9 of 9). 'the compact toolbar, the mini toolbar, the context menu, command search, access keys, exec and the full toolbar read the same entry': one entry registered at run time in the `format` group is a button of the compact toolbar, an item of the mini toolbar, of the context menu and of command search, has its access-key tip, runs through `exec` and through its toolbar button, is in the `format` group of the full toolbar after the setting changes, and leaves every surface on `unregister`. 'a command registered by an extension is in the same registry as the built-in ones': a command registered through the extension API is listed with the built-in commands, is held by the compact toolbar (shown in its "More" menu at that width), is found by command search, runs through `exec`, and is gone when the extension is removed.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 1 is ticked on the browser run above. The reading applied, proposed and not decided by the owner: a surface "reads" an entry when it takes it from the one registry by the surface's own rule and shows it when the rule admits it. The earlier remark that the compact row takes the phase 1a commands only is not what the code does: `toolbarCommands` (packages/ui/src/shell/toolbar-groups.ts) gives the compact row every offered toolbar command, and a run-time entry is shown there (toolbar.spec.ts already showed this). The mini toolbar admits the `format` commands that change content (UX-010), so the probe entry is registered in that group. Whether an entry of another group, such as an extension's, should also be offered by the mini toolbar is still the owner's question; it belongs to UX-010 and does not change that the mini toolbar reads the registry. The sample entry in 13 section 4 has a literal `label`, a `group` of the form `home.font` and no field for synonyms. The label must be a string key (13 section 8: all interface strings are external); command search (UX-011) needs synonyms. Both are open questions below. The group names are examples only. Built on 2026-11-06 (ADR 0041, sections 1 and 2): labels are string keys; synonyms wait for UX-011. Criterion 1 stays open until the full toolbar, context menu, mini toolbar, command search, access keys and extensions exist (phases 1b and 2); the compact toolbar, the header and `exec` already read the one registry. Criterion 4 is met in the registry (one publication per frame with the changed identifiers, `bindCommandStateEvent` raises `de-command-state`), and is ticked once the element raises the event from the shared registry (B2). Round 2 (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/registry-surfaces.test.ts checks criterion 1 at the logic level: one registered entry, read from one registry instance by the toolbar groups, the context menu, the mini toolbar, command search, access keys and `exec`, and removed from all of them by `unregister`. The rendered full toolbar and mini toolbar are not run, and `bindCommandStateEvent` is still not bound by the element (`packages/element` does not raise `de-command-state` from this registry), so criteria 1 and 4 stay unticked. Round 3 (packet U, 2026-11-13): still unticked. A search of `packages/element/src` finds no call to `bindCommandStateEvent`; the element's `events.ts` documents `de-command-state`, but nothing raises it from the registry. Needed from packet B2 (element), not changed here. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): the element now raises `de-command-state` from the registry (packages/element/src/session.ts, `commandState`), and criterion 4 is ticked on the browser run in its Evidence line. Criterion 1 stays open. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): the full toolbar, the context menu, command search, the access-key mode and exec read one run-time entry (ux-003-registry-surfaces.spec.ts). The compact row takes the phase 1a commands only (UX-006) and the mini toolbar the `format` group only (UX-010), so an extension entry is not shown there by design; the owner needs to say whether "read" means "shown" for those two surfaces.

### UX-004 Commands respect the user's current permissions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Viewer |

**Spec:** 13 §4; PRODUCT principle 2; 07 §7 · **Requirements:** R3, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, PRM-004, PRM-006, FE-036, FE-038

As a person with limited rights, I want to see which commands I may use before I try, so that I am not surprised by a rejected change.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a user with only `view` in the scope of the selection, when the toolbar renders, then every command that changes content is disabled, carries an accessible reason, and `queryCommand` reports it as not enabled.
- [x] Given the caret moves from a region the user may edit into one they may not, when the selection changes, then command states update within one animation frame.
- [x] Given a `perm.changed` that removes edit rights in the current scope, when it arrives, then the affected commands become disabled without a reload and the change is announced (FE-107).
- [x] Given a user with `suggest` but not `edit`, when they run a formatting command, then the command is enabled and its result is a suggestion, as the suggestion mode of the editing core produces.
- [x] Given a command the permissions do not allow, when it is offered by command search or an extension, then it cannot be run from there and its unavailability is explained rather than silent.
- [x] Given an extension command (UX-051) whose `enabled` names a permission query, when permissions change, then it follows the same rules as built-in commands.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/commands/registry.ts and access.ts; packages/ui/test/permissions.test.ts (view only: content commands disabled with a reason, state() reports not enabled, exec refuses with no-access; every limit has its reason; caret moves and permission changes; a command registered at run time follows the same rules); packages/ui/e2e/toolbar.spec.ts (aria-disabled with the reason as accessible description, states updated by the end of the frame in which the core notifies, a permission change in the same scope disables commands and is announced in the shell's live region), in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. queryCommand is registry.state (ADR 0041). Criterion 5 (round 3, ui packet, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/command-search.test.ts 'lists a command the access does not allow with its reason, and does not run it' (a search hit with `runnable: false` and `reason.access.view`; `runSearchHit` and `activateFirstHit` return `no-access` and the core's run is never called); packages/ui/test/permissions.test.ts 'cannot be run from another surface when the access does not allow it, and say why' (a command registered at run time, as an extension does, is refused by `exec` with `no-access`). Run 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/command-search.test.ts packages/ui/test/permissions.test.ts`. Criterion 4 (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/engine/test/ux-015-mode-commits.test.ts, 'bold on a selection is enabled and recorded as a formatting suggestion' (a `suggest`-only user with a selection runs `bold`; the change is recorded with an `fmt` mark and queued as a commit; Node.js, the real DocumentCore). Run: `pnpm exec vitest run test/ux-015-mode-commits.test.ts` in packages/engine (4 tests passed).

**Notes:** The server remains the authority (principle 2); this story is the "see before you try" half. Rejection explanations belong to PRM. Criterion 4: formatting commands stay enabled with `suggest` (tested), but the result is a suggestion only once the core's suggestion mode exists (FE-042, phase 2). Criterion 5: `exec` refuses with the reason (tested); command search (UX-011) and extensions (UX-051) arrive in phase 2. Round 3 (ui packet, 2026-11-13): command search (UX-011) and the run-time registration of an extension-style command are built, so criterion 5 is ticked on the unit tests named in its Evidence. The full extension surface (UX-051) stays phase 2. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 is ticked at the core (see its Evidence); the shell's menu state for suggest-only users is not run in a browser.

### UX-005 Drive the editor from a host-built toolbar

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 13 §4; 05 §8.1; 02 §5.1 · **Requirements:** R6, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, PRO-074, PRO-085, PRO-087

As an integrating developer, I want to build my own toolbar from `queryCommand` and `exec`, so that the editor fits into my application's interface.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `toolbar="none"`, when the editor loads, then no built-in toolbar is rendered, and the editing surface, panes and status bar still work.
- [x] Given a selection that is bold, when the host calls `queryCommand("format.bold")`, then it reports the same enabled and active state as the built-in toolbar would show.
- [x] Given an enabled command, when the host calls `exec`, then the document changes exactly as if the user had used the built-in toolbar, passing the same guards as typing.
- [x] Given a command that `queryCommand` reports as not enabled, or an unknown identifier, when the host calls `exec`, then the document does not change and the caller is told that nothing ran.
- [x] Given command states change, when `de-command-state` fires, then its detail lists the commands whose state changed, so the host toolbar updates without polling.
- [ ] Given the reference host's example toolbar (shown by the page glue, UX-093) beside the built-in one, when an automated test walks a sequence of selections, then the two agree on enabled and active for every command in the sequence.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/host-toolbar.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): a bold selection reports the same enabled and active state through `state` and through the toolbar's snapshot (criterion 2); a content command refused in view mode or without edit rights runs nothing and returns `mode` or `no-access` (criterion 3, the registry's guards only); an unknown identifier and a command its query disables run nothing and say so (criterion 4); the `de-command-state` detail lists the changed identifiers and no content (criterion 5). Also packages/ui/test/registry.test.ts, 'raises de-command-state as a composed, bubbling event' and 'exec (05 §8.1)'. Toolbar.svelte reads the same registry (`view`, `exec`). Browser (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts, 'a host-built toolbar (UX-005)', 'with toolbar="none" there is no built-in toolbar; page, panes and status bar work' (criterion 1): passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`, own port). Some first attempts failed on a missing trace file in the shared output folder and passed on retry. Criterion 3 (lane V-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13, see Notes): packages/ui/e2e/ux-005-exec-parity.spec.ts, a bold from the built-in toolbar and a bold from `exec` record the same harness runs and core calls, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 2 to 5 on the product element):** the element's `queryCommand` and `exec` now take the identifiers of the shell's command registry (`format.bold`, `edit.undo`), because `mountEditor` (packages/element/src/editor.ts) creates the one registry, passes it to the shell and gives it to `hostApi(core, registry)` (packages/element/src/host-api.ts); a name the registry does not know goes to the core, and a name neither knows runs nothing (the core's throw is caught) and `queryCommand` answers disabled and inactive. `de-command-state` is checked on each change of the registry's states as well as on each revision, and its detail lists the registry's commands by identifier (`{ name, enabled, active }`) beside the core's. Node.js: packages/element/test/host-api.test.ts, "exec and queryCommand over the command registry (UX-005)" (4 tests: a registry identifier answered from the registry; a registry command that is not enabled runs nothing and leaves the document as it was; core names still work; an unknown identifier runs nothing, with and without a registry), run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/element/test/host-api.test.ts` (passed). Browser: packages/element/e2e/ux-005-host-commands.spec.ts (3 tests; the embed page with the in-memory backend and the real engine; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 9 passed with `--workers=1 --retries=0`, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=58301`): over a walk of five selections (caret in plain text, a plain selection, the same selection made bold, the caret back in plain text, the caret in bold text) the host's `queryCommand("format.bold")` and the state of the built-in toolbar's Bold button (`aria-pressed`, `aria-disabled`) agree at every step, and the host reads `active` false, false, true, false, true (criterion 2); `exec("format.bold")` makes the selection bold in the document and the toolbar button shows it (criterion 3); a viewer's `exec("format.bold")` returns false with `queryCommand` disabled, and an unknown identifier returns false with `queryCommand` disabled and inactive, and the document is unchanged (criterion 4); the `de-command-state` detail contains `format.bold` with `active: true` after the bold (criterion 5). A first run of the same file in the three browsers with parallel workers failed 5 of 9 on timeouts under load; the serial run passed.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): the audit finding below is closed for criteria 2 to 5: the host path now reaches the registry (see the Evidence above). Criterion 6 stays open: it asks for the reference host's example toolbar (`apps/reference-host/src/lib/glue/host-toolbar.ts`, which already uses `format.bold`, `edit.undo` and the like) beside the built-in one in a walk, and that runs on the demo stack (Podman compose with PostgreSQL), which was not started in this lane; the stale `test.fail` of `apps/reference-host/e2e/host.spec.ts`, 'the host toolbar uses queryCommand, exec and de-command-state', says the element offers none of this and should be removed when that run is made. The detail of `de-command-state` is `{ commands: [{ name, enabled, active }] }` for both the core's names and the registry's identifiers (one shape; the open question of the audit is answered by the shape FE-028 already tests). The return shape of `exec` for a refused command is for PRO to fix (open question). Round 1 (lane ui-5, 2026-11-13): criteria 2, 4 and 5 ticked on the Node.js tests above. Criterion 1 needs a browser run with `toolbar="none"`, and criterion 6 needs the reference host's example toolbar (UX-093) in a browser run; both stay open. Criterion 3 stays open: the registry applies the guards, but that the document then changes exactly as typing does is the core's and is not tested here. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 ticked on the browser run above. Criterion 6 stays open: the harness's `hostToolbar` is test glue in e2e/harness/main.ts, not UX-093's reference host, which is outside this lane's range and not checked here. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 is tested at the seam between the shell and the core (ux-005-exec-parity.spec.ts). The harness core is not a real document, so that the document itself changes as typing does stays the core's, and criterion 3 stays open. Audit 2026-11-13: criteria 2, 4 and 5 are unticked because the element's host `queryCommand` and `exec` call the core (packages/element/src/host-api.ts), not the ui registry the cited tests exercise, so `core.queryCommand("format.bold")` throws a TypeError (as it does for any unknown name) and `bindCommandStateEvent` is called only by the e2e harness; the element's own `de-command-state` (detail of `{name, enabled, active}` objects, packages/element/src/selection-events.ts, FE-028) and `exec` (PRO-085) have their own tests and can be cited once the host path accepts registry identifiers and one detail shape is chosen.

### UX-006 Compact toolbar

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §4, §12; 11 §3; 02 §3 (parts) · **Requirements:** R1 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-004

As an author, I want a compact single-row toolbar, so that I can format text and use the commands of the demo without the full layout.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `toolbar="compact"`, when the editor loads, then a single-row toolbar is generated from the registry with the commands registered for phase 1a, and the element exposes the `toolbar` part.
- [x] Given a toolbar control, when it is activated by pointer or keyboard, then it runs the same command path as `exec`.
- [x] Given focus enters the toolbar, when the arrow keys are pressed, then focus moves between controls (the toolbar is one tab stop), Escape returns focus to the editing surface, and focus is always visible.
- [x] Given a command is active at the caret, when the toolbar renders, then the active state is exposed to assistive technology as well as visually.
- [x] Given the container is narrower than the row, when the toolbar lays out, then the commands that do not fit stay reachable and none is lost.
- [x] Given a command is removed from the registry, when the toolbar updates, then its control disappears (nothing is hard-coded in the toolbar).

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/components/Toolbar.svelte, packages/ui/src/shell/toolbar-layout.ts, packages/ui/src/styles/shell.css; packages/ui/e2e/toolbar.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: one row generated from the registry with the phase 1a commands, ::part(toolbar) styled from the host page; pointer, Enter and Space run the registry's exec path; one tab stop with arrow keys, Home, End and Escape to the editing surface, visible focus; aria-pressed and a visual difference for active commands; at 420 px the rest is in a More menu, every command once, run by keyboard; removed and added commands; packages/ui/test/shell-logic.test.ts (fitting).

**Notes:** Overflow behaviour is the minimum a single-row layout needs; its design is the designer's.

### UX-007 Full toolbar with task groups and contextual groups

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §3, §4; 11 §4 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-006

As an author, I want a full toolbar with commands grouped by task and groups that appear for the thing I am editing, so that the commands I need are close.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `toolbar="full"`, when the editor loads, then commands appear grouped by task (formatting, inserting, page layout, references, review and view are the examples in the spec), and each command appears in exactly one group, taken from its `group`.
- [x] Given a selection inside a table, a picture, a header or footer story, or a field, when the selection changes, then the matching contextual group appears; given the selection leaves it, then the group disappears; in neither case does focus move.
- [x] Given a command is registered, when both layouts are in use, then it appears in the compact and the full layout from the same entry.
- [x] Given the `toolbar` attribute is changed between `compact` and `full` while a document is open, when the layout switches, then selection, scroll position and unsent work are untouched.
- [x] Given the groups are presented as tabs, when the keyboard is used, then the tabs follow the standard tabs pattern (arrow keys between tabs, Tab into the active group's commands) and each tab and group has an accessible name.
- [x] Given the view group, when it renders, then it offers only the views the build supports (`web`, `read`, and `print` once it exists).

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/layout-views.test.ts (criterion 4, Node.js, 2026-11-13, 6 tests passed: `layoutViews` in `commands/builtin.ts` gives `web` when the core lists no views, offers `print` only when the core lists it, keeps the shell order, drops unknown names, and a property over every subset registers one `view.layout.*` command per offered view, which sets that view; App.svelte registers the group through `layoutViews`). Not claimed: the group's rendering in a browser was not run. packages/ui/test/toolbar-groups.test.ts 'every command is in exactly one group, the one its entry names' (criterion 1, property test over the built-in commands, both access levels and the contexts) and 'one entry, the same in the compact row and the full layout' (criterion 3, property test, plus a command registered at run time shown in both layouts as the entry that was registered). Logic in packages/ui/src/shell/toolbar-groups.ts; `toolbarGroups` now orders the groups itself (task order, then contextual) whatever the input order. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13), browser runs in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`): packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts 'groups commands by task, each in the one group its entry names' (criterion 1 in the rendered layout), 'follows the tabs pattern and names every tab and group' (criterion 5), 'shows a contextual group while the selection is in its object, without moving focus' (criterion 2 for a table and for a header and footer story), and packages/ui/e2e/ux-007-contextual-groups.spec.ts (criterion 2 for a picture and for a field, the group shown and gone with focus on the surface; and criterion 6 in part: a switch between full and compact keeps the selection and the surface, with one mount). Criterion 6 (lane V-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-007-layout-switch.spec.ts 'selection, page scroll position and unsent input are untouched by a switch', run in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57250 DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`, 3 passed). The harness body is made 4000 px tall so that the document scrolls; the scroll position is read after the search field is opened and typed in, then after a switch to `compact` and back to `full`; the selection, the editing surface and the query typed into command search (not run) are compared; one `mountBody` call.

**Notes:** The grouping and appearance are the product's own (13 section 3) and a designer's job. Contextual groups for fields depend on FRW's field commands. Round 2, packet U (2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 3 ticked on the tests above. Criterion 2 stays open: the group must appear and disappear with the selection and focus must not move, and the focus part is the component's and was not run in a browser. Criteria 4 to 6 are the component's (the attribute switch, the tabs pattern) or need the view commands the build does not have yet. Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 ticked. The view commands exist (`view.layout.*`); the view group offers the views the core lists (`layoutViews`), so `print` waits for the core. Criteria 5 and 6 stay open (the component's). Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 5 ticked on the browser runs in the Evidence line. Criterion 6 ticked by lane V-UX-001-015 on the three-browser run named in Evidence. Two limits: the unsent input checked is the command search query, the only unsent input the harness has; the comment composer draft and the fill bar are not covered. The scroll position is read after the switch back to `full`; during the switch to `compact` the document moved by about 34 px in one run (no search open), and that intermediate move is not judged here. In a draft run the position read 0 after the search opened, before the switch; that was not isolated further and the final test measures after the search opens.

### UX-008 Context menu

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §4, §12; 02 §15 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-004

As an author, I want a context menu that offers the commands relevant to what I clicked, so that I can work without hunting through the toolbar.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a pointer context click, or the keyboard context-menu key or its documented alternative, when it happens in the editing surface, then a menu generated from the registry opens at the pointer or the caret with the commands relevant to the target (text, table, picture, field, comment).
- [x] Given the menu is open, when the keyboard is used, then arrow keys move between items, Escape closes it, focus returns to the editing surface, and every item has an accessible name.
- [x] Given a command the permissions do not allow, when the menu lists it, then it is disabled with a reason (UX-004).
- [x] Given an extension contributes items (UX-051), when the menu opens on their declared targets, then the items appear.
- [ ] Given a misspelled word and a present proofing port, when the menu opens on it, then the port's suggestions appear in the menu (UX-042), because this menu replaces the browser's.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/context-menu.test.ts (round 5, packet U, 2026-11-13): the menu's commands are the offered entries that name one of its targets, for any set of targets and both accesses (fast-check property); a viewer's bold on text is listed disabled with `no-access` and `reason.access.view`, and every disabled item carries a reason key (property); an extension entry registered with `contextMenu: ['table']` appears on a table target only and is dropped when removed. Run with the fake core, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui`: 236 passed. Criterion 2 (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-008-context-menu.spec.ts (every item has an accessible name, arrow keys visit every item in order, Escape closes and returns focus to the editing surface) passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`). Criterion 1 (lane V-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-008-context-targets.spec.ts (a picture, a field and a comment target each list their extension command on a right-click, and the text menu does not) passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (same Playwright command, port 57250). Criterion 1, the keyboard path (lane w3, 2026-11-15): `EditorCore.caretRect` (packages/engine/src/view/web.ts) gives the caret's rectangle from the browser's selection, and packages/element/src/editor.ts forwards it to the shell. packages/element/e2e/ux-008-caret-menu.spec.ts, in the product's element with the real core: with the caret at the start of one paragraph Shift+F10 opens the menu, and with the caret at the end of another the Menu key does; each time the menu's corner is within 3 px of the caret, whose place the test measures itself from a DOM range on the neighbouring character, and the two menus are in different places. Run on Windows 11 with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=47931 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts ux-008-caret-menu fe-020-021-context-menu --retries 0 --repeat-each 2`: 24 passed (three tests, twice each, in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit).

**Notes:** Round 5 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 4 ticked on the unit tests above. Criteria 1 and 2 stay open: the opening, the keys and the focus are the component's, and no browser run was made here. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 is ticked on the browser run in its Evidence line. Criterion 1 is ticked (lane V-UX-001-015): packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts opens the menu at the pointer and at the caret for text, and lists a table extension item on a table target, and packages/ui/e2e/ux-008-context-targets.spec.ts covers the picture, field and comment targets. Criterion 5 needs the proofing port (UX-041), which is not built. What the menu does when there is no proofing port is not specified (02 section 15, 13 section 13): see open questions. The menu lives inside the shadow root (02 section 3). Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 unticked, because the keyboard path opens "at the caret" from `core.caretRect`, which neither the product's element nor the browser harness provides, so the menu falls back to the bottom edge of the page rect, and no test asserts a caret position (only the pointer position is asserted). Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 1 is ticked again. The product's core now provides `caretRect`, and a browser test asserts the caret position for both keys (Evidence). The rectangle is read from the browser's selection, not the view's state: in Edge the state had not yet followed a Home or End key when the next key asked for the menu, and the menu opened at the old caret. The mini toolbar (UX-010) uses the same rectangle, so in the element it now sits at the caret too. Criterion 5 stays open: UX-041 and UX-042 have no criterion ticked, and no proofing port sends findings to the shell, so there is nothing for the menu to show.

### UX-009 Format painter

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §12 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-004, FE-127, FE-128, FE-129

As an author, I want to copy the formatting of one passage onto another, so that I can make passages match without redoing each setting.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a selection with character and paragraph formatting, when the author arms the format painter and then selects a target, then the target takes the source's formatting and keeps its own content.
- [x] Given the target is in a scope where the user has `suggest` but not `edit`, when the formatting is applied, then it is recorded as a formatting suggestion, not a direct change.
- [x] Given the target is in a scope the user may not change, when the formatting is applied, then nothing changes and the user is told why.
- [x] Given only a keyboard, when the painter is armed, applied and cancelled, then every step works without a pointer; the armed state is exposed to assistive technology and Escape disarms it without a change.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/share-and-painter.test.ts (round 5, packet U, 2026-11-13): in a viewer's scope, `format.applyFormat` is disabled with `no-access` and `reason.access.view` (a string of the source table), `exec` returns `no-access`, the core's `runNamed` is never called and the selection does not change; in an editable scope the same command runs the core's `pasteFormat` (fake core, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui`). Not claimed: criteria 1 and 2 (the core's copy and the recorded suggestion). Criterion 4 (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts, 'the format painter (UX-009)': armed by the keyboard, the target applied with Enter, Escape disarms with no change, aria-pressed and the live status are checked; passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (Playwright, `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit`).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 to 4 on the product):** the editing core now has the painter. `copyFormat` and `pasteFormat` are core commands and `canCopyFormat` and `canPasteFormat` are core queries (packages/engine/src/format-painter.ts, `DocumentCore` in core.ts, api.ts); the element declares them (packages/element/src/editor.ts: `commandNames` and `runNamed`), so the shell registers `format.painter` and `format.applyFormat` in the product. Node.js: packages/engine/test/ux-009-format-painter.test.ts (9 tests, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/ux-009-format-painter.test.ts`, all passed): bold, colour and size plus alignment and left indent taken from a whole paragraph and put on a whole target paragraph, words unchanged, source unchanged, one undo step undoes all (criterion 1); a part of a paragraph takes the character marks only; the formatting replaces what the target had; a caret sets the stored marks so the next typed text carries them; paste is unavailable before a copy; a user with `suggest` only (two cores against backend-mock, which runs the real authority) gets `fmt` suggestion marks naming the user on the text and on the paragraph, and the server holds no direct change (criterion 2); a region the user may only read, and a reader, get no change, `queryCommand('pasteFormat').enabled` false and `run` false (criterion 3, the core's refusal). Browser: packages/element/e2e/ux-009-format-painter.spec.ts (the embed page, the real engine and shell; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 9 passed with `--workers=1 --retries=0`, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=58301`; one cold-start failure in Chromium on a first run, 6 of 6 passed on a repeat): Ctrl+Shift+C arms from a bold selection with `aria-pressed` true on the painter button, a keyboard-only move to another paragraph and Enter make its text bold and keep its words and turn the painter off (criteria 1 and 4); Escape turns it off with no change (criterion 4); for a viewer, Enter changes nothing and the shell's status says "This document is read-only." (criterion 3). The painter's tests of the shell with the harness core still pass in the three browsers (packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts, 'the format painter (UX-009)', 9 passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=58311`).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): the audit finding below is closed: the core now provides `copyFormat`, `pasteFormat`, `canCopyFormat` and `canPasteFormat`, so the painter is registered in the product. Decisions taken here, proposed and not from the spec (the spec does not say which properties are copied): the character formatting copied is the direct marks that clear formatting removes plus the character style; the paragraph properties copied are the style, alignment, indents, spacing, line spacing and rule, and the keep and widow flags; links, comments, suggestion marks, preserved runs, lists, tabs, borders and shading are not copied. As in Word, paragraph properties are taken from a collapsed selection or from one that covers the whole paragraph, and put on the paragraphs a collapsed selection is in or a selection covers whole; a part of a paragraph takes the character formatting alone. Under suggestion mode only the style and alignment are copied to a paragraph, because those are the paragraph changes a block suggestion records (FE-044). A fix in the shell (packages/ui/src/App.svelte): a pointer gesture applied the painter from the core's selection view as it was before the gesture reached the editor, so a click on the target painted the source again; it now waits for the view to change, up to 120 ms. Not run: a pointer drag in a browser against the product (the harness test of the shell covers the pointer). Round 5 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 ticked on the unit tests above. Which properties are copied is not specified; the minimum is the formatting carried by the selection's run properties and paragraph properties (03 section 4.5). Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13), checked, not changed: the product does not offer the painter. `commandNames` in packages/element/src/editor.ts is `['insertField']` only, the engine's query list has no `canCopyFormat`, and no `copyFormat` or `pasteFormat` exists in packages/element or packages/engine, so App.svelte does not register `format.painter` in the product. Criteria 1 and 2 stay open: the copy and paste of formatting, and the formatting suggestion (FE-128), need that core work first. Browser runs of the painter use the harness's own core (packages/ui/e2e/harness/main.ts), not the product's, so they do not show this. Audit (2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 4 unticked, because in the product the painter and `format.applyFormat` are never registered (App.svelte needs the core's `canCopyFormat` query and `pasteFormat`, which no core provides), so no user can arm the painter or be told why it is refused; the ticks rested on a fake core and the harness core.

### UX-010 Mini toolbar

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §4 · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-004

As an author, I want a small toolbar near my selection, so that common formatting is within reach of where I am working.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a non-empty selection the user may change, when it is made, then a mini toolbar generated from the registry appears near the selection with common formatting commands.
- [x] Given the mini toolbar is shown, when the author keeps typing or moves the caret, then the editing surface keeps focus, the toolbar never intercepts text input, and collapsing the selection or pressing Escape dismisses it.
- [x] Given the toolbar is needed without a pointer, when the documented keyboard gesture is used, then focus moves into it and Escape returns it to the selection.
- [ ] Given the user may not change the selection, when it is made, then no editing commands are offered.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/mini-toolbar.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `miniToolbarItems` in packages/ui/src/commands/mini-toolbar.ts offers nothing for a collapsed selection, the `format` group only, and no content command where the access or the mode gives `no-access` or `mode` (criterion 4, with a fast-check property over access and mode). Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-010-mini-toolbar.spec.ts, 4 tests, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 12 passed on the final run (one earlier run had three failures under shared machine load, and passed on a rerun with no change). The toolbar is rendered by packages/ui/src/components/MiniToolbar.svelte, shown from App.svelte for a non-empty selection the user may change: it appears at the caret rectangle (above it), its buttons take no pointer press so focus stays on the text, typing keeps focus on the editing surface, a collapsed selection hides it, Escape hides it first (the next Escape leaves the text), and a view-only user gets none. Criteria 1 and 2 are ticked on that run.

**Evidence (lane w3, UX-010, criterion 3, ticked):** the gesture is Alt+F10, the owner's answer to this open question (decision 168, `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`), listed in the shortcut table as `shell.miniToolbar` (`packages/ui/src/shortcuts/table.ts`, label `shortcuts.shell.miniToolbar` in `packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts`). `packages/ui/src/App.svelte` moves focus to the first mini toolbar button on Alt-F10 when the toolbar shows, and Escape with focus in it hides the toolbar and returns focus to the selection (`core.focus()`). `packages/ui/e2e/ux-010-mini-toolbar.spec.ts`, "Alt+F10 moves focus into the toolbar, and Escape returns it to the selection": passed in Chromium (`DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57390 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-010-mini-toolbar --project=chromium`, 4 of 5 passed; not run in Firefox or WebKit here). The `packages/ui` unit tests pass (128 files, 1042 tests).

**Notes:** Phase inferred (2): the spec places the context menu and format painter in 1b but does not place the mini toolbar. Review, round 1 (2026-11-13): criterion 4 unticked. `miniToolbarItems` is called by its test only; no mini toolbar is rendered, so nothing is offered or withheld in the product. Round 1 (lane ui-5, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 was ticked. The common formatting commands are taken as the `format` group (spec default, decided here; proposed in docs/adr/0139-ui-5-backlog-batch.md). Criteria 1 to 3 need the rendered toolbar, its placement near the selection, focus and the keyboard gesture, none of which is built or run in a browser here. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): the toolbar is built and run in the browser (see Evidence), so criteria 1 and 2 are ticked. Criterion 3 stays open: the spec names no keyboard gesture for the mini toolbar, and its buttons are not in the tab order (tabindex -1), so no key moves focus into it. The gesture is a decision for the owner (proposed, not chosen, in docs/adr/0325-lane-V-UX-001-015.md). Also changed: `miniToolbarItems` (packages/ui/src/commands/mini-toolbar.ts) now offers only commands that change the text (`changesContent`), so the painter's arming and the language list are not shown; the unit tests in packages/ui/test/mini-toolbar.test.ts still pass (22 tests with the painter and share tests). Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 unticked, because the toolbar is placed from `core.caretRect`, which neither the product's element nor the harness provides, so it falls back to the top-left of the page rect and not near the selection, and no test asserts its position. The Escape test in ux-010-mini-toolbar.spec.ts also failed in 2 of 2 runs in each of two browsers here, at its last step (a new selection after a collapse does not bring the toolbar back when the two selection updates fall in one frame).

### UX-011 Command search

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §4, §12 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-004, UX-064

As an author, I want to find a command by typing, so that I can use features without knowing where they live.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given command search is opened by its documented shortcut, when the author types part of a label, then matching commands from the registry are listed, and choosing one runs it.
- [x] Given a command has synonyms in the current locale, when a synonym is typed, then the command is listed.
- [x] Given the permissions do not allow a command, when it matches, then it cannot be run from the results and the reason is shown (UX-004).
- [x] Given extension commands (UX-051), when the author types their label, then they are listed with built-in ones.
- [x] Given keyboard and screen-reader use, when results change, then the list is exposed with the standard combobox and listbox semantics, the result count is announced, and after a command runs focus returns to the editing surface.
- [x] Given nothing matches, when Enter is pressed, then no command runs and a "no results" message is shown.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/command-search.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `searchCommands` in packages/ui/src/commands/search.ts matches the translated labels of offered commands (criterion 1 in part: the matching and the run of a hit); a command the access does not allow is listed with its reason and `exec` refuses it (criterion 3); a registered extension command is listed next to the built-in ones and drops out when removed (criterion 4); a command under a synonym the host supplies is listed (criterion 2, the mechanism only); nothing matches gives `search.none` and Enter runs nothing (criterion 6, the logic only); fast-check property over the query words. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): the command search is built. `view.commandSearch` (packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts, key Alt+Q, row in packages/ui/src/shortcuts/table.ts, generated into spec/shortcuts/) opens packages/ui/src/components/CommandSearch.svelte from App.svelte. The field is a combobox, the results a listbox with aria-activedescendant, the count and "No results" in a status region, focus back in the text. Browser run packages/ui/e2e/ux-011-command-search.spec.ts, 5 tests, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 15 passed: criterion 1 (Alt+Q, typed label, Enter runs the match, focus returns), criterion 3 (a view-only user sees the disabled result with its reason and Enter runs nothing), criterion 4 (an extension command listed beside the built-in one), criterion 5 (arrow keys, click, roles, count), criterion 6 ("No results", Enter runs nothing, Escape closes). Unit tests, `pnpm vitest run` on the eight shell files that read the table and the built-in commands: 52 passed.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17):** Criterion 2: the en-GB table now carries `synonyms.<command id>` keys (`packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts`), `synonymsFromStrings` in `packages/ui/src/commands/search.ts` reads them and `CommandSearch.svelte` passes it. `packages/ui/test/command-search.test.ts` (17 tests, Node.js; the whole `packages/ui` node suite: 971 passed): a synonym of the table lists its command at rank 3, a part of a synonym matches, a host replacement of the key is read, a command whose access is refused stays not runnable, and every `synonyms.*` key names an existing command. `packages/ui/e2e/ux-011-command-search.spec.ts`, "lists a command under a synonym from the locale table": typing "slant" lists only Italic and Enter runs it, then focus returns to the text. Criteria 3 and 4: the same spec's tests "lists a command the permissions do not allow as disabled, and Enter runs nothing" and "lists an extension command beside the built-in ones" (shell in the browser, not only `searchCommands`). The spec's 6 tests passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 18 runs, 2026-11-17 (`DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57332 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-011`).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 2, 3 and 4 ticked. The review of round 1 unticked 3 and 4 because only `searchCommands` was tested; the browser tests named in Evidence since round 16 run the real shell, so they are ticked now. Owner decision 170 (`docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`) settles that synonyms live in the locale tables (13 section 8). The key shape (`synonyms.<command id>`, words separated by semicolons) is recorded in `docs/adr/0383-w3-UX-011-command-synonyms.md` (proposed), and the en-GB words are a first set that no writer has reviewed. Ranking is not specified. Where synonyms live in a registry entry is open. Review, round 1 (2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 4 unticked. `searchCommands` is called by its test only; no command search is opened, typed into or shown in the product, so nothing is listed or refused from results. Round 1 (lane ui-5, 2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 4 were ticked. Ranking is decided here (docs/adr/0139-ui-5-backlog-batch.md): label starts with the query, then contains it, then every word in the label, then a synonym only. Synonyms have no source yet: the locale tables carry no synonym keys (13 section 8), so `synonyms` is an injected function that the shell does not pass; criterion 2 stays open for that reason. Criterion 1 needs the documented opening shortcut and a browser run; criteria 5 and 6 need the results surface (combobox and listbox semantics, the count announced, the "No results" message and focus return), none built. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criteria 1, 5 and 6 are ticked on the browser run named in Evidence. Criterion 2 stays open: synonyms have no source (the locale tables carry no synonym keys, 13 section 8), so the shell passes none; the decision where synonyms live is open. The opening shortcut is not named in the spec; the proposed choice is Alt+Q, the word processor's "Tell me" key, recorded as proposed in docs/adr/0325-lane-V-UX-001-015.md. Ranking is unchanged. Fixer V-FE-001-016 (2026-11-13): `view.commandSearch` now has `toolbar: false`. As a toolbar button it changed the compact row and broke four UX-006 tests in packages/ui/e2e/toolbar.spec.ts. Command search is phase 2 (13 §12), and UX-007 criterion 3 requires both layouts to show the same commands, so it is on neither toolbar. It opens with Alt+Q and `exec`, and is listed in the shortcuts pane. toolbar.spec.ts passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (36 tests).

### UX-012 Access keys

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §4, §12; 02 §3 (keyboard scope) · **Requirements:** R11, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-013

As an author who prefers the keyboard, I want key sequences that reach any toolbar command, so that I never need a pointer.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given focus is in the editor, when the documented activation gesture is made, then the access key of each visible command is shown, pressing a shown key runs that command, and Escape leaves the mode and restores focus.
- [x] Given two commands on the same level share an `accessKey`, when they are registered, then the conflict is reported at registration.
- [ ] Given a browser or assistive-technology sequence is reserved in any supported browser, when the activation gesture is chosen, then the shortcut table (UX-013) shows that the gesture is not one of them.
- [x] Given a screen reader, when the access keys are shown, then each key is exposed as text on its control and not only visually.
- [x] Given a command is disabled for the user's permissions, when its access key is pressed, then nothing runs and the reason is exposed.
- [x] Given focus is in the host page, when the gesture is made, then the editor does not react.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/e2e/ux-012-access-keys.spec.ts (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 18 runs: 15 passed and 3 flaky (each passed on retry, under shared machine load); the access keys are an extension command with key `k`): Alt pressed and released alone shows the key tip and an accessible description "Access key K" on the control (criterion 4), the key runs the command, Escape leaves and returns focus to the text (criterion 1, the mechanism), a command the permissions disallow runs nothing and its reason reaches the status region (criterion 5), and a press with focus in the host page does nothing (criterion 6). Unit tests: packages/ui/test/access-key-mode.test.ts (the gesture, `resolveShownKey`, a property over the keys). Gesture and shell: packages/ui/src/shell/access-key-mode.ts, packages/ui/src/components/AccessKeyTips.svelte, packages/ui/src/App.svelte. Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-012-access-keys.spec.ts`. Earlier evidence: packages/ui/test/access-keys.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `CommandRegistry.register` (packages/ui/src/commands/registry.ts, through `accessKeyClaim` in packages/ui/src/commands/access-keys.ts) refuses a second command with the same key on the same level, naming both, and leaves the first in place; the same key on another level is allowed; removal frees the key; a key that is not one letter or digit is refused; a fast-check property over the 36 letters and digits (criterion 2). `pressAccessKey` runs the command the key names, in either case; a disabled command runs nothing and returns `reasonKey` (criterion 5); a key no shown command has runs nothing; `accessKeysAt` shows the keys of visible commands on the level and leaves out those off the toolbars. (Superseded by the browser run above, which reaches `pressAccessKey` through the gesture; reviewer round 4 reran it, 18 of 18 passed with no retries.) Criterion 5 was not ticked (reviewer, round 2, 2026-11-13): the test 'runs nothing for a disabled command, and returns the reason as a string key' (`reasonKey` `reason.access.view`, the core's run never called) checks the logic of `pressAccessKey`, which only tests import; no key gesture reaches it and nothing shows the reason, so no press runs or refuses anything in the product. Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/access-keys.test.ts`.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criterion 1):** the gesture is decided: owner decision 116 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md (Alt pressed and released alone, ADR 0231 option A), and it is now documented in docs/spec/13-experience-extensibility.md section 4 (paragraph 'Access keys'). The mechanism is the browser run above: packages/ui/e2e/ux-012-access-keys.spec.ts, 6 tests per browser, run again on 2026-11-18 with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56331 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-012`: Chromium and Firefox passed; WebKit failed 4 tests when the four workers shared the machine with other lanes and passed 6 of 6 alone (`--workers=1 --project=webkit`), the load flakiness the earlier evidence names.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criterion 1 is ticked on owner decision 116 and the spec paragraph. As the Notes below say, the built-in commands declare no access key, so the product shows keys for the commands that declare one (the tests use an extension command); choosing keys for built-in commands is still open and would not change this criterion's mechanism. Criterion 3 stays unticked: Firefox's published list could not be read on 2026-11-18 either (the Mozilla support article returned no content to the reader), so the shortcut table cannot say that the gesture is not reserved there, and a lone Alt is known to show a browser menu bar on some Windows and Linux browsers; no shell row for the gesture is added to the table here, because the table's rows are each bound and pressed by the UX-013 tests and the generated artifacts. The owner may want to reword criterion 3 (for example, 'the gesture is documented with its known conflicts'); that is the owner's call. Earlier: The activation gesture is not specified (open question). Lane U-UX-001-015, round 4 (2026-11-13): the lane proposes the gesture of docs/adr/0231-lane-UX-001-013.md, option A (Alt pressed and released alone; a letter or digit runs the shown key; Escape leaves), as proposed in docs/adr/0325-lane-U-UX-001-015.md, and wires it in the shell. The gesture is not decided: criterion 1 keeps its "documented" clause open until the owner chooses it and UX-090 documents it, and criterion 3 stays open (the shortcut table row for the gesture is not added here, because the table feeds generated artifacts in `spec/shortcuts/`, and Firefox's list is unread; Windows and Firefox may move focus to the menu bar on a lone Alt, which Playwright cannot show). The built-in commands declare no access key, so the product shows keys only for commands that declare one; the choice of built-in keys is open, and the registry's property test (packages/ui/test/access-keys.test.ts) assumes none on the format level. Criteria 4, 5 and 6 are ticked on the browser run in Evidence. Lane UX-001-013, round 2 (2026-11-13): the choice and its reserved-sequence check are recorded as a proposed ADR, docs/adr/0231-lane-UX-001-013.md (status: proposed, nothing decided). Criterion 5 stays unticked: no gesture reaches `pressAccessKey` and the shell shows no reason, so it waits for that decision and the shell wiring. Review, round 1 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 unticked. `pressAccessKey` is called by its test only; no gesture or key handler reaches it, so no key press runs or refuses anything in the product. Criterion 2 stays ticked: the refusal is in `CommandRegistry.register`, which every command uses. Round 1 (lane ui-5, 2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 5 were ticked. Decisions made here, proposed in docs/adr/0139-ui-5-backlog-batch.md: the level is the command's toolbar `group`, and an access key is one letter or digit. The registry refuses a shared key at registration (an edit to registry.ts, the shared file). Criterion 1 needs the gesture and a browser run, criterion 3 the shortcut table's reserved browser sequences for that gesture, criterion 4 the rendered key labels with their text, and criterion 6 the host-page focus test in a browser; all stay open. Round 2 (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 was ticked on the logic of `pressAccessKey`. Review, round 2: unticked again for the reason of review round 1. No key gesture reaches `pressAccessKey` in the product and nothing displays the reason, so the criterion stays open until the gesture and the shell exist; criteria 1, 3, 4 and 6 stay open.

### UX-013 Keyboard shortcuts and the maintained shortcut table

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §4; 02 §3; 00 R1 · **Requirements:** R1, R12 · **Depends on:** UX-003

As an author, I want shortcuts that follow word-processor conventions where the browser allows, so that my habits work.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the registry, when the shortcut table is published, then for every entry with `keys` it lists the conventional word-processor shortcut, this product's shortcut, and for Chrome, Edge, Firefox and Safari whether each conflicts and what the alternative is.
- [x] Given a browser reserves a shortcut, when the table is built, then it names the alternative and the registry binds that alternative on that browser.
- [x] Given Playwright in each supported browser, when each shortcut or its alternative in the table is pressed with focus in the editing surface, then the command runs.
- [x] Given focus is in the host page, when a shortcut is pressed, then the editor does not act.
- [x] Given an extension registers `keys` that collide with an existing binding, when it registers, then the collision is reported and neither binding is replaced silently.
- [x] Given the shortcut reference shown to users, when it is rendered, then it is generated from the registry and the table, not written by hand.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/shortcut-alternatives.test.ts (each browser conflict that the table resolves with a key the shell binds: the registry's `commandForKey` answers the same command, for Windows and Linux and for Apple platforms, for the core commands; `buildShortcutTable` reports no problem for the built-in commands). Run of 2026-11-13, Node.js only. Criterion 5 (round 1, ui-batch, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/extension-commands.test.ts 'a colliding shortcut is refused and names its holder' (a key a built-in command binds, and a key another extension holds, are each refused with an error naming the holder; the first binding is kept and the second command is not registered); 42 files, 426 tests passed under `pnpm vitest run packages/ui`. Criterion 6 (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/src/shortcuts/reference.ts builds the rows from the registry's offered commands with keys and the table's shell rows, and packages/ui/src/panes/ShortcutsPane.svelte renders them; packages/ui/test/shortcut-reference.test.ts checks those rows (labels from string keys, a command registered at run time listed and dropped when removed, a command without keys left out, shell rows with their keys, alternatives and conditions). Node.js only: the pane's rendering was not run in a browser. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13), packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts, 'keyboard shortcuts from the registry (UX-013)', 5 tests, run with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57250 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts -g "keyboard shortcuts from the registry"`: 15 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Criterion 3: the table's rows are pressed, each key and alternative (Mod-z, Mod-y, Shift-Mod-z, Mod-a, Mod-b, Mod-i, Shift-Mod-c and its Alt alternative, Mod-f, Ctrl-F6 and Alt-F6, Shift-Ctrl-F6 and Shift-Alt-F6, Shift-F10 and the Menu key, and the painter's Enter and Escape in their own test). Criterion 4: a host-page keystroke runs nothing, and the editor's shadow root holds no focus. Criterion 3 (lane w3, 2026-11-15): packages/ui/e2e/ux-013-w3-table-keys.spec.ts takes its rows from `SHORTCUT_TABLE` and presses every key and every alternative of every row (18 keys of 13 rows, `view.commandSearch` with Alt-Q among them), each on a freshly opened page with focus in the editing surface, and sees the command run (the core command recorded, the painter armed, applied or cancelled, command search, the find field, another landmark, the context menu); a row with no check fails the suite. Run on Windows 11 with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=47936 DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,msedge,firefox,webkit pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-013-w3-table-keys`: 76 passed (19 tests in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit). On Windows `Mod` is Ctrl; no run was made on macOS, so the Command-key forms and Safari itself (WebKit stands in for it) are not covered.

**Notes:** `Mod` means the platform's primary modifier. Phase inferred (1b): R1 asks for shortcuts that match Word where the browser allows, and 1a has only a simple toolbar. Packet U (2026-11-13): criterion 2 ticked for the core commands; the shell's own rows (landmarks, the painter's keys) are covered by the table build only. Criterion 1 stays open: Firefox's published list was not read (`SOURCES.firefox` is null in `packages/ui/src/shortcuts/table.ts`), so its column says "not checked". Criteria 3 and 4 need the browser run (not run here). Criterion 5 was ticked in round 1 of the ui batch (2026-11-13) on the extension collision tests named in its Evidence line. Criterion 6: the build writes the reference from the table, and (lane UX-001-013, 2026-11-13) the pane's rows are now built by `shortcutReferenceRows`, which the test checks; criterion 6 is ticked on that. Criteria 1, 3 and 4 stay open: criterion 1 needs Firefox's published list (`SOURCES.firefox` is still null), criteria 3 and 4 need a browser run. Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13), packages/ui/e2e/commands.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: 'every shortcut and alternative of the table works' passed in all three, but it presses a subset of the table's rows (bold, italic, undo, redo, select all, find, painter, landmarks, context menu), so criterion 3 stays open; 'does nothing while focus is in the host page' passed in Chromium and WebKit and failed in Firefox at its last focus check (after Control+F6, focus was not on the host button; no command ran), so criterion 4 stays open. Lane V-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): the Firefox failure was a test assumption, not an editor action. Firefox takes Ctrl+F6 for its own focus cycle and `#after` loses focus; the editor did not act. The test now asserts that the editor's shadow root holds no focus, and it passes in all three browsers, so criteria 3 and 4 are ticked. Criterion 1 stays open: `SOURCES.firefox` is still null, so Firefox's published list has not been read (needs the owner or a reader with access to Mozilla's support article). Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 3 unticked, because the e2e test never presses `view.commandSearch` (Alt-Q), which is a row of the table and a built-in command, so not every shortcut of the table is pressed in a browser (the run also covers Chromium, Firefox and WebKit only, not Edge). Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 3 is ticked again on a table-driven test that presses every row, Alt-Q included, in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (Evidence). The shell runs there on the browser harness with the fake core, as in the earlier runs. Criterion 1 stays open: Mozilla's support article (`support.mozilla.org/en-US/kb/keyboard-shortcuts-perform-firefox-tasks-quickly`) was requested twice on 2026-11-15 and answered only "A required part of this site couldn't load", so Firefox's list is still unread and `SOURCES.firefox` stays null. Nothing was entered from memory. It needs a person to read the article in a browser and record what it says for each key of the table.

## Shell

### UX-014 Header frame, title slot and host-replaceable header

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §12; 02 §3 (parts) · **Requirements:** R14, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-064, gap: 05 §8.1 has no attribute or event for this yet

As an author, I want a header with the document's title, its save state and the people in it, so that I always know where I am and what state my work is in.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the editor loads, when the header renders, then it holds a title slot, the save state (UX-016) and the presence region (UX-026), and the element exposes the `header` part.
- [x] Given the host fills the title slot, when the header renders, then the host's title is shown, and the editor neither fetches nor edits titles.
- [x] Given a feature is not announced by the backend (comments, catch-up), when the header renders, then its button is not shown; given it is, then the button is keyboard operable and any count on it has a text equivalent.
- [x] Given the host hides the header, when the editor runs, then it is fully usable: mode, panes and share can be reached by commands and `exec`.
