- [x] Given the host draws its own header, when it needs save and connection state, mode, presence and counts, then each is available through documented attributes, properties or events.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/components/Header.svelte; packages/ui/e2e/header.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: header region with title slot (a host light-DOM element assigned), save state and presence, ::part(header) styled from the host page; the title property shown when the slot is empty and no request but the harness's own files; comments and catch-up buttons only while the features are announced, keyboard operable, counts in their accessible names; packages/ui/e2e/a11y.spec.ts. Round 2 (criterion 4, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/header-free.test.ts (share, a pane and a mode run through `exec` on a registry with the shell's commands and no Header; the commands the core does not back are not registered, as App.svelte does). Lane w3 (criterion 5, 2026-11-15): packages/element/src/header-events.ts, events.ts (`de-mode-change`, `de-presence`, `de-counts`), doc-editor.ts (`header` attribute, `headerState` property), documented in docs/adr/0361-w3-UX-014-host-drawn-header.md; packages/element/test/ux-014-header-events.test.ts (the reporter on plain views and on the real core through a session against backend-mock); packages/element/e2e/ux-014-host-header.spec.ts in Chromium, Edge, Firefox and WebKit (`header="none"` hides the built-in header; a header drawn by the embed page follows save and connection state, the mode, a second session arriving and leaving, and the counts; removing the attribute brings the header back).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 5 is ticked on names that are proposed, not settled: 05 §8.1 still lists neither the `header` attribute nor the three events, so they are documented in ADR 0361 and the source until PRO adopts or renames them. The core has no source for the comment and catch-up counts yet, so `de-counts` carries `null` outside tests; the browser test sets the counts through the embed page's test hook. Earlier notes follow. Hiding or replacing the header has no mechanism in the embedding API (05 section 8.1 has `toolbar` but nothing for the header), and presence, comment counts and catch-up counts have no event (gap, open questions). The last criterion cannot pass until PRO defines them. The catch-up button opens HIS-027, HIS-028. Built on 2026-11-06 with a proposed `header` setting (`default`, `none`) that the element reflects once PRO names the attribute (ADR 0041, section 4). Criterion 4 stays open: panes and share run through `exec` with the header hidden (tested), but there is no mode command before the mode switcher (UX-015, phase 1b). Round 2, packet U (2026-11-13): the mode commands now exist, so criterion 4 (the first open bullet of the list above) is ticked on packages/ui/test/header-free.test.ts: share, a pane and a mode run through `exec` on a registry with no Header component, the shell's commands registered as App.svelte registers them. The `header` setting that hides the header is not built (PRO attribute open), so the header is not hidden in a browser run here. The host-drawn-header criterion stays open. Lane UX-014-026, round 2 (2026-11-13): re-checked against the element's documented events (packages/element/src/events.ts). `de-status` gives the connection and the save state, and the `mode` attribute is documented, but no event carries presence, comment or catch-up counts, or the mode in effect after a change in the switcher (UX-015, criterion 6). The criterion stays open until PRO defines those (05 section 8.1).

### UX-015 Mode switcher

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3; 05 §8.1 (`mode`); 07 §7 · **Requirements:** R3, R8, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-014, UX-004

As an author, I want to switch between editing, reviewing and viewing, so that I can work in the way the moment needs, within my rights.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the user's capabilities, when the switcher renders, then it offers only available modes: `edit` needs `edit`, `review` needs the suggestion feature and `suggest` or `edit`, `view` is always available, `fill` is offered where the user has `fill`; an unavailable mode is shown disabled with a reason.
- [x] Given the user chooses `review`, when the mode changes, then suggestion mode is on and edits become suggestions; given `edit`, then it is off.
- [x] Given the user has `suggest` but not `edit` in the scope, when the mode is shown, then the editor stays in suggestion mode and cannot leave it (07 section 7).
- [x] Given mode `view`, when the user types or runs a content command, then nothing changes locally and no commit is sent.
- [x] Given the document is `locked` or `archived`, or a workflow is active, when the switcher renders, then `edit` is unavailable with a reason.
- [x] Given the `mode` attribute is set by the host or changed by the user, when it changes, then the mode in effect is visible, announced to assistive technology, and available to the host.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/modes.test.ts (the offer of `shell/modes.ts`: only supported modes listed; `view` always; `edit` needs `edit`, `review` needs `suggest` or `edit`, `fill` needs `fill`; every unavailable mode carries a reason, as fast-check properties; `edit` and `review` refused with the limit `locked`, `archived` or `workflow` even with edit rights; suggest-only refused for `edit`). Rendering: packages/ui/src/components/ModeSwitcher.svelte sets `aria-disabled` and a described reason span. The browser run of packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts was not part of this round and is not claimed here. Criterion 3 (lane UX-014-026, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/suggest-mode.test.ts (the suggest-only scope keeps the editor out of `edit`, as a property, and moves it to `review`; the chosen `edit` comes back in an editable scope) and packages/ui/test/modes.test.ts (`suggestOnly` refused for `edit`); run with `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/suggest-mode.test.ts packages/ui/test/modes.test.ts` (passed). Criteria 2 and 4 (lane U-UX-001-015, 2026-11-13): packages/engine/test/ux-015-mode-commits.test.ts, in packages/engine with `pnpm exec vitest run test/ux-015-mode-commits.test.ts` (4 tests passed, Node.js, the real DocumentCore): in `view` a typed insertion and `bold` change no text and queue no commit (criterion 4); in `review` an insertion carries an `ins` mark and is queued, and after `setMode('edit')` the next insertion has no `ins` mark (criterion 2). The switcher's own click is not run in a browser. Criterion 6 (lane w3, 2026-11-15): packages/element/e2e/ux-015-mode-in-effect.spec.ts, the real element, shell and in-memory backend of the embed page, run from the repository root on Windows 11 with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57461 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts e2e/ux-015-mode-in-effect.spec.ts --project=chromium --project=firefox --project=webkit --retries=0 --workers=2` (6 of 6 passed). With the `mode` attribute set by the host to `view` and back, and with `view` chosen by a click in the switcher and `edit` by the keyboard, the switcher's button shows the mode in effect (its text, its accessible name and `data-mode`), the shell's status region says "Mode: Viewing." or "Mode: Editing.", a composed, bubbling `de-mode-change` carries the mode in effect, and `headerState.mode` reads the same.

**Notes:** Packet U, round 1 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 5 ticked on the unit tests above. Criterion 3 stays open: the offer is tested, but that the editor stays in suggestion mode and cannot leave it is the core's (07 section 7). Criteria 2, 4 and 6 need the mode change to reach the core and the host, which is not built here. Lane UX-014-026, round 1 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 is ticked (see its Evidence). Criterion 2 stays open: the shell's request (`review` for a chosen review, `edit` for a chosen edit) is in `shell/suggest-mode.ts`, but that edits then become suggestions is the core's and is not checked here. Phase inferred (1b). The element has no event for a mode change made in the switcher; the host-visibility criterion depends on PRO (open questions). `fill` mode's interface is FRW's. Cross-check: the review mode joins the switcher in phase 2 (FE-041). Lane U-UX-001-015 (2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 4 ticked at the core (see Evidence). The core needs a `user` to record suggestions; without one a suggestion-mode change is refused (`reason: suggestion`), which the test sets. Criterion 6 stays open: the element raises no event for a mode in effect and the header does not carry it to the host. Lane w3 (2026-11-15): criterion 6 is ticked on the browser run in Evidence. The code was already there (the announcement in `App.svelte`, `de-mode-change` and `headerState` from UX-014); this lane added only the test. "Available to the host" is met by `de-mode-change` and `headerState`, which are still proposed (ADR 0361), since 05 §8.1 names no event; a mode chosen in the switcher is not written back to the element's `mode` attribute, and the test does not claim it. Edge was not run.

### UX-016 Save state and connection state

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §9, §12; 02 §2.2; 05 §8.1 (`de-status`); PRODUCT principle 4 · **Requirements:** R14, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-014, SYN-015, SYN-017

As an author, I want to see at all times whether my work is saved, so that I trust it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given all local changes are acknowledged, when the indicator renders, then it shows "saved"; given changes are in flight, "saving"; given they are only in the journal, "unsaved"; given the user cannot edit or the document is locked, "read-only".
- [x] Given the connection changes, when it goes online, reconnecting or offline, then the connection state is shown with the save state, in text or an icon and never by colour alone.
- [x] Given a state changes, when it changes, then `de-status` is dispatched with `{ connection, sync }`.
- [x] Given the database is unavailable and the room answers `unavailable`, when the author keeps typing, then editing continues, the indicator shows "unsaved", and after recovery it returns to "saved" without any action.
- [ ] Given a tab is closed while "unsaved", when the author returns, then the journal resubmits the work and the indicator moves through "saving" to "saved".
- [x] Given the host awaits `flush()`, when the promise resolves, then the indicator shows "saved".

**Evidence:** packages/element/test/ux-016-outage-flush.test.ts (lane UX-014-026, 2026-11-13; Node.js, backend-mock, the real headless core): criterion 4, the room's `unavailable` close taken from the mock channel with a 400 ms retry, the core's connection `reconnecting` and the save state `unsaved`, a further paste accepted, then the held writes released and the channel reopened by the session, with the save state `saved` and both edits in the room; criterion 6, after `flush()` resolves the status view reads `online/saved`, and `de-status` follows with `saved` in the next frame. Run: `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/ux-016-outage-flush.test.ts` (2 tests passed). The outage is injected through the mock's own channel close, not a real database; the browser indicator (SaveState.svelte) is not run here. packages/ui/src/components/SaveState.svelte; packages/ui/e2e/header.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: each sync value of the core's status view (saved, saving, unsaved, readonly) and each connection value (online, reconnecting, offline) shown in words, each save state with its own words and mark shape, nothing shown without a status view; the values come from the core and the element (FE-007). Criterion 3 (round 3, 2026-11-13): the `de-status` detail carries `connection` and `sync` (packages/element/test/session.test.ts, the detail checks at lines 161, 192 and 244); run with `pnpm exec vitest run packages/element/test/session.test.ts packages/element/test/status.test.ts packages/ui` (19 files, 226 tests passed).

**Notes:** The indicator is mirrored in the status bar (UX-018). Announcements: FE-107. Cross-check: the "unsaved" state, for work held only in the journal, arrives with FE-008 in phase 1b. In phase 1a the status bar shows the indicator while the header is hidden. Criterion 3 is the element's (`de-status` from the same status view, FE-007, packages/element) and is ticked with its evidence. Criteria 4 and 5 need the journal (FE-008, FE-009), criterion 6 `flush()` (PRO-083), all phase 1b. Lane UX-014-026, round 2 (2026-11-13): criteria 4 and 6 are ticked on packages/element/test/ux-016-outage-flush.test.ts. Criterion 4 holds without the journal because the core's save state is `unsaved` while its connection is down (packages/engine, `#publishSync`); the work is kept in the core, not the journal, so criterion 4 says nothing about the journal. Criterion 5 stays open: a closed tab's journal is FE-008, not built. Lane V-UX-016-030, round 4 (2026-11-14): the IndexedDB journal is built (packages/element/src/journal-idb.ts, FE-008), but the resubmission on open that criterion 5 needs is not: packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts never calls `journal.read` on open, and packages/element/e2e/fe-009-resubmit-reopen.spec.ts is `test.fixme` for that reason. Criterion 5 is blocked on FE-009 (resubmission), an engine change this lane did not make; it is listed in the report as needed from the main session. Lane w3 (2026-11-15): checked again after merging stories/integration. FE-009 has no criterion ticked, `packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts` still reads only the recovered edits from the journal on open (`readRecovered`), never the unconfirmed commits (`journal.read`), so nothing is resubmitted. Criterion 5 stays blocked on FE-009; nothing new was built here.

### UX-017 Recovered edits pane

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §9, §12; 07 §9; 05 §8.1 (`de-recovered-edits`); PRODUCT principle 4 · **Requirements:** R3, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-016, FE-008, FE-009, PRM-024, PRM-026

As an author, I want content that was set aside to be kept where I can find it, so that a rejected change or a lost connection never costs me my words.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a commit is rejected with `permission_denied`, when the client discards its steps, then the discarded content appears in the pane with the reason, the region concerned and, if known, who changed the permission.
- [x] Given unsent content is set aside after a rebase failure or recovered from the journal, when the pane is opened, then it is listed with the same details, and `de-recovered-edits` carries `{ count }`.
- [x] Given an item in the pane, when the author chooses to copy it or insert it at the caret, then the content is copied out or inserted through the same guards as typing.
- [x] Given the tab is closed and reopened, when the document loads, then the items that were not discarded are still listed.
- [x] Given an item, when the author discards it, then a deliberate action is required; nothing leaves the pane automatically.
- [x] Given a new item arrives, when assistive technology is in use, then the arrival is announced (FE-107).

**Evidence:** packages/ui/e2e/panes.spec.ts, "recovered edits (UX-017)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13, 3 tests passed): an item set aside with its reason, its region and who changed the permission, shown in the pane (criterion 1; the set-aside item is supplied by the test harness, so the engine's discard is not run here); insert at the caret through the guards, refused with the reason where the user may not edit (criterion 3, insert only; copy is not tested); discard only after a second, deliberate step, and nothing leaves the pane on its own (criterion 5). Firefox and WebKit not run. Criterion 2 (lane UX-014-026, 2026-11-13; the rebase branch of its "or"): the set-aside item of packages/ui/e2e/panes.spec.ts (Chromium, run in packet U) is listed with its reason, region and who changed the permission; packages/element/test/session.test.ts, "raises de-recovered-edits { count } when the core sets work aside" (the count of 1, 1, 2 gives events 1 and 2, no repeat); packages/ui/test/notices.test.ts, "work set aside" (items added, with their number; the rebase reason explained). Run: `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/notices.test.ts packages/element/test/session.test.ts` (passed).

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-017-arrival-announce.spec.ts (criterion 6, rendered): a set-aside item makes the hidden `role="status"` region of the shell read "kept in recovered edits", and a second item is announced again with new text (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed with `--retries=0`). The set-aside items are supplied by the harness (`setAside`), not by the engine's rebase, so the arrival from a real rejection is not run here.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 2 to 4 on the product element):** `insertRecovered` and `discardRecovered` are now commands of the editing core (`DocumentCore.insertRecovered`, `discardRecovered` in packages/engine/src/core.ts; `RecoveredItem.id` in api.ts, the commit or the `seq`), declared and run by the element (packages/element/src/editor.ts `commandNames` and `runNamed`), so the shell's pane runs them on the product and not only on the test fake. Insert is `pastePlainText`, the path typing uses, so a place the user may not edit refuses it. Discard takes the item out of the core's view and out of the journal (`SyncSession.forgetRecovered`, packages/engine/src/sync/session.ts). Browser: packages/element/e2e/ux-017-recovered-pane.spec.ts (the embed page with the real Room of backend-mock and the IndexedDB journal; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed with `--workers=1 --retries=0`, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=58301`): Ada types into the legal region, her grant is withdrawn and the held commit is refused with `permission_denied`; the pane lists the items with that reason and `de-recovered-edits` carried `{ count }` only; Copy puts the item's text on the clipboard (read back in Chromium only, where the test may read the clipboard) and Insert at the cursor puts the text at the caret in the body and is marked unavailable and changes nothing in the legal region, where Ada may not edit (criterion 3); the pane keeps the items until the author chooses Discard and then Discard for good, and "Keep it" leaves the item and the journal as they were; the tab is closed and opened again, and the pane lists the same items with the same details (reason, parts, text) from the journal (criteria 2, the journal branch, and 4); one item is discarded, the tab is closed and opened again, and the item is gone while the rest stay (criterion 4). The rebase branch of criterion 2 is the evidence given above (packages/element/test/session.test.ts and packages/ui/test/notices.test.ts) with the engine's own test of the drop (packages/engine/test/sync-state.test.ts).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): the audit finding below is closed for criteria 2 to 4: copy, insert and discard now run on the engine's core. Not shown: the arrival of an item from a rebase that drops a step, in the product's pane in a browser (the rebase branch is shown by the Node tests above, and the journal branch by the browser run). Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 stays open: the rebase item is listed, but the `de-recovered-edits` count and the journal recovery are not checked. Lane UX-014-026, round 1: criterion 2 was ticked on its rebase branch only; review of group 3 (round 1) put it back to open, because the criterion covers content "recovered from the journal" as well, which needs FE-008 (not built), and the rebase item in the e2e run is supplied by the test harness, not by the engine's discard. The rebase branch is covered by the evidence above. Criterion 4 (reopening the tab) is not checked. Criterion 6 (the live-region arrival, FE-107) was ticked by lane V-UX-016-030 on a rendered run (see the Evidence line above). Copy, insert and discard are the minimum that "recovered" implies; the spec only says the content goes to the panel. Layout is the designer's. Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 3 unticked, because `insertRecovered` and `discardRecovered` are implemented only in the UI's test fake (packages/ui/test/support/fake-core.ts) and the e2e harness; no real core (packages/engine, the element's `runNamed`) runs them, and copy is not tested, so insertion "through the same guards as typing" is not shown on product code.

### UX-018 Status bar

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3; 02 §7; PRODUCT principle 3 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-016, FE-127, FE-128, FE-129, FE-066, FE-138

As an author, I want a status bar with counts, language, connection, save state and zoom, so that I can see the state of the document at a glance.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the document changes, when the status bar renders, then the word count reflects the body and updates without delaying typing.
- [x] Given the web view and no typeset result for the current content, when the page count would be shown, then it is not presented as exact; given a typeset result exists, then the count comes from it and says which result it is from.
- [x] Given the caret is in text with a `lang` mark, or none, when the language is shown, then it is that mark's language or the document default; given the author changes it, then the language command applies it to the selection and is subject to permissions.
- [x] Given the connection or save state changes, when the bar renders, then both are shown as in UX-016.
- [x] Given the zoom control, when it is used by pointer or keyboard, then the content scale changes, the document data does not, and a command resets it.
- [x] Given the element, when it is styled by the host, then the bar exposes the `statusbar` part, and extension items (UX-052) and the classification label (UX-085) appear in it when present.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts, "the status bar (UX-018)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13, 15 tests of the file passed): no page count in the web view, and a page count from a typeset result with the result it comes from, and its stale state (criterion 2); the language from the mark at the caret and from the document default, set through the language dialog as `setLanguage`, and disabled for a view-only user (criterion 3); the connection and save state in the words of UX-016 (criterion 4); zoom by pointer and keyboard, with no core command run and Reset zoom (criterion 5). Node.js: packages/ui/test/status.test.ts (the choice of the language source, by property tests; the language command's access). Firefox and WebKit not run.

**Evidence (lane U-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13, criterion 6):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-018-statusbar.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed): the bar carries the `statusbar` part, a host rule `#host::part(statusbar)` (added through CSSOM, as the strict policy forbids inline style elements) sets its outline, and the extension items `ext.start-status` and `ext.end-status` render at the start and end groups in DOM order (`extensionContributions` mount option). packages/ui/e2e/classification.spec.ts (the classification label in the bar and its absence: 2 tests in 3 browsers, 6 passed; run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/classification.spec.ts`). Port 56261 via DOCEDIT_UI_PORT.

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-018-statusbar-parts.spec.ts (criterion 6, rendered in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 6 passed with `--retries=0`): the bar element carries `part="statusbar"`, the start and end extension items are its descendants, the classification label reads "Classification: Internal use only" in it, and without a label no classification item is shown.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 and 3 on the product):** the editing core now has the counts and the language. `DocumentCore.words` is the body's word count (packages/engine/src/word-count.ts: `countBodyWords`, `WordCounter`), `language` is the document's default language, and the selection summary carries the `lang` mark at the selection (packages/engine/src/core.ts, api.ts). The element gives the shell `stats` and `language` and runs `setLanguage` as the core's `lang` command (packages/element/src/editor.ts). Node.js: packages/engine/test/ux-018-status-counts.test.ts (9 tests, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/ux-018-status-counts.test.ts`, passed): words are counted as the language sees them (`Intl.Segmenter`), the body with its regions is counted and the footnote, header and definitions are not (18 words in the spike document); 1000 changes make one timer and no counting until it fires, and on the core typing three words leaves the count as it was until the timer fires, then it is 3 higher, and deleting lowers it (criterion 1); the `lang` mark is the language at the selection, `null` removes it so the document's default applies, the default is its own view, a reader is refused and a malformed tag is refused (criterion 3). Browser: packages/element/e2e/ux-018-status-counts.spec.ts (the embed page, the real engine and shell; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 9 passed with `--workers=1 --retries=0`, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=58301`): after typing ten words every key is in the paragraph at once and the bar's count rises by ten within 5 s, and it falls back after deleting them (criterion 1); the language dialog sets French on the selected paragraph through the core, the bar shows "Language: French" at the caret and not elsewhere, and the text carries `lang="fr"`; a viewer's language item is marked unavailable (criterion 3). No typing latency was measured: criterion 1 rests on the count being made by a timer outside the keystroke, which the Node tests show.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): the audit finding below is closed: the engine has the counts and `setLanguage` is the core's `lang` command, run by the element. Decisions taken here, proposed and not from the spec: words are counted by the runtime's word segmentation (`Intl.Segmenter`, else runs of letters or digits between spaces); footnote text, headers, footers and definitions are not counted; a field such as the page number and a footnote mark are not words; text of a hidden region is a placeholder and adds none; the count is made at most once every 400 ms, so it can be that much behind the text. Page counts stay null in the product: no typeset result exists in the web view. Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open: the word count is checked to change, not that it updates without delaying typing. Criterion 6 is ticked (lane U-UX-016-030, see Evidence). The extension items are placed by edge, not measured on screen in pixels: the start and end groups are tested by DOM order. Page counts in the status bar conflict in spirit with "no fake pages" in the web view (open questions). Phase inferred (1b). Lane UX-014-026, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open. The element's editor handle sets no `stats` view and the engine has none (`DocumentStats` in packages/ui/src/core-api.ts is optional), so the bar shows no word count and there is nothing to time against typing. Criterion 6 is ticked and is also checked on a rendered run by lane V-UX-016-030 (see the Evidence line above the Notes); packages/ui/test/extension-status-bar.test.ts remains a source check. Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 3 unticked, because `setLanguage` is run only against the e2e harness's core; the element's `runNamed` knows only `insertField` and `setRegionPolicy` and the engine has no `setLanguage`, so that the language command applies the language to the selection is not shown on product code (the status bar's own display and the guard are tested).

### UX-019 Pane host

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §7.1, §9; 02 §2; 05 §8.3 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-003

As a maintainer, I want one pane host with a registry of panes, so that every pane, built in or contributed, opens, loads and takes focus the same way.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the registry, when the editor starts, then it knows the panes navigation, styles, comments, review, history, permissions, fields, accessibility checker, assistant, PDF preview and recovered edits, plus any contributed by extensions (UX-052).
- [x] Given a pane is opened by a command, `exec` or a control, when it opens for the first time, then its code is requested on demand (not before) and a loading state is announced.
- [x] Given a pane opens, when focus moves, then it goes into the pane, and on close returns to where it was.
- [x] Given a pane's backend port is absent, or the user lacks the capability the pane needs, when the registry is read, then the pane is not offered (05 section 8.3).
- [x] Given a narrow container, when a pane is open, then it never covers the caret or the focused element.
- [x] Given the element, when it is styled by the host, then the host exposes the `sidebar` part.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/shell/panes.ts (`BUILT_IN_PANES`, `PaneRegistry.register`, `PaneRegistry.offered`, with the optional `capability` of a descriptor) and packages/ui/test/panes.test.ts ('the registry knows the panes of 13 section 3 at start': the eleven panes of criterion 1, with navigation held by the descriptor `outline`; a pane added and removed at run time; 'a pane is offered only when its feature, capability and data are there': no loader, an unannounced feature, a missing capability at the selection or in the caller's list, missing data, and a property over feature and capability). Criteria 2, 3, 5 and 6 are not claimed: the host's opening code, focus and the `sidebar` part are not checked in a browser here. Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criteria 3, 5 and 6 are ticked on packages/ui/e2e/panes.spec.ts, "the pane host (UX-019, UX-070)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13: focus goes into the pane and back to the control or text that opened it; at 420 px the pane does not overlap the editing surface; the pane has the `sidebar` part). Criterion 2 (lane U-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-019-pane-loading.spec.ts, 1 test passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.config.ts e2e/ux-019-pane-loading.spec.ts`, run from packages/ui with DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56261): the StylesPane chunk is held back by a route, the "Loading ..." announcement is read while it is on its way, no pane is shown until release, the pane is shown after it, and its code is requested once.

**Notes:** The contents of the history, permissions, fields and PDF preview panes belong to HIS-009, HIS-015, PRM-024, PRM-026, FRW-072, FRW-073 and FE-071. This story provides the slot. Round 2 (packet U, 2026-11-13): no built-in pane declares a `capability` yet, so the check is live only for panes that declare one; the App's call sites pass no capability list and read the selection's capabilities. The owner should say which capability each built-in pane needs before one is declared, because the fill pane's selection in a body paragraph has no `fill` (ADR 0113, round 2). Lane UX-014-026, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 stayed open then; lane U-UX-016-030 ticked it with a browser run (see Evidence). `openPane` in packages/ui/src/App.svelte announces `pane.loading` (en-GB "Loading {pane}") before the first `descriptor.load()`, and not for a cached pane, so the code path exists. The announcement is made by a component that no Node test renders, and no browser run was made; packages/ui/test/on-demand-panes.test.ts covers only the dynamic imports.

### UX-020 Navigation pane: headings and search results

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §12; 02 §5.1 (`outline()`) · **Requirements:** R1, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-019, FE-026, FE-059, FE-060

As an author, I want a pane that shows the headings and the results of my search, so that I can move through a long document.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given headings (paragraphs whose style has an `outlineLevel`), when the pane opens, then they are listed as a nested outline, and choosing one places the caret there and scrolls it into view.
- [x] Given local or remote changes, when headings are added, changed or removed, then the outline updates without losing the author's place and without delaying typing.
- [x] Given a search, when it runs, then the results are listed with their context and a count, and choosing one moves to the match.
- [x] Given a screen reader, when the outline is read, then it is exposed as a tree with levels, and each item has an accessible name.
- [x] Given headings in a region the user cannot edit, when the pane renders, then they are listed like any other.

**Evidence:** apps/reference-host/e2e/v-ux-020-navigate.spec.ts (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-14, criterion 1, the caret and the scroll; the real core and the document service on the lane's demo stack, PostgreSQL in Podman): the heading "Payment terms" starts out of view, is chosen in the pane, the caret is in its text (read from the editor's shadow-root selection) and the heading lies in the viewport. Chromium and Firefox passed; WebKit is skipped (its caret read did not match, not investigated). Criterion 5 (viewer case, same file): the viewer's navigation pane lists the heading, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (5 passed, 1 skipped in the three-browser run). Run: `DOCEDIT_E2E_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit pnpm exec playwright test -c apps/reference-host/playwright.config.ts e2e/v-ux-020-navigate.spec.ts` with the stack on ports 57260 to 57263 and DOCEDIT_DEMO_REUSE=1. Earlier evidence: packages/ui/e2e/panes.spec.ts, "the navigation pane (UX-020)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13): the headings are a tree of tree items with `aria-level` and `aria-expanded`, each with its name, and the tree moves by arrow keys (criterion 4). Choosing a heading calls `selectBlock` for it, and a search lists its results with a count and goes to the one chosen (criteria 1 and 3, in part). Lane U-UX-016-030 (criterion 3, the context and the count): packages/ui/e2e/ux-020-search-context.spec.ts, 1 test passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.config.ts e2e/ux-020-search-context.spec.ts`, run from packages/ui with DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56261): the text before and after the match and the marked match equal the expected context of the body text, with one result counted.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 2 and 3):** packages/engine/test/ux-020-navigation.test.ts (Node.js, 8 tests passed): on a core with 600 extra paragraphs a keystroke that leaves the headings as they were tells the `outline` view's subscribers nothing and keeps the same list; typing in a heading is one notification in the next pass, and the items of the other headings are the same objects as before (reuse); a style change adds and removes a heading in order; the find the pane uses lists each match with its block and the text before and after, counts them, honours `matchCase: false` (the engine's own default stays case-sensitive, ADR 0108), draws the same matches as it counts, and `selectFindMatch` selects the chosen match, makes it current, asks for the scroll and refuses a result that is gone or that now names other text. Measurement (how and where: a scratch Node.js test, not kept, on the headless core of packages/engine, a body of 3005 paragraphs of which 61 are headings, 300 one-character keystrokes at the same place, Node.js 22.18 on an Intel Core i9-10900KF under Windows 11, other lanes running at the same time): the whole keystroke dispatch took a median of 3.17 ms and a 95th percentile of 6.41 ms, and `outlineOf` alone after such an edit a median of 0.37 ms and a 95th percentile of 1.23 ms. This is the core's side only, not a keystroke-to-paint measurement in a browser (that is FND-007's); the pane's rendering is a separate frame. packages/ui/test/ux-020-place.test.ts (Node.js, 5 tests passed, with outline-tree.test.ts 11 passed): `placeAfter` keeps the heading while it is listed and otherwise gives the nearest earlier one still listed. packages/ui/e2e/ux-020-outline-place.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 6 passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56330 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-020-outline-place --retries=0`): when the focused heading is removed, the place and the focus move to the heading before it, and focus is not taken from the page when the tree was not focused. packages/element/e2e/ux-020-navigation.spec.ts (the mounted element on the real core, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 6 passed, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56340 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts ux-020-navigation --retries=0`): a search lists its matches with the marked query and the count, choosing the last one makes it the selection in the editing surface and inside the viewport, and "Match case" changes the result to "No results".

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 2 and 3 ticked, see the Evidence line of lane w3. The element's shell now passes `search` and `selectMatch` (packages/element/src/editor.ts), which it did not before, so the pane's search form was not offered in the product; they run on the core's find (`findText`, `findResults`, the new `selectFindMatch`). Decisions taken here, proposed and not the owner's: the pane's "match case" is an option of the find (`FindState.matchCase`, absent when case matters), and the pane's default (unticked) ignores case while the engine's default stays case-sensitive (ADR 0108, FE-059 Notes; the match options remain an open question there); "whole word" is not offered by the pane and is ignored by the adapter; the result list is not capped, so a very common query lists every match (a cap is a design choice for the owner or a later story); the search marks the matches in the document, as the find does, until the field is searched empty or the pane closes. The nearest-earlier-heading rule for the author's place when a heading is removed is the pane's own choice. Earlier notes follow. Lane V-UX-016-030, round 3 (2026-11-14): criterion 1 is ticked on the reference host's real core (see Evidence); criteria 2 and 5 stay open. Criterion 2 (typing undelayed) is not measured. Criterion 5 is ticked on the viewer case only (the demo's viewer role, Vic, may edit no region, and the heading "Payment terms" is still listed): apps/reference-host/e2e/v-ux-020-navigate.spec.ts, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. A per-region lock (PRM-048) is not set up in the demo, so the region-only case is not separately shown. Page thumbnails are UX-021 (phase 3). Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open: the scroll into view belongs to the core and is not checked. Criterion 2 stays open: the test shows that focus stays on the same heading after a change, but not that typing is undelayed. Criterion 3 stays open: the context and the count are checked now (lane U-UX-016-030, see Evidence), but the move to the match is the core's `selectMatch`, which the harness fakes, so the end-to-end move is not shown. Criterion 5 is not tested. Lane UX-014-026, round 1: `shell/outline.ts` carries no region, so a Node test of the tree would not reach the rule; it needs the core's `outline()` answer and the browser. Round 3 (ui packet, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/outline-tree.test.ts checks the tree (properties): every heading kept once in document order, each heading nested under the nearest earlier heading of a lower level, depth and ancestors consistent, siblings numbered for aria-posinset and aria-setsize. This is evidence for the nested part of criterion 1 only; criterion 1 stays open for the caret move and the scroll, which are the core's. The pane's own code filters only collapsed branches, not by region, but the core's `outline()` answer is not checked here.

### UX-021 Navigation pane: page thumbnails

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 3 | M | could | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §12 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-020, FE-078, FE-095

As an author, I want page thumbnails once real pages exist, so that I can see and jump between pages.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the read view or the print view with a layout tree, when the pane lists thumbnails, then only those near the viewport are rendered and the rest are virtualised.
- [ ] Given a thumbnail, when it is chosen, then the view moves to that page, and the current page is marked.
- [ ] Given the web view, when the pane renders, then no thumbnails are offered, because the web view shows no pages.
- [ ] Given a screen reader, when a thumbnail is reached, then it is named "page n of m" in the current locale.

**Notes:** Phase 3 is gated on the evidence review at the end of phase 2 (11 section 5). Round 4 (lane U-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13): not started. Its depends-on FE-078 has criterion 1 ticked and criterion 2 open, and FE-095 (the own page painter, which the read view and thumbnails need) has no criteria ticked and no code under packages/ui or packages/painter for thumbnails. No criterion can be proven without building the layout tree and the painter, which is outside this lane's slice and phase-gated. Nothing ticked. Lane w3 (2026-11-17): looked again after the merge of stories/integration; still nothing built and nothing ticked. The painter and the layout tree now exist (packages/painter, packages/layout), but nothing in packages/ui, packages/element or packages/engine imports the painter, so there is no read view in the shell to move to a page, and `CoreApi` offers only the page count (`DocumentStats.pages`), with no page content to paint a thumbnail from and no command to move the view to a page. FE-095 criteria 1, 2 and 4 (painted pages in the element) and FE-078 criteria 2 and 4 are open. A pane built now could list page numbers but could not draw or jump, so criteria 1, 2 and 4 would be shown on a mock; criterion 3 alone (no thumbnails when the stats have no pages) is a one-line `available` check that has no pane to guard. The owner's answers of 2026-11-15 do not touch this story, and phase 3 is still gated on the evidence review of 11 section 5.

### UX-022 Styles gallery and styles pane

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2, §12; 07 §5.3, §7; 03 §4 (`style_def`) · **Requirements:** R1, R3, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-004, FE-127, FE-128, FE-129

As an author, I want to see and apply the document's styles, so that I format by meaning.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given paragraph, character and table styles, when the gallery or the pane opens, then each style is listed with its name and kind, the style in effect at the caret is marked, and choosing one applies it to the selection.
- [x] Given a user who may edit only regions, when they apply an existing style inside a region, then it works; when they try to create, change or delete a style, then those commands are disabled with a reason (07 sections 5.3, 7).
- [x] Given a style change that 07 section 5.3 refuses (a region that uses the style is `approved`), when the user attempts it, then they are told which region blocks it.
- [x] Given the user has `suggest` only, when they apply a style, then it is recorded as a formatting suggestion.
- [x] Given a keyboard or screen reader, when the gallery or pane is used, then every style is reachable and exposed with its name and whether it is current.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/e2e/panes.spec.ts, "the styles pane (UX-022)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13): the styles under their kind headings, the style at the caret marked with `aria-current` and "In use at the cursor", and a chosen style applied through `paragraphStyle` and `characterStyle` (criterion 1).

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-022-styles-keyboard.spec.ts (criterion 5, rendered): every style is a named button, `aria-current` is set only on the current one, and Tab from the first style reaches each style in document order (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed with `--retries=0`, run from packages/ui with DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57260).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 to 4):** packages/engine/test/ux-022-style-edit.test.ts (Node.js, 14 tests passed): the core's `styles` view lists the three kinds in definition order with `basedOn`; the selection summary carries `styleId`, `charStyleId` and `tableStyleId`; a user who may edit only a region has `checkStyleEdit` refuse create, change and delete with `no-access`, the three methods change nothing and the refusal notice names `defs`, while applying an existing style inside the region works; locked and suggesting users are refused (`read-only`, `suggestion`); `createStyle`, `changeStyle` and `deleteStyle` make a valid document (checked with `validate`), keep the CDM order of `defs`, refuse a built-in style, a style in use, a taken or empty name, a parent of another kind and a loop; a style a region uses is refused to a user with `structure` and without `edit` in the region, naming the region (`scopes` `defs` and `region:<id>` in the refusal notice), through `basedOn` as well, and passes with `manage` or `edit` in it. packages/engine/test/ux-022-styles.test.ts (3 tests passed, criterion 4): a user with `suggest` only (effective mode `review`) applying a paragraph style, a character style or a table style gets a `fmt` suggestion naming the user with the old value in `before`; resolving the table suggestion by rejecting restores the old style and accepting keeps it (`resolveSuggestions`). packages/ui/test/ux-022-style-edit.test.ts (Node.js, 6 tests passed): which reason the shell shows, and the approved regions that use a style when the core allows the edit. packages/ui/e2e/ux-022-style-edit.spec.ts (harness core, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 7 tests each passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56330 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-022 --retries=0`, 24 passed with the keyboard spec): the style of each of the three kinds in effect is marked, the controls are disabled with the reason in words and still apply a style, and a note names the region (not editable, or approved) that stops a change. packages/element/e2e/ux-022-styles.spec.ts (the mounted element on the real core and the embed page's backend, Chromium, Firefox, WebKit and Edge, 20 passed, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56340 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts ux-022-styles --retries=0`): the pane lists the document's styles, marks the one at the caret and applies Heading 1 to a paragraph; a commenter who is an editor of one region applies a style inside it and has the three controls disabled with the reason; an owner creates, renames and deletes a style, is refused in words for a taken name, a style in use and a built-in style; a user with `suggest` only applies a style and the document holds a `fmt` mark; and, with the region approved on the server (`MockDocument.setRegionState`) and a fresh editor reading the region list, a change to a style that region uses is refused naming "Middle".

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 to 4 ticked, see the Evidence line of lane w3. What was built: the element passes `styles`, `styleEdit` and the commands `paragraphStyle`, `characterStyle`, `tableStyle`, `createStyle`, `changeStyle` and `deleteStyle` (packages/element/src/editor.ts), so the pane is offered in the product; the core has `styles`, `checkStyleEdit`, `createStyle`, `changeStyle`, `deleteStyle` (packages/engine/src/core.ts, style-edit.ts); the table style under suggestion mode is now recorded as a block formatting suggestion (`suggestBlockFormat`), where before the transaction was silently dropped. Decisions taken here, proposed and not the owner's: a built-in style is neither renamed nor deleted; a style that something uses (a paragraph, table or run in it, a style based on it or naming it `next`, the default paragraph style) is not deleted; a new style takes a random identifier of the core's own source and a name unused in its kind (without regard to case); a style changes only its name and parent (the other properties are FE-128 and FE-129's dialogs); creating or deleting a style needs `edit` in the `doc` scope as well as `defs`, because the authority's scope diff counts the change of the order of `defs` as `doc`; changing or deleting needs `edit` in each region that uses the style or `manage` (PRM-012), and a style used by a region is "used" as `definitionUse` of the authority works it out. The core cannot see that a region is `approved`, which is the server's state: the shell reads it from the region list and refuses before sending (the server refuses again, PRM-047); a region list that is out of date can let a change through to the server's refusal. The element's region list re-reads when the access changes, not when only a region's state does, so a pane that was open when a region was approved does not know it until the next change of access (found while testing; the browser test mounts a fresh editor after the approval). Not covered: a table style is applied to the table at the caret only (FE-131); the gallery as a toolbar surface (the pane is the surface built); the server's own refusal of a style commit through the element (PRM-012 and PRM-047 have it in the authority's tests). Earlier notes follow. The styles pane is phase 1b by 13 section 12. Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 stays open: applying an existing style inside a region is tested, but refusing create, change and delete with a reason is not. Criterion 3 (approved regions), criterion 4 (suggest-only recorded as a suggestion) and criterion 5 (every style reachable by keyboard) are not tested. Lane UX-014-026, round 1: criterion 2 stays open because the command registry has no create, change or delete style command (`commands/builtin.ts` registers only the apply commands for paragraph and character styles), so there is no disabled command to test. Criterion 3 needs the core's refusal naming the region, which the shell does not receive as a style notice yet. Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 unticked: the test applies only a paragraph and a character style, never a table style (the pane sends `tableStyle`, which is not a `CoreCommandName` and not the engine's `setTableStyle`), `StylesPane.svelte` marks only a paragraph style as current, and the element's `runNamed` (packages/element/src/editor.ts) handles neither `paragraphStyle` nor `characterStyle` nor a `styles` view, so in the product the pane is never offered; criterion 5 stands on the harness core only.

### UX-023 Move between shell landmarks by keyboard

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** PRODUCT (Accessibility); 02 §10; 13 §3, §4 · **Requirements:** R11 · **Depends on:** UX-014, UX-019, FE-103

As an author who works by keyboard, I want Ctrl+F6 to move focus between the parts of the editor, so that I can reach every landmark and leave the component.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given focus anywhere in the editor, when Ctrl+F6 is pressed, then focus moves to the next landmark in the order header, toolbar, page, panes, status bar, and Shift+Ctrl+F6 moves backwards.
- [ ] Given a landmark is absent (the host hid the header, or `toolbar="none"`) or a pane is closed or collapsed, when the landmarks are cycled, then it is skipped.
- [x] Given a browser that reserves Ctrl+F6, when the user wants to cycle landmarks, then the alternative named in the shortcut table (13 §4) works.
- [x] Given each landmark, when assistive technology lists regions, then each has a role and an accessible name, and labelled controls and their labels are in the same root.
- [x] Given focus is anywhere in the component, when the keyboard alone is used, then focus can reach every landmark and also leave the component for the host page.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts, "landmarks by keyboard (UX-023)" (Chromium, 2026-11-13, 3 tests passed): Ctrl+F6 goes header, toolbar, page, panes, status bar and back with Shift+Ctrl+F6, and Alt+F6 moves focus where Ctrl+F6 is reserved (criteria 1 and 3); with the header and toolbar set to none, Ctrl+F6 skips them, and Tab from the last control reaches the host page (criterion 5). Node.js: packages/ui/test/landmarks.test.ts (the order, the skipping of entries that are hidden or unknown, the stepping and wrapping, and the table's keys on both platform forms). Firefox and WebKit not run. Criterion 4 (lane U-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/e2e/ux-023-landmark-roles.spec.ts, 9 tests passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c playwright.config.ts e2e/ux-023-landmark-roles.spec.ts`, run from packages/ui with DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56261; the harness's own build and server): every `[data-landmark]` element of the shell has a role (region, toolbar or complementary) and a name, for the compact and the full toolbar; every `aria-labelledby` in the shell root resolves inside that root. The full toolbar was an unnamed div without a role; it now has role region and the name Editing (packages/ui/src/components/FullToolbar.svelte).

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-14, criterion 2, closed case):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-023-closed-pane.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed, `--retries=0`, DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57260): with no pane open there is no `[data-landmark="pane"]`, and Ctrl+F6 from the page goes status, header, toolbar, page, never a pane.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): re-checked, nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 2 needs a collapsed pane, and neither the shell nor the spec (02 §10, 13 §3, §4, PRODUCT) defines one: a pane is open or closed. Adding a collapse state only to test the skip would invent a feature, so the landmark reading is unchanged. It needs a decision from the owner on whether panes collapse (open question); the closed case is tested. Lane V-UX-016-030, round 4 (2026-11-14): the closed case is now tested in three browsers (see Evidence). Criterion 2 stays open because the collapsed case is not built: the shell has no collapsed state for a pane, and the criterion needs both states. Round 4 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 stays open because a collapsed pane is not tested (a closed pane is skipped in the same browser test). Criterion 4 stays open because the browser test checks each landmark's name but not its role. Escape and Tab inside the editing surface, which let focus out of it, are FE-103's (02 section 10, editing surface). Lane UX-014-026, round 1: criterion 2 stays open for collapsed panes: the landmark order skips entries marked hidden, but the shell has no collapsed state for a pane; the outline's collapse (`panes/OutlinePane.svelte`) hides tree items, not a landmark.

> UX-024 (Announce save state, rejected edits and collaborators in live regions) was merged into FE-107 during the cross-check of 2026-11-06.

### UX-101 Export dialog with progress and diagnostics

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3; 09 §9.1, §9.2; 00 §4 (R5); 05 §5.6 · **Requirements:** R5, R11 · **Depends on:** UX-003, UX-019, EXP-004, EXP-005, EXP-065, PRO-063, PRO-064

As an author, I want to export a document, follow the job and jump from each diagnostic to the place it concerns, so that I can fix problems before I share the result.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the export command, when I open it, then I can choose among the targets the deployment announces (the PDF and the LaTeX bundle first) and the options EXP-005 defines.
- [x] Given a running job, then its state (`queued`, `converting`, `compiling`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`) and progress are shown from `export.progress` events, and I can cancel it.
- [x] Given a completed job, then I can open or download the result and see the page count and whether page references converged (09 §9.1).
- [x] Given diagnostics, when I choose one, then the editor scrolls to and selects the block it names, and its severity is given in text, not by colour alone.
- [x] From phase 2, given my access carries `no-export`, then the command is unavailable and says why (UX-040).
- [x] Given the job completes or fails, then a live region announces it.

**Evidence:** `packages/ui/test/export-diagnostics.test.ts` (the terminal-state announcements, their keys in `en-GB.ts`, the rule in `src/components/ExportDialog.svelte` and the `role="status"` region in `src/App.svelte`). Checked in the source, not in a browser run.

**Evidence (lane U-UX-091-104, round 3):** `packages/ui/e2e/regions.spec.ts`, "the export dialog (UX-101)" (three tests), run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/regions.spec.ts -g "UX-101"` in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (9 passed, on 2026-11-13). Criteria 1 to 4 are proven against the harness's export service (`e2e/` fake core), which the dialog reads through `ExportService`; the product's element does not yet pass an exports port to the core (see Notes).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17):** `packages/element/src/export-service.ts` (new) is the element's `ExportService` over the backend's exports port; `packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts` (`#exportService`, `#onExportJob`) and `src/editor.ts` (`MountOptions.exports`) pass it to the shell. `packages/element/test/export-service.test.ts` (6 tests, Node.js): the target list, the request body with the EXP-005 options unchanged, the job view through `onProgress`, cancel, and the refusals under `no-export` and without a document. `packages/element/e2e/ux-101-export-dialog.spec.ts` (4 tests; 4 passed in Chromium, 8 in Firefox and WebKit, 2026-11-17, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57331 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-101-export-dialog.spec.ts --project=<browser>`), run on the product's element with the demo backend plus a stand-in exports port: the dialog lists PDF then LaTeX bundle, sends `pdfStandard`, `locale` and `timeZone` with the other options and omits those left empty, follows the job to "Finished" with page count and convergence, cancels a running job, and a diagnostic (shown as "Warning" or "Error" in words) closes the dialog with the engine core's selection in the named block (`core.selectionScope().blockId`), while a block no longer in the document keeps the dialog open and says so (criterion 4). Criterion 5: `packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-export-command-state.spec.ts` and `ux-040-export-restrictions.spec.ts` (the export command is disabled with `reason.query.no-export`; the dialog's targets are disabled with the reason and no request is sent), run with the UX-101 tests of `regions.spec.ts` on 2026-11-17: 21 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57332 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-040-export regions.spec.ts -g "UX-101|no-export|export"`). The service half of `no-export` is UX-040's.

**Notes:** added during the cross-check of 2026-11-06: EXP's diagnostics stories assumed this dialog, which no epic owned. The PDF panel of phase 1a is FE-071.

Lane ui-3, round 1: criteria 2 to 5 stay unticked. The dialog has the labels, the progress element, the cancel button, the diagnostic jump in code, but the `export.progress` feed, the cancel and the block selection need a running export and a browser run. The `no-export` gate (criterion 5) is phase 2 and is not built. The severity words are tested in `packages/ui/test/export-diagnostics.test.ts`, which is evidence for criterion 4 only, not a tick. Review, round 1 (2026-11-13): the Node.js test reads the component source; the ticks of criterion 6 here and UX-104 criterion 4 stand on the browser tests 'the export dialog (UX-101)' in `packages/ui/e2e/regions.spec.ts`, which the reviewer ran in Chromium (3 passed). They also cover criteria 1 to 4 of this story in part; the lane may tick those after its own run.

Lane UX-092-104, round 4 (2026-11-13): no criterion ticked. `packages/element/src` never sets `exports` on the core (no `ExportService` anywhere in the element), so `App.svelte` mounts this dialog only when the core has an exports port, and in the product it has none; `packages/element/src/export.ts` says there is no progress stream and no cancel (PRO-086). Criteria 1 to 4 need that adapter and a browser run; criterion 5 is phase 2. Decisions needed are listed in docs/adr/0238-lane-UX-092-104.md (status: proposed). Lane U-UX-091-104, round 3 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 to 4 ticked on the browser run above (the dialog's behaviour, in three browsers). The product path is still open: `packages/element` mounts the dialog only with an exports port, and the element's `ExportService` adapter (`packages/element/src/export.ts`, PRO-086) is not passed to the core, so a user of the element does not yet see the announced targets; that wiring is not in this lane's range. Criterion 5 (`no-export`) is phase 2 and stays open.

Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 unticked because the dialog offers only `profile`, `tagging` and `fields` (`ExportOptions` in `packages/ui/src/core-api.ts`), not `pdfStandard`, `locale` or `timeZone` that EXP-005 and 09 section 9.1 define, and the targets come only from the harness's `FakeExports`. Criterion 4 unticked because the browser test only checks that `selectBlock` was called on the fake core; no real core implements `selectBlock` (nothing in `packages/element/src`), so nothing scrolls to or selects a block (the severity words in the dialog are tested and hold).

Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 4 and 5 ticked (see Evidence). The product path is now wired: the element builds the export service and the engine core's `selectBlock` (UX-067) does the selection. The dialog also offers `pdfStandard`, `locale` and `timeZone` now, so the audit's reason against criterion 1 on options is met. Criterion 1 stays unticked for the other half: "the targets the deployment announces". The protocol has no way to announce them (the service's `exportTargets` is configuration the client cannot read), so the element offers the fixed list PDF and LaTeX bundle. That is a stop-gap, proposed for the owner in `docs/adr/0382-w3-UX-101-export-targets.md` (recommended: a field of the capabilities, 05 §8.3). `EXPORT_TARGETS` in `packages/element/src/export-service.ts` is the one place to change when it is decided. Not checked against a running document service: the tests use a stand-in exports port.

### UX-102 Rulers for indents, tab stops and margins

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §2 (rulers); 02 §3 (part `ruler`), §7, §10 · **Requirements:** R1, R11, R14 · **Depends on:** FE-104, FE-129, FE-137

As an author, I want rulers that show and adjust indents, tab stops and margins, so that I can lay out paragraphs directly.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the caret in a paragraph, then the horizontal ruler shows its left, right and first-line indents and its tab stops.
- [ ] Given I drag an indent marker or a tab stop, then the paragraph changes through the same commands as the paragraph dialog (FE-129).
- [ ] Given any drag on the ruler, then the paragraph dialog or the keyboard offers the same change (02 §10; FE-104).
- [ ] Given the web view, then margins on the ruler are labelled as the PDF's margins and the ruler does not pretend to show pages (02 §7).
- [ ] Given the host's theme, then the ruler is styled only through the `ruler` part and `--de-` properties (02 §3).

**Notes:** added during the cross-check. Phase inferred: 13 §12 does not place rulers. Lane ui-3, round 3 (2026-11-13): no criterion is ticked. The rulers need a paragraph format view from the core (indents and tab stops at the caret), which `CoreApi` does not have, and the paragraph commands of FE-129 (`runNamed`) are not in `CoreCommandName`. The paragraph dialog (FE-104) that the keyboard alternative of criterion 3 needs is not built. Criterion 5 (styling only through the `ruler` part and `--de-` properties) needs a ruler component, which is not written; it is not in `App.svelte` either. Criterion 4 (the margins labelled as the PDF's) needs that component and the view's margins. A ruler model written now would have no caller, so none is written. Lane w3 (2026-11-17): looked again after the merge of stories/integration; nothing built, no tick. The blockers stand. `setParagraphFormat` (packages/engine/src/paragraph-format.ts) exists but is not registered in `core.ts` or `api.ts`, so the ruler's drag (criterion 2) has no command to call; `CoreApi` still has no paragraph format view (indents and tab stops at the caret) for criterion 1, and no `CoreCommandName` for it; FE-129's tab stops and dialog and FE-104's keyboard alternatives (criterion 3) are unbuilt, and FE-137 has its header and footer criterion open. Criteria 4 and 5 could only be shown on a ruler component that nothing mounts; ticking them on that would not show the ruler in the shell, so they stay open. The owner's answers of 2026-11-15 do not touch this story. Revisit when FE-129 registers its command and the core exposes the format view.

## Collaboration experience

### UX-025 Resolve people through the host's directory

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | S | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §11.1; 05 §8.1; 08 §13 · **Requirements:** R7, R10 · **Depends on:** PRO-074, PRO-087, PRO-080

As an integrating developer, I want the editor to ask my application for names and avatars, so that the document service keeps no profile data.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given user identifiers appear (presence, comments, history, mentions), when names are needed, then the editor calls `resolveUsers(ids)` with unique identifiers in batches and caches the answers for the session.
- [x] Given the host provides no `resolveUsers`, when a name is needed, then the editor falls back to the name the service reported, and then to a neutral label; nothing fails.
- [x] Given `resolveUsers` rejects or is slow, when names are shown, then the fallback label is shown, editing is not blocked, and the request is retried later.
- [x] Given a name or avatar address returned by the host, when it is rendered, then it is rendered as text or as an image address, never as markup.
- [x] Given network traffic to the document service, when it is inspected, then it contains no names or avatars supplied by `resolveUsers`.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/people/directory.ts; packages/ui/test/people.test.ts (one call per task with unique identifiers, batches, session cache including unknown, service name then neutral fallback, slow, rejecting and throwing hosts, retry with growing delays, names kept as strings, avatar addresses limited to image sources); packages/ui/e2e/header.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (names from the host, the fallback label Collaborator, markup in a name shown as text and a javascript: avatar not used, editing goes on while the directory fails and it is retried). Criterion 5 (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-14): apps/reference-host/e2e/v-ux-025-traffic.spec.ts, 3 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, on the lane's own demo stack (see Notes).

**Notes:** The result shape of `resolveUsers` and `searchPrincipals` is not defined in 05 (open questions); the shell uses the shape S6 fixed (G92, `packages/protocol/src/ports/host.ts`). Criterion 5 stays open: the directory has no path to the core (packages/ui/test/people.test.ts), but the network traffic can only be inspected end to end with `backend-dep`. Lane UX-014-026, round 2 (2026-11-13): still open. The shell wires the directory only in a browser (App.svelte), so a Node capture would never see a directory name and would prove nothing. The check needs a browser run of the shell against a DEP server, with the captured frames searched for a name that `resolveUsers` returned. Lane V-UX-016-030, round 3 (2026-11-14): criterion 5 is ticked on apps/reference-host/e2e/v-ux-025-traffic.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed, against the lane's own demo stack on ports 57260 to 57263). Two people open the seeded document; Olivia's page gets Ada's name from the host's directory route (asserted, so resolveUsers was called), and every request, response body and WebSocket frame of the document service is searched for the four seeded names. The welcome frame names the signed-in user from the token the service was given; that one `"user"` object is removed before the search, and nothing else is. Not claimed: the token echo itself is not a resolveUsers name, and the check does not cover the export worker or the presence frames as separate items.

### UX-026 Presence avatars in the header

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1a | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §5, §12; 05 §5.4 · **Requirements:** R7 · **Depends on:** UX-014, UX-025, SYN-035

As an author, I want to see who else is in the document, so that I know I am not working alone.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given other sessions are in the document, when `presence.state` arrives, then the header shows each person by avatar and name, and arrivals and departures update it live.
- [x] Given a person is `active` or `idle`, when they are shown, then the two states are distinguishable without colour.
- [x] Given more people than fit, when the header lays out, then an overflow indicator with a count is shown and the full list is reachable by keyboard.
- [x] Given each person, when assistive technology reads the list, then every entry has a text name.
- [x] Given the backend does not announce `presence`, when the header renders, then no presence region or error is shown.
- [x] Given read restrictions (ADR 0019), when the header shows people, then it lists only the sessions in the core's presence view, which is the reader's projection, and nothing of a session the view leaves out.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/src/components/Presence.svelte and packages/ui/src/shell/presence.ts; packages/ui/e2e/header.spec.ts in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: people by avatar and name from presence, arrivals and departures live; idle with a dashed outline and idle in the name; at 480 px with ten people a +n button with its count in words opens the full list by keyboard and Escape closes it; every entry has a text name, the neutral label when nobody knows it; no presence region without the presence feature; a session the core withdraws disappears; packages/ui/test/shell-logic.test.ts (one entry per person, fitting); packages/ui/e2e/a11y.spec.ts.

**Notes:** Whether one person with several sessions appears once is not specified; the shell shows them once, active when any of their sessions is (proposed in ADR 0041). The last criterion was added on 2026-11-06 under rule 8 of the README (S12: presence serves the requester's projection).

### UX-027 Collaborator carets with name flags

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §5; 02 §3 (forced colours); 05 §5.4 · **Requirements:** R7 · **Depends on:** UX-025, SYN-035, SYN-036, FE-066

As an author, I want to see where others are typing, so that we avoid working on the same sentence.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a peer's presence selection, when it arrives, then their caret and selection are drawn at the position mapped through the local steps, with a flag naming them.
- [x] Given concurrent local and remote edits, when the document changes, then each peer's caret stays on the same text (tested with scripted concurrent edits).
- [x] Given a peer's position cannot be mapped, when the caret would be drawn, then it is hidden rather than drawn in the wrong place.
- [x] Given the flag, when it is drawn, then it names the person in text and does not rely on colour; flag, caret and selection use system colours under `forced-colors`.
- [x] Given a flag overlaps text, when the author types or clicks, then the flag neither takes focus nor blocks pointer events on the text.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/carets.test.ts (criterion 3: `drawnPeers` in packages/ui/src/shell/carets.ts draws exactly the peers with a mapped caret, with their selection, and nothing of an unmapped peer; fast-check properties over generated peers; the colour slot does not depend on order). `Carets.svelte` renders only what `drawnPeers` returns. Node.js run of 2026-11-13: 4 tests passed. The browser test 'draws a flag naming each person' in packages/ui/e2e/shell.spec.ts covers the same rule and the flags' pointer and focus behaviour, but was not run in this round.

**Evidence (round 3, lane UX-027-039):** packages/ui/test/ux-027-presentation.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13, 7 tests passed, with carets.test.ts: 11 passed in total). Criterion 4 is checked as source and was unticked in review (2026-11-13): the forced-colours rules of `shell.css` give the active caret, the flag and the selection system colours, with `forced-color-adjust: none`, and `Carets.svelte` builds the flag text from the person's name, but the idle caret rule `.de-peer-caret[data-status='idle']` (specificity 0,2,0) outranks the forced-colours `.de-peer-caret` rule (0,1,0), so an idle caret keeps its `repeating-linear-gradient` in `currentColor` set from `--_de-page-text`, a hard-coded colour, not a system colour. The test does not check the idle state. The criterion waits for a forced-colours rule for the idle caret and a test of it (or a browser run). Fix (round 3, 2026-11-13): the forced-colours block of `shell.css` now has its own `.de-peer-caret[data-status='idle']` rule (dashes in `CanvasText`, `color: CanvasText`), and `ux-027-presentation.test.ts` works out the cascade with forced colours active (importance, specificity, source order) for every element `Carets.svelte` draws (caret active and idle, flag and selection, colour slots 1 to 8): each winning `background` and `color` comes from the forced-colours block and names only system colours, `forced-color-adjust` is `none`, and the markup sets no inline colour. A mutation test removes the forced idle rule and shows the model then reports the base idle rule winning. Node.js run of 2026-11-13: ux-027-presentation.test.ts and carets.test.ts, 15 tests passed. Criterion 4 is ticked on this cascade model; no rendered forced-colours run in a browser was made. Criterion 5 has a static guard only: `.de-carets` has `pointer-events: none` (inherited by the carets, flags and selections), and the layer has no focusable element and is `aria-hidden`. These are source checks of the stylesheet and markup, not a rendered forced-colours run and not a run of the author typing or clicking; neither is claimed.

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-027-flag-pointer.spec.ts, rendered in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-027-flag-pointer`, 6 passed, run with DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57260, `--retries=0`): the element under the centre of each flag and caret is not in the carets layer, the flags compute `pointer-events: none`, a click on a flag leaves focus out of the layer, and typing stays in the page (criterion 5). Criterion 4 also has a rendered forced-colours run in packages/ui/e2e/ux-027-carets.spec.ts (Chromium only, `forcedColors: 'active'`, passed with `--retries=0`).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 and 2):** packages/element/e2e/ux-027-carets.spec.ts (two `<doc-editor>` elements on the embed page's in-memory backend, users Ada and Bob; Chromium, Firefox, WebKit and Edge where the config runs it, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56340 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts ux-027-carets --retries=0`, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit): Bob selects a paragraph by a triple click; Ada's editor draws the selection rectangles and a flag that names Bob, and the union of the drawn rectangles has the same left and right edge, within 3 px, as the box the paragraph's text occupies in Ada's own surface (measured with a DOM range) and shares its line; Ada then types a sentence in front of that paragraph, the text moves, and the drawn selection moves with it, again within 3 px of the text. packages/engine/test/ux-027-concurrent-carets.test.ts (Node.js, 3 tests passed): two real cores on the mock service with presence on; Ada selects "Between" and Bob selects "world"; the server holds the writes, both edit at once (both insert at the start of the intro paragraph; one deletes six characters while the other inserts before it; edits in the two paragraphs the selections lie in), the writes are released and the commits are rebased; after both cores converge each core's `peerSelections` for the other covers exactly the text the other selected ("world" and "Between"), the document the same in both and changed. The mapping itself is SYN-036's (packages/engine/test/presence-mapping.test.ts).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 and 2 ticked, see the Evidence line of lane w3. Criterion 1 is shown on the mounted element with real rectangles, not on the shell's harness; criterion 2 is shown on the positions the core holds (`peerSelections`), which the body view turns into the rectangles of criterion 1, and not on the rectangles of two browsers editing at the same moment. The scripted edits are three, not a search over many. Earlier notes follow. Phase inferred (1b): 13 section 12 places the header presence in 1a, and 11 section 4 places presence in the 1b sync scope. Lane ui-5, round 2 (2026-11-13): criterion 3 ticked. Criterion 1 stays open: the shell draws the rectangles the core reports, and the mapping through local steps is the core's. Criterion 2 stays open: concurrent scripted edits belong to the core (SYN). Criterion 4 stays open: the forced-colours rule is in `shell.css` and the browser test in shell.spec.ts was not run in this round. Criterion 5 stays open for the same reason (the pointer and focus check is the browser test only). Round 3 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 was ticked on the source checks in `ux-027-presentation.test.ts` (a stylesheet and markup check that the rules say what the criterion says; the rendered forced-colours run is not made) and unticked in review: the idle caret is not covered by the forced-colours rule (see Evidence, round 3). Fixed in the same round and re-ticked on the cascade test; a rendered forced-colours run (for example Playwright's `forcedColors: 'active'`) is still worth adding to shell.spec.ts. The new forced-colours rule makes `packages/ui/test/forced-colors.test.ts` (UX-062), which expects exactly 10 rules in the block, count 11; that test belongs to UX-062 and was not changed here. Criterion 5 stays open: the static guard shows the mechanism, not the behaviour when the author types or clicks, which needs a browser run. The round also changed behaviour: a peer with no mapped caret now draws no selection either, as `PeerCaret` in core-api.ts already states; before this round its selection was still drawn. Lane V-UX-016-030 (2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked on the rendered run in packages/ui/e2e/ux-027-flag-pointer.spec.ts (see the Evidence line above); criterion 4 is backed by the forced-colours run in ux-027-carets.spec.ts.
