Criteria 1 and 2 stay open: the mapping and the concurrent edits belong to the core.

### UX-028 Start a comment thread on a range or a block

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5; ADR 0015; 07 §6, §12; 05 §6.2 (threads) · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-025, UX-004, PRO-059, PRO-060, SYN-032, FE-066

As a commenter, I want to comment on a passage or on a figure, table or equation, so that my remark is tied to what it is about.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given `comment` in every scope the selection covers, when the author adds a comment on a text range, then a thread is created with `target.range { from, to, baseSeq }` and the first comment, it appears in the document and the pane, and no commit is made.
- [x] Given a figure, a table or an equation is selected as a block, when the author comments, then the thread targets `{ block: id }`.
- [x] Given the user lacks `comment` in a covered scope, when the command is offered, then it is disabled with a reason; given the server refuses the request (permissions changed, or `baseSeq` outside the rebase window), then the typed text is kept and the reason is shown.
- [x] Given the document is `locked`, a region is `approved`, or a workflow is active, when the author comments, then it still works, because commenting never changes the document.
- [x] Given a document already has 5,000 threads, when another is created, then it is refused, nothing is created and the user is told.
- [x] Given the commented range moves because of local or remote edits, when the document changes, then the highlight stays on the same text.
- [ ] Given another client creates a thread, when `thread.created` arrives, then it appears without a reload.

**Evidence (round 8 fix, lane ui-2):** packages/ui/e2e/comments.spec.ts (Playwright, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 2026-11-13: 7 tests, 21 runs passed, run with `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium` then `firefox,webkit`). Criterion 2: the test runs the comment command `review.comment` through the registry (packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts, registered in packages/ui/src/App.svelte when the core has `threads`) on a block anchor, types in the comment box it opens (packages/ui/src/components/CommentBox.svelte), posts, and reads the request the core's `threads.create` received: `{ target: { block: 'fig1' }, body }`, with no `range` key and no core command run. The box calls `createCommentThread` with the anchor the command took, its scopes and `threads.count()`. The same file shows a text range giving `{ range: { from, to, baseSeq } }`, the command disabled with `reason.query.noComment` when a covered scope lacks `comment`, and a `permission_denied` answer keeping the typed text with the reason shown. The `threads` port is new in packages/ui/src/core-api.ts (`ThreadsService`); the fake is `FakeThreads` in packages/ui/test/support/fake-core.ts. Text model and refusal mapping: packages/ui/test/comment-composer.test.ts (Node.js).

**Evidence (round 8, lane ui-2):** packages/ui/test/ux-028-comment-create.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13), `createCommentThread` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-create.ts. Criterion 2 ticked on the controller: a block selection gives `{ block: id }`, a text range gives `{ range: { from, to, baseSeq } }` with no block key. The same file checks the refusals the controller owns (criterion 3 for a scope without `comment`, criterion 5 at 5,000 threads, the draft kept on refusal). No core request or rendered command is built. Criterion 2 was unticked again in review (round 8, 2026-11-13): `comment-create.ts` is imported by its test only, so no comment command, selection or core call uses it; it is the same reason as in the round 2 review. It waits for the comment command and a run that shows the target.

**Evidence (round 2, lane UX-027-039):** packages/ui/test/thread-target.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13, round 2, lane UX-027-039): `threadTarget` in packages/ui/src/shell/thread-target.ts gives `{ block: id }` for a block selection (figure, table or equation by identifier) and never a `range` key beside it (criterion 2, examples and a property test). The same file checks the range target's shape and the 5,000-thread gate (`threadLimitGate`). Criterion 2 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): it describes what the comment command sends when the author comments on a selected block, not a function's result; `thread-target.ts` is imported by its test only, and no command, selection or core call uses it. It waits for the comment command and a run that shows the target.

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** criterion 3 in packages/ui/e2e/comments.spec.ts (Chromium, 2026-11-13, 7 passed: the command disabled with `reason.query.noComment` and no box, and the `permission_denied` refusal keeping the text with its reason) and packages/ui/e2e/ux-028-stale-base.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, the `stale_base` refusal keeping the text with the rebase-window reason). Criterion 5 in packages/ui/e2e/prm-040-thread-limit.spec.ts (Chromium, 2026-11-13, 3 passed: at 5,000 threads the box explains why, keeps the text and sends nothing; at 4,999 it posts). Run on DOCEDIT_UI_PORT 57261; the shared `test-results` folder raced in parallel runs, so these were re-run one at a time where a trace file went missing.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 4 and 6):** Criterion 4: packages/ui/e2e/ux-028-frozen-content.spec.ts (4 tests per browser, 12 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56331 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-028-frozen`, 2026-11-18): with the access limit `locked`, `archived`, `approved` or `workflow` and the capabilities view and comment, an editing command (`format.bold`) is disabled while `review.comment` is offered and enabled, the box posts, the thread request is `{ target: { block }, body }`, no command of the core runs and nothing is named or committed, and the status line says 'Comment added.'. This is the shell with the harness core, like the evidence of criteria 2, 3 and 5: the element has no `threads` port yet, so the service's answer under a lock is not run. Criterion 6: packages/engine/src/view/ux-028-range-follows.universal.test.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed, `pnpm exec vitest run --project browser packages/engine/src/view/ux-028-range-follows.universal.test.ts`): in the mounted body view the `de-comment-range` element covers the same four words after an insertion before the range (the range moves right), an insertion inside it (it grows and shows the old words with the new), an insertion after it, and a deletion before it (it moves left); the thread stays attached. Remote edits go through the same mapping: packages/engine/test/fe-048-range-agreement.test.ts and fe-048-thread-service.test.ts (FE-048 criteria 2 and 3, the client's range equals the authority's after local and remote commits, Node.js).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 4 and 6 ticked. Criteria 1 and 7 stay open: the element has no `threads` port (no `anchor`, `list` or `create` over the backend's review port, `createThread` in packages/backend-dep/src/review.ts, and no handling of `thread.created`), so a thread cannot be created in the product, appear in the pane, or arrive from another client; the harness core is a fake. That wiring is a feature across engine, element and the sync client (the same gap is recorded in FE-048 criterion 1), not a test gap. Earlier: Lane V-UX-016-030 (2026-11-13): criteria 3 and 5 ticked on the browser runs above. Criterion 1 stays open: a created thread is not added to the pane (the pane reads its list when it opens, and the fake port does not push the new thread), and the no-commit rule is tested only by the request shape. Criteria 4, 6 and 7 stay open (a locked document, the range mapping and `thread.created`). Round 8 fix (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 re-ticked on the comment command and the comment box (see Evidence), which answers the review's "no caller". Choices made here, not stated in the spec: the command is `review.comment` (review group, context menu on text, tables and pictures, feature `comments`, no key); the core offers a new optional `threads` port (`anchor`, `count`, `create`) in core-api.ts, which the engine and the element do not implement yet; the service codes are read as `permission_denied` for changed permissions and `stale_base` for a `baseSeq` outside the rebase window (05 §5.8), and `threadLimit` stays the working code. Criterion 3 now has its UI in the browser test (the disabled command with its reason, the kept draft with the service's reason) but is not ticked in this fix; criterion 1 still needs the pane and the engine's request, and criteria 4 to 7 are unchanged. Round 8 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 ticked on `createCommentThread` (`comment-create.ts`), which answers the round 2 review's "no command uses it". Still open: criterion 1 (the core's request, the no-commit rule and the pane), criterion 3's UI half (the disabled control and its reason are not drawn; the server refusal keeps the draft in `refuseFromServer`), criterion 4 (the controller takes no document state, so the rule is the same when locked; the core must report the scope's capabilities), criterion 5's UI half (the message is not shown), and criteria 6 and 7 (the core's range mapping and `thread.created`). The comment body is the typed text as given, since the body format is not defined. The error code for the 5,000-thread limit is not named in the spec. The comment body format is not defined in 03 or 05 (open questions). Round 2 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 ticked on the target shape only. Criterion 1 stays open: the thread is created, shown and not committed by the core and the pane, none of which is built. Criterion 3 stays open: the disabled command and the kept draft on refusal are not built; `canReply` in comment-rules.ts is the rule only. Criterion 4 stays open: no browser run and no core command. Criterion 5 stays open: `threadLimitGate` refuses at 5,000 (ADR 0233, decision 5, working code `threadLimit`), but the refusal from the core and the message to the user are not wired. Criteria 6 and 7 need the core's range mapping and the `thread.created` event. The 5,000 limit is the story's figure; the spec does not name the code. Decisions: ADR 0233, decisions 5 and 6.

### UX-029 Reply, edit, resolve, reopen and delete

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5; ADR 0015; 07 §6, §12; 05 §6.1, §6.2 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-028

As a commenter, I want to carry a conversation to its end, so that a question is answered and closed.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a thread, when the user replies, then the reply appears for everyone in the document live.
- [x] Given a comment, when it is shown, then edit and delete are offered only to its author or to a user with `manage`, and a thread can be deleted only by its author or by `manage`.
- [ ] Given `comment` in the thread's scope, when the user resolves or reopens it, then the state changes for everyone live; resolved threads leave the default list but stay reachable by a filter.
- [x] Given the commented text is deleted, when the range collapses, then the thread is shown as detached and stays readable; given the deletion is undone, then the thread is not re-attached.
- [x] Given the user lacks `comment`, when the thread is shown, then the reply box is absent or disabled with a reason.
- [x] Given a change is rejected because the resource changed (`If-Match` fails), when the answer arrives, then the latest state is shown and the user's draft is kept.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/comment-rules.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13, with review-rules.test.ts: 22 tests passed): `canEditComment`, `canDeleteComment` and `canDeleteThread` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-rules.ts allow the author and a user with `manage`, and no one else (criterion 2, a property test over users and capabilities); `canReply` withdraws the reply with the reason `noComment` exactly when `comment` is absent (criterion 5, property test). These are the rules; the menus and the reply box that call them are not built, and nothing but the test imports the module. Criteria 2 and 5 were unticked in review (2026-11-13): they describe what the shell offers and shows, not a function's result; they wait for the comment menu and the reply box and a run that shows them.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 2, 4, 5 and 6):** the thread view is new: packages/ui/src/components/ThreadView.svelte, opened from a row of packages/ui/src/panes/CommentsPane.svelte; the port gained self, changes, detail, eply, ditComment, deleteComment, deleteThread and setState (packages/ui/src/core-api.ts, optional), and the harness's FakeThreads answers them as a service with versions (packages/ui/test/support/fake-core.ts). packages/ui/e2e/ux-029-thread-view.spec.ts (10 tests, 30 runs with the other comment specs: 186 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-029 ux-030 ux-034 comments ux-068 ux-023`, 2026-11-18). Criterion 2: the author of a comment is offered Edit and Delete on hers only and Delete thread on her thread; a user who wrote nothing and has no manage is offered none; with manage all of them (the rules of comment-rules.ts, now called by the view). Edit, delete a comment and delete the thread each send a request with the version. Criterion 4: a detached thread is marked 'The commented text was removed. The thread stays here to read.' and its comments stay readable (view); packages/engine/test/fe-048-core-threads.test.ts, new test 'does not re-attach a detached thread when the deletion is undone' (Node.js, headless core): after the text is deleted and the deletion undone, the text is back, the thread is still detached and no decoration is drawn; redo changes nothing. Criterion 5: without comment the reply box and Post reply are disabled, the reason 'You do not have permission to comment on this part of the document.' is the box's accessible description, and Resolve is not offered. Criterion 6: with the service answering precondition_failed and the latest thread, the view shows the latest comments (the other user's reply), says the thread changed, keeps the typed reply in its box, and a second Post goes through with the new version (the two requests carry different versions). Axe finds no violation in the thread view. Criteria 1 and 3 stay unticked, see Notes.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 2, 4, 5 and 6 are ticked at the shell level, with the harness's threads port, as UX-028's criteria 2, 3 and 5 are. Criteria 1 and 3 stay unticked: they say the reply, the resolve and the reopen reach everyone live, which needs the service's comment.* and 	hread.* events and the element's 	hreads port (not built; the element has no thread wiring, see UX-028 Notes and FE-048 criterion 1). The view follows the port's changes and shows a reply or a state change from another client without a reload (tests 'a reply from another client appears without a reload' and 'resolving elsewhere changes the open view'), and resolved threads leave the default list and stay reachable by the filter; what is missing is the live source in the product. Choices made here, proposed, not in the spec: the port's write methods and their If-Match version (tag) are the editor's working shape; a user without comment sees no Resolve button (the reply box is the place that shows the reason); a mention in a comment is shown as @name text. Whether an author who has lost comment may still edit their comment stays open, as before. Earlier: Round 7 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): the rules for criteria 3, 4 and 6 are in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-threads.ts (`setThreadState`, `anchorAfterRemoval`, `anchorAfterUndo`, `writeOutcome`; `precondition_failed` is the If-Match code of core-api.ts), tested in packages/ui/test/comment-threads.test.ts (Node.js, 34 tests with comment-rules.test.ts passed). No criterion is ticked this round: each one ends in something shown, sent or refreshed (live thread updates, the detached and conflict views, the draft kept on screen), and none of that is built or run in a browser. Detached threads are the cost of ADR 0015 (range collapse; undo does not restore). Round 1 (lane UX-027-039): criteria 2 and 5 are ticked on the rule above. Criteria 1, 3, 4 and 6 stay open: they need the core's thread commands and `thread.*` events, the service's `If-Match` answer and the rendered thread, none of which is built or run in a browser. The rule uses only the spec's wording: whether an author who has lost `comment` may still edit their comment is not specified, and the rule does not decide it.

### UX-030 Comments pane

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §5, §12; 05 §5.6 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-028

As an author, I want a pane that lists the document's threads, so that I can read and answer them in order.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given threads, when the pane opens, then they are listed in document order with author, time, state, target excerpt or block kind, and reply count.
- [x] Given a thread, when it is chosen, then the document scrolls to its range or block and highlights it; given a highlight is chosen in the page, then the thread is selected in the pane.
- [x] Given the filters open or resolved and assigned to a person, when one is chosen, then only the matching threads are listed.
- [ ] Given notifications `thread.*` and `comment.*` arrive, when they do, then the affected threads are refreshed from the control plane and the change is announced.
- [x] Given the header comments button, when threads are open, then it shows the count of unresolved threads with a text equivalent, and `exec` can open the pane.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when the pane is used, then each thread and each comment is reachable and named with its author and state.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 2, 5 and 6):** Criterion 2, the document side: packages/engine/src/view/ux-030-select-thread.universal.test.ts (2 tests; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `pnpm exec vitest run --project browser <file>` with the two other comment-range files, 9 files and 12 tests passed). In the mounted body view `selectThread({ id, block })` on the core (packages/engine/src/core.ts, `selectedThread`) marks the thread's range with `de-comment-selected` (a solid 2px underline and an outline, styled in packages/element/src/styles/element.css; the decoration is in packages/engine/src/comment-decorations.ts) and scrolls it into view: in a 60px scrolling box the box's `scrollTop` moved and the marked text lay inside it. A thread on a block marks the block node. A click on a thread's highlight sets `pickedThread` on the core (`{ id, n }`). None of it changes the document (`doc.eq` before and after). The shell side: `reveal` and `picked` on the threads port (packages/ui/src/core-api.ts), a 'Show in document' button on each row and on opening a thread, and a highlight chosen in the page selecting its row (`aria-current`, focus, scroll, announced) even when a filter hid it (packages/ui/src/panes/CommentsPane.svelte); packages/ui/e2e/ux-030-pane-page.spec.ts, 2 tests of criterion 2, with the harness's fake port. Criterion 5: the comments button counts the open threads the port lists when the host gives no count (packages/ui/src/components/Header.svelte), named 'Comments, N open threads' for a screen reader, following the port's changes (3 to 1 to 3 in the test); a host count is used as given; the button by keyboard and `exec('view.comments')` open the pane (same spec, 2 tests). Criterion 6: each comment in the thread view is a focusable group named by author, time and state (packages/ui/src/components/ThreadView.svelte), the rows keep their names, and Tab goes row, show, open; axe finds no violation (same spec, 1 test). Run: `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56433 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-029 ux-030 ux-034 comments ux-068 ux-023 header --retries=0`, 249 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 2026-11-18. Criterion 4's announcement rule, the part of it that is built: packages/ui/test/ux-030-thread-change.test.ts (Node.js, 4 tests with a property) and the spec's 'a refreshed list is announced' test; criterion 4 stays unticked, see Notes.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/comment-rules.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `filterThreads` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-rules.ts lists open threads by default, resolved ones through the resolved filter, and the threads assigned to one person, in document order; a fast-check property checks that every listed thread matches the state and the assignee, and that none is left out (criterion 3, the filter rule only). Criterion 3 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): the pane and its filter controls are not built, and nothing but the test imports `filterThreads`.

**Evidence (lane V-UX-016-030, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-030-comments-pane.spec.ts, rendered by the real pane (packages/ui/src/panes/CommentsPane.svelte, opened through `view.comments` in the harness with `commentsPane: true`; threads from the port's `list()` in packages/ui/src/core-api.ts, fake in packages/ui/test/support/fake-core.ts). Run with `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-030-comments-pane --retries=0` on DOCEDIT_UI_PORT 57261, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: 15 passed. Criterion 1: document order from threads given out of order, the author, time, state, the excerpt or block kind, the reply count, a detached thread's words, an axe run with no violation. Criterion 3: the open default, the resolved and all filters, the assignee filter combined with the state, and the empty message. Criterion 6 is not ticked: the pane lists threads, not the comments inside them, so the reachability of each comment is not shown; each thread's row is named (`role="group"`, tabindex 0) and the test checks the names.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 2, 5 and 6 ticked at the shell level with the harness's threads port, as UX-028 and UX-029 were; the engine part of criterion 2 runs in a real body view. Criterion 4 stays unticked: the pane reads the list again and announces what changed (new thread, reply, resolve, reopen, delete) whenever the port's `changes` moves, but nothing in the product feeds `changes` from `thread.*` and `comment.*` notices, because the element has no `threads` port (UX-028 Notes, FE-048 criterion 1). What the ticks do not cover: (a) the element does not pass `selectThread`, `pickedThread` and the list to the shell, so in the product the pane is not connected to the body yet; (b) the header count in the product still has no source, since the element has no threads port and `core.counts` has none; the count the shell derives from the list is shown when that port exists; (c) the keyboard run is Tab and Enter in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, with the accessible names read from the accessibility tree, and no screen reader was run; (d) choices made here, not in the spec: choosing a row means a 'Show in document' button (and opening the thread also shows it), the mark is a solid underline with an outline, one change is announced per refresh in the order new thread, reply, state change, deletion, and a highlight chosen in the page shows a thread that a filter hid by switching the list to All. Earlier: Round 7 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): `paneRows` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-threads.ts gives the row model (document order, author, time, state, excerpt or block kind, reply count, and the parts of the accessible name), tested in packages/ui/test/comment-threads.test.ts. No criterion is ticked: the rows are not rendered, and the scroll and highlight, the `thread.*` refresh, the header count and the spoken names are not built or run in a browser. The filters are the minimum implied by resolve and assign; the spec does not list them. Round 1 (lane UX-027-039): criterion 3 is ticked on the filter rule. Criteria 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6 stay open: the rows (author, time, reply count, target excerpt), the scroll and highlight, the live refresh from `thread.*` and `comment.*`, the header count and the keyboard names are not built. Lane V-UX-016-030 (2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 3 ticked on the rendered pane (see Evidence). Choices: the pane reads the list once when it opens, so criterion 4 stays open; the pane's text is the new `comments.*` keys in `src/i18n/en-GB.ts`; the filters are state and assignee only, as the spec gives none other. Criteria 2, 4 and 5 stay open: the scroll and highlight need the core's range mapping, the live refresh needs the `thread.*` and `comment.*` events, and the header count is not wired to the pane. Criterion 6 stays open for the comments inside a thread, which the pane does not list yet.

### UX-031 Mention people in a comment

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12; 05 §6.2, §7 (`dep.comment.mentioned`) · **Requirements:** R8, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-028, UX-025, PRO-059, PRO-060, BE-055

As a commenter, I want to mention a person, so that they are told that I need them.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a comment is being typed, when the mention trigger and a name fragment are typed, then `searchPrincipals` is called, its results are listed, and choosing one inserts a mention node in the comment body.
- [x] Given results arrive out of order, when a newer query exists, then stale results are ignored.
- [x] Given a mention, when it is displayed, then the name comes from `resolveUsers`, and the node stores only the principal identifier.
- [ ] Given the comment is posted, when the service stores it, then `dep.comment.mentioned` is raised and the host receives it (checked end to end in the reference host, UX-098).
- [ ] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when the picker is used, then it is a standard combobox: results are announced and choosing is by keyboard.
- [x] Given the host provides no `searchPrincipals`, when a comment is typed, then mentions are not offered and plain comments still work.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criterion 3 and owner decision 35):** Criterion 3: packages/ui/e2e/ux-031-mention-display.spec.ts (3 tests; Chromium only for the first run, then Chromium, Firefox and WebKit with the neighbouring comment specs: 99 passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56437 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-031 ux-029 ux-030 comments --retries=0`, 2026-11-18). A posted comment is shown in the thread view (packages/ui/src/components/ThreadView.svelte) with each mention as `@` and the name the host's `resolveUsers` gave through the people directory (`shell.people`; the call carries `u2`, `u3`, `nobody`, no `user:` prefix and no group), as text: a name that is markup is shown as the text it is, with no element and no script run. The node keeps `data-principal` only (the body part is `{ type: 'mention', principal }`, packages/ui/src/core-api.ts), and showing it writes nothing to the service. A person the host does not know, a group (which has no lookup), no `resolveUsers` and a failing `resolveUsers` all show the neutral label 'Collaborator'. The node holding only the principal was already checked in packages/ui/e2e/comments.spec.ts and packages/ui/test/comment-composer.test.ts. Owner decision 35 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md, a mention inside content the person cannot see is refused with a message, no access request): same spec, 1 test, and packages/ui/test/ux-031-mention-refusal.test.ts (Node.js): with the service answering `mention_hidden`, the comment box shows 'A person you mentioned cannot see the part of the document this comment is on, so it was not added. Remove the mention to post it. Your text is kept.', keeps the text, offers no access request (no access control, `onAccessRequest` not called), and the same comment without the mention posts.

**Evidence (round 3, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-031-mention-keyboard.spec.ts (2 tests, 4 runs passed across Firefox and WebKit, 2 passed in Chromium; `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-031-mention-keyboard.spec.ts` with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56275` and `DOCEDIT_BROWSERS` set per browser). Criterion 5, keyboard and announcement part only: `@o` lists Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper, ArrowDown and ArrowUp move `aria-activedescendant` between them, Enter chooses Grace Hopper, the shell's live region reads `2 people or groups found.`, and Escape closes the list and keeps the dialog and the text. Not claimed: the `combobox` role (the field is a multi-line textarea; see ADR 0327 Part A).

**Evidence (round 8 fix, lane ui-2):** packages/ui/e2e/comments.spec.ts (Playwright, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 2026-11-13, passed). The comment box (packages/ui/src/components/CommentBox.svelte), opened by the comment command, drives `createMentionPicker`. Criterion 1: typing `@Gr` calls the host's `searchPrincipals` with `{ text: 'Gr', purpose: 'mention', docId }`, the result is listed as an option, Enter puts `@Grace Hopper` in the text, and the posted body holds `{ type: 'mention', principal: 'user:u2' }` between the text parts (axe finds no violation with the list open). Criterion 2: the harness holds the answer for `L` until after the answer for `Le`; the list still shows only the `Le` result after the late answer has arrived. Criterion 6: with no `searchPrincipals`, typing `@Gr` shows no list, the field has no `aria-autocomplete`, nothing is searched, and the plain comment is posted. The text-to-body model (`placeMention`, `bodyParts` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-composer.ts) is tested in packages/ui/test/comment-composer.test.ts (Node.js, with a fast-check property).

**Evidence (round 8, lane ui-2):** packages/ui/test/ux-031-mention-picker.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13), `createMentionPicker` and `mentionTrigger` in packages/ui/src/shell/mention-picker.ts. Criterion 1 (controller level): a trigger calls `searchPrincipals` with the fragment, lists the results and `choose` yields a mention node. Criterion 2: the newest query wins whatever order the answers come in (fast-check property over generated orders). Criterion 6: without `searchPrincipals` nothing is offered or searched. The rendered combobox and the typed comment box are not built. Criteria 1, 2 and 6 were unticked in review (round 8, 2026-11-13): `mention-picker.ts` is imported by its test only, so no comment box calls it and nothing is listed or inserted in the product; they describe what the comment box does, not a controller's result. They wait for the comment box and a run that shows the picker.

**Evidence (round 2, lane UX-027-039):** packages/ui/test/mention-search.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13, round 2, lane UX-027-039): `acceptResults` in packages/ui/src/shell/mention-search.ts shows the results of the newest query only (criterion 2: a unit example and a fast-check property over generated sequences of queries and late results). The same file checks that a mention node holds the principal identifier and no name (criterion 3, rule only) and the rule `mentionsOffered` (criterion 6, rule only). Criterion 2 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): it describes what the picker shows when results arrive out of order, and no picker exists; `mention-search.ts` is imported by its test only. It waits for the picker and a run that shows it.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criterion 3 ticked. Criterion 4 stays unticked: it asks for the service to raise `dep.comment.mentioned` and the host to receive it, checked in the reference host (UX-098), which is not built; the editor only sends the body. Criterion 5 stays unticked: the field is still a multi-line `textarea`, and whether a comment may be multi-line, so that the field could become a single-line combobox, is the owner's question in ADR 0327 Part A (docs/adr/0327-lane-U-UX-031-045.md); the owner's answers of 2026-11-15 do not list it. Owner decision 35 is applied as a refusal the editor shows when the service refuses; the working code `mention_hidden` is the editor's, proposed, because 05 section 5.8 names no code, and the refusal itself belongs to the service (SR-M5, second review): nothing in the editor knows who may see what. Gaps found, not in the criteria: the reply box and the edit box of the thread view take plain text and have no mention picker, so a mention can be made only in the first comment of a thread; an edit of a comment that holds a mention shows it as `@Name` text and saves plain text. The shown name for a group is the neutral label, since `resolveUsers` has no groups. Earlier: Round 3 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 5's keyboard half is proven in three browsers (see Evidence). It stays unticked: the field is a multi-line `textarea`, so the `combobox` role is a design decision for the owner (ADR 0327 Part A). Criterion 3 stays open for a reason found in this round: no screen renders a posted comment body, so nothing displays a mention's name from `resolveUsers`; the display of a thread is not built. Round 8 fix (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2 and 6 re-ticked on the comment box in the browser (see Evidence). Choices made here, not stated in the spec: the box is a plain text field; a chosen mention is shown as `@` and the name and posted as a node with the principal only; a mention whose text the author then edits is posted as plain text. The body is a list of text and mention parts (`CommentBodyPart` in core-api.ts), the working shape while the body format is open. Criterion 5 stays open: the field carries `aria-autocomplete`, `aria-controls` and `aria-activedescendant` and the count of results is announced, but a multi-line `textarea` cannot take the `combobox` role, and no screen-reader run was made. Criteria 3 and 4 are unchanged. Round 8 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2 and 6 ticked on the controller in `mention-picker.ts`, which supersedes the "no picker exists" reason in the round 2 Evidence. Still open: criterion 3 (the name is looked up by `displayName` but not rendered), criterion 4 (the post and `dep.comment.mentioned`, UX-098) and criterion 5 (the combobox and a keyboard run, which need a browser). Trigger rule chosen here, not stated in the spec: `@` at the start of the text or after whitespace, then a non-empty fragment with no whitespace. Round 2 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): the stale-result rule is written and tested (criterion 2 unticked in review, see Evidence). Criterion 1 stays open: the trigger, the `searchPrincipals` call and the combobox are not built. Criterion 3 stays open: the name from `resolveUsers` is not rendered; only the stored node is checked. Criterion 4 stays open: the post and the `dep.comment.mentioned` event are the service's and UX-098's. Criterion 5 stays open: the combobox and the keyboard run are not built. Criterion 6 stays open: `mentionsOffered` is the rule only; the picker and "plain comments still work" are not built. Decisions: ADR 0233, decisions 7 and 8. Earlier note: whether a person without access to the document may be mentioned is the host's decision; the editor shows what the host returns. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M5: under ADR 0019 a mention in a thread inside hidden content would make the host notify someone who cannot read it; the review proposes refusing such a mention, or turning it into a request for access, and carrying the scope in the webhook ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

### UX-032 Assign a thread as a task

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12; 07 §12; 05 §6.2 (`assignee`), §7 (`dep.task.assigned`) · **Requirements:** R8, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-029, UX-025, UX-031, HIS-027

As a commenter, I want to assign a thread to a person, so that a remark becomes a task with an owner.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a thread and `comment` in its scope, when the user assigns it to a person found with `searchPrincipals`, then the thread's `assignee` is set, it is shown as a task with the assignee, and `dep.task.assigned` is raised.
- [ ] Given an assigned thread, when the user reassigns or clears the assignee, then the change is shown to everyone live.
- [x] Given the assignee opens the document, when the comments pane renders, then a filter shows the threads assigned to them, and the same items appear as "for you" items in catch-up (HIS-027, HIS-028).
- [x] Given the editor, when it runs, then it lists no tasks across documents; the host builds that from the webhook.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when a task is shown, then the assignee is exposed as text.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5):** Criterion 1: packages/ui/e2e/ux-032-assign.spec.ts (5 tests; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit with the neighbouring comment specs: 123 passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56441 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-032 ux-031 ux-029 ux-030 ux-034 comments --retries=0`, 2026-11-18). The thread view (packages/ui/src/components/ThreadView.svelte) has an 'Assign to' combobox (a single-line input with `role="combobox"`) for a user with `comment` in the thread's scope when the host gave `searchPrincipals` and the port has `assign`: typing 'Gr' calls `searchPrincipals` with `{ text, docId, purpose: 'participant', kinds: ['user'] }`, lists Grace Hopper, Enter chooses by keyboard, and the port gets one write `assign` with the version (`If-Match`) and `user:u2`; the view then says 'Task, assigned to Grace Hopper.' and the status line 'Thread assigned.'. No core command ran. Without `comment` the field is absent; without `searchPrincipals` it is absent and the task still shows. The search rules (newest query wins, people only, nothing without `searchPrincipals`): packages/ui/test/ux-032-assignee-search.test.ts (Node.js, 4 tests with a property) and `createAssigneeSearch` in packages/ui/src/shell/assignee-search.ts. The event: the document service raised nothing for an assignment before this story; services/document-service/src/control-plane/thread-routes.ts now raises `dep.task.assigned` (data `{ thread, assignee, seq }`) when a PATCH names a person, and none for the same assignee again or for a clear; services/document-service/test/control-plane/ux-032-task-assigned.test.ts (Node.js, memory and PostgreSQL both ran, 2 tests passed, 2026-11-18) with the existing thread tests (pro-060-thread-changes, pro-036-thread-events). Criterion 3: the pane's assignee filter (UX-030) now lists the people by name from `resolveUsers`, and a row says 'Task, assigned to <name>.', in the same spec (last test: the filter lists Ada Lovelace and Grace Hopper, choosing Grace leaves her one thread); the service lists the open threads assigned to the caller as `task` items of the activity (services/document-service/test/control-plane/his-027-activity.test.ts, HIS-027) and the catch-up pane lists them under 'for you', after the mentions (packages/ui/e2e/his-028-catch-up.spec.ts, HIS-028). Criterion 4: packages/ui/test/ux-032-assignee-search.test.ts, 'has no command that lists or opens tasks' (no built-in command id names a task; the header panes are comments, catch-up and history); the editor reads one document's threads and sends nothing about tasks anywhere but the thread's own `PATCH`. Criterion 5: the assignee is text in the thread view, in the pane row and in the filter ('Task, assigned to Grace Hopper.'); the field is a labelled combobox with `aria-expanded`, `aria-controls` and `aria-activedescendant`, and the keys are ArrowDown, ArrowUp, Enter and Escape; axe finds no violation with the list open (same spec). Criterion 2 stays unticked, see Notes.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5 ticked at the shell level with the harness's threads port (as UX-028 and UX-029), plus the service's event. Criterion 2 stays unticked: the open thread view and the list follow the port's `changes` and show another client's assignment or clear without a reload (tests 'reassign and clear change the view, and another client's change appears without a reload', with the harness's `remoteAssign`), but nothing in the product feeds `changes` from `thread.updated`, because the element has no `threads` port (UX-028 Notes, FE-048 criterion 1). What the ticks do not cover: (a) the same gap means a user of the real element cannot yet assign a thread; (b) the service's `dep.task.assigned` carries `{ thread, assignee, seq }`, and the webhook schema (spec/dep/webhooks/dep.task.assigned.v1.schema.json) requires `seq` only, so the other two fields are the service's working choice, proposed; clearing raises nothing, as 05 section 7 names no event for it; (c) the search purpose `participant` and `kinds: ['user']` are the editor's working choice, since 05 does not say which purpose an assignment uses; (d) `assign` and the `assignee` of a thread's detail are optional on the port, a working shape like the other write methods; (e) the catch-up half of criterion 3 rests on two separate runs (the service's activity, and the pane with a fake port), not on one run from the assignment to the catch-up; (f) the combobox keys were run in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, and no screen reader was run. Whether resolving the thread completes the task is not specified; the protocol has only `open` and `resolved` (open questions). Round 7 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): `assignThread` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-threads.ts sets, reassigns and clears the assignee with `comment` in scope, and returns the `dep.task.assigned` event to send (none for a clear, since 05 section 7 names no event for it: open). Tested in packages/ui/test/comment-threads.test.ts. No criterion is ticked: the search for principals, the live change, the task view and the sending of the event are not built or run.

### UX-033 React to a comment

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | could | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-029, gap: 05 §6.2 has no reactions resource or event yet

As a commenter, I want to react to a comment, so that I can acknowledge it without writing a reply.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a comment, when the user reacts, then the reaction is shown with a count and the people who reacted (names via `resolveUsers`); reacting again removes it.
- [ ] Given another client reacts, when the change arrives, then it is shown live.
- [ ] Given a reaction, when it is made, then no commit is created.
- [ ] Given the backend does not announce reaction support, when comments render, then no reaction control is offered.
- [ ] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when a reaction control is used, then it has a name, a pressed state and a count.

**Notes:** 05 has no resource, feature name or event for reactions and does not say which reactions exist. Blocked until PRO defines them. Who may react is not specified; `comment` is assumed (inferred). Lane w3 (2026-11-17): re-checked; docs/spec has no reactions resource, event or feature name (a search finds the word only in 11 and 13), so nothing is built and no criterion is ticked. Status blocked.

### UX-034 Link to a comment

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | should | Commenter |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12 · **Requirements:** R8, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-030, gap: 05 §8.1 has no attribute or event for this yet

As a commenter, I want to link to a comment, so that I can point a colleague, or a notification, straight to it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a comment, when the user chooses to copy its link, then the editor hands the host the document and comment identifiers, and copies the address the host returns, because the host owns the address space.
- [ ] Given the host opens the editor for a comment identifier, when the document has loaded, then the comments pane opens, scrolls to the thread and focuses it.
- [x] Given the comment was deleted or the user cannot view the document, when the link is followed, then a clear message is shown and nothing else is revealed.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when a linked comment is focused, then its author, time and thread state are announced.

**Evidence (round 14, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-034-linked-comment.spec.ts (3 tests, 9 runs passed across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57270 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-034-linked-comment.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). `openLinkedComment` in packages/ui/src/App.svelte looks the comment up in the thread list the user may view. A comment that is deleted or not viewable gets only the message `comments.link.unavailable`: no list, no filter, no thread text (criterion 3). A resolved thread that the default filter hides is shown, focused, and announced in the shell's live region as author, time and state (criteria 2 to 4 in part; criterion 4 is checked by the live region text and focus, not by a screen reader run). Criterion 2's host path and criterion 1 remain open (see Notes).

**Notes:** Round 14 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): the shell's entry for a followed link is built: `openLinkedComment` (App.svelte) opens the comments pane, and the pane (packages/ui/src/panes/CommentsPane.svelte) shows, focuses and announces the thread, or shows the unavailable message. The thread reaches the pane through `linkedThread` in the shell context (packages/ui/src/context.ts). Decision, proposed in ADR 0327 part B (not stated in the spec, whose method is open): the shell entry takes a comment identifier and the element's host method is a later decision. Criterion 2 stays unticked: the element has no host method for a comment identifier, so "the host opens the editor" is not met. Criterion 1 stays unticked: the copy needs the link's address form and the method, both open questions in the spec. Round 4 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): `linkedCommentAnnouncement` in packages/ui/src/shell/linked-comment.ts gives the three parts criterion 4 names (author, creation time, state) and no other part of the thread, tested in packages/ui/test/ux-037-034-pane-rows.test.ts (Node.js, property over generated threads; decision 11, ADR 0233). Criterion 4 stays open: the focus, the spoken sentence and a screen reader run are not built. Round 7 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): `commentLinkRequest` and `resolveCommentLink` in packages/ui/src/shell/comment-threads.ts give the request shape (the two identifiers only) and the bare `unavailable` outcome for a deleted or hidden comment, tested in packages/ui/test/comment-threads.test.ts. No criterion is ticked: the clipboard write, the message, the pane opening and the focus are not built, and the method, event and address form remain open (see the spec's open questions). Lane w3 (2026-11-17): re-checked; still blocked. Criterion 1 needs the address form and the host method, and criterion 2 needs the host method for a comment identifier, and 05 section 8.1 still names neither. Nothing new is ticked. Status blocked.

### UX-035 Markup views

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12; 08 §14 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-004, FE-041, FE-042

As an author, I want to choose how suggestions are shown, so that I can read the text with or without the markup.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given suggestions, when the view `all` is chosen, then insertions, deletions and formatting changes are shown with their authors; `simple` shows the text with a marker where changes exist; `none` shows the text as if every suggestion were accepted; `original` shows the text as if every suggestion were rejected.
- [x] Given a view is chosen, when it changes, then the document does not change and no commit is sent.
- [x] Given block-level suggestions drawn as decorations, when the view changes, then they follow the same view.
- [x] Given a suggestion is shown, when assistive technology reads it, then it is exposed as inserted, deleted or reformatted with its author, and the distinction does not rely on colour.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/markup-views.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13, round 2, lane UX-027-039): `viewSegments` and `viewText` in packages/ui/src/shell/markup-views.ts give the four views over runs of text with a change kind and author. Properties over generated runs: `none` is the text without deletions, `original` the text without insertions, `all` every run with its change and author in order, `simple` the `none` text with one marker per changed run; a fixed example reads as each view is named. Criterion 1 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): it describes what the editor shows for each view, the definitions are inferred and unconfirmed, and `markup-views.ts` is imported by its test only (no view switch or rendered suggestion uses it).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17):** The views are built end to end. Engine: `markupView` and `setMarkupView` on the core (`packages/engine/src/core.ts`), the decoration rules in `src/suggestion-markup.ts`, drawn by `src/view/web.ts` with the rules in `packages/element/src/styles/element.css`. Shell: the commands `review.markup.all`, `.simple`, `.none` and `.original` (`packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts`, `App.svelte`), passed on by `packages/element/src/editor.ts`. Criteria 1 and 3: `packages/engine/src/view/ux-035-markup-view.universal.test.ts` (1 test; passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit on 2026-11-17 with `pnpm exec vitest run --project browser <file>`): computed styles of an insertion, a deletion and a deleted paragraph in `all` (lines, not colour), `simple` (a 3px bar on the insertion; the deleted run and the deleted block are out of the text with a bar left), `none` (no cue; deletions hidden) and `original` (insertion hidden, deletions drawn plain); `packages/engine/test/ux-035-markup-view.test.ts` (7 tests, Node.js) and `packages/engine/test/fe-046-markup-views.test.ts` (the classes of each view for runs and for a block mark). Criterion 2: the same Node.js tests (the engine state object and the revision are the same after four choices, so no transaction was applied), `packages/element/e2e/ux-035-markup-views.spec.ts` (the revision does not move) and `packages/ui/e2e/ux-035-markup-views.spec.ts` (choosing a view runs no content command). Criterion 4: the element spec types a suggestion in review mode in the real element: its `::before` carries "Inserted by <name>" out of sight, the accessibility tree of Chromium (`Accessibility.getFullAXTree`) holds that text, and the kind is a line (underline), not colour; the ui spec checks that the words use the host's directory name ("Inserted by Ada Lovelace"). The element spec (3 tests) passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (`DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57331 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts ux-035`); the ui spec (4 tests) passed in the same three browsers (`DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57332 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-035`).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 to 4 ticked on the evidence above. What the ticks do not cover: (a) owner decision 169 (`docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`) settles the four definitions as built (ADR 0233, decision 4): `all`, `simple`, `none` as if accepted, `original` as if rejected; the drawing and the commands are recorded in `docs/adr/0384-w3-UX-035-markup-views.md` (proposed), which replaces the default of ADR 0132 where `none` drew nothing; (b) a table row or table taken out by a view leaves no bar, and a formatting change in `original` is not drawn at its previous value; (c) the accessibility tree was read in Chromium only, and no screen reader was run, so criterion 4 stands on the words being in the tree, not on a listening test; (d) the commands are not on the toolbar, so a person finds them by command search or a host button (`exec`), and there is no visible markup-view control yet. Earlier notes: The spec names the four views and does not define them; the definitions here are inferred and need the designer's confirmation. Round 2 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 was ticked, then unticked in review, on the inferred definitions (ADR 0233, decision 4), and the designer's confirmation is still outstanding. Criterion 2 stays open: the pure view reads the runs and leaves them unchanged (checked), but "no commit is sent" and the document's unchanged state are the core's and are not checked here. Criterion 3 stays open: block-level decorations are not in the model. Criterion 4 stays open: the accessible names of the change kinds, and the check that they do not rely on colour, need the rendered markup and a browser run.

### UX-036 Review suggestions one by one or all at once

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Editor |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12; 04 (origins); 07 §5.2 (P4); 08 §14 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-035, PRM-014, FE-041, FE-042, FE-047

As an editor, I want review tools that step through suggestions and accept or reject them one by one or all at once, so that I decide what enters the text.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given suggestions in scopes where the user has `edit`, when they accept one, then the commit carries origin `suggestion.accept` and is recorded in history with it (08 §14); when they reject one, then `suggestion.reject`; next and previous move between suggestions.
- [ ] Given "accept all" or "reject all" over the current selection, region or author filter, when it runs, then it applies to every matching suggestion in scopes where the user has `edit`, leaves the others pending, and tells the user how many were left.
- [x] Given a user who has `suggest` but not `edit`, when suggestions are shown, then accept and reject are not offered for other people's suggestions, and not for their own.
- [x] Given another person resolves a suggestion first, when the change arrives, then the local view updates without an error.
- [x] Given a keyboard, when suggestions are reviewed, then every action is reachable without a pointer.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5):** Engine: packages/engine/test/ux-036-review.test.ts (Node.js, 9 tests, 2026-11-18, members joined to the mock service, which runs the authority's room). `suggestionItems()` lists the suggestions in document order with author, kind, words and the capabilities and `canResolve` of each (packages/engine/src/suggestion-list.ts, `core.ts`). `resolveSuggestions(action, target, author, suggestion)` now takes one suggestion by its mark identifier (`ResolveScope.suggestion`, `suggestion-resolve.ts` and `suggestion-resolve-block.ts`), and every accept or reject crosses the channel as one commit with origin `suggestion.accept` or `suggestion.reject` (new `RESOLVE_ORIGIN` in `meta.ts`, read in `dispatchTransaction`; the test reads `traffic.submitted`, which records each submitted commit's origin and step count): accept one, reject one, reject all, and an unknown identifier sending nothing. The service's log keeps the origin of a commit (packages/backend-mock/src/document.ts); the history pane's entry for it is HIS's. `stepSuggestion(1 | -1)` goes to the next or previous suggestion after the selection, wraps, and selects its words; `resolveAtSelection` resolves the suggestion the caret is in, or those in a selection (test). Shell: the commands `review.next`, `review.previous`, `review.accept`, `review.reject`, `review.acceptAll` and `review.rejectAll` (packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts, `App.svelte`, `core-api.ts`; the element passes them on in packages/element/src/editor.ts); packages/ui/e2e/ux-036-review.spec.ts (9 tests; Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 27 runs passed, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56449 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-036 --retries=0`, 2026-11-18, with the harness core): stepping says 'Insertion by Ada Lovelace: words s1' and wraps; accept and reject at the caret and over a selection say how many were handled; accept all over the document leaves the one in a part the user cannot edit and says '2 suggestions accepted. 1 suggestion left pending, because you cannot edit that part.'; with a selection it covers only the selection. In the real element: packages/element/e2e/ux-036-review-tools.spec.ts (1 test; Chromium, Firefox, WebKit and Edge, 4 passed, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57343 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-036-review-tools.spec.ts`): suggestions typed in review mode, `review.next` selects the later one, `review.reject` removes its words, `review.previous` finds the other and `review.accept` keeps its words and drops the mark. packages/engine/src/view/ux-036-reveal.universal.test.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 3 passed): the body scrolls a scrolling box to the suggestion that was stepped to, the document unchanged. Criterion 3: a user with `suggest` and not `edit` gets `canResolve` false and no `edit` in `caps` for every suggestion, her own included (engine test, 'a suggester may resolve none'), resolving does nothing for her, and the shell registers `review.accept`, `review.reject`, `review.acceptAll` and `review.rejectAll` only while some suggestion can be resolved (`mayResolveAny` in `App.svelte`): the spec's 'a user who suggests but cannot edit is offered neither' shows `offered: false` for the four and `offered: true` for next, and that they appear when `edit` is given. Criterion 4: the engine test 'another person resolves a suggestion first' (two cores on one room): the first core's list drops the suggestion on the commit's arrival, with its text as the other person left it; resolving or revealing it again does nothing, sends nothing and throws nothing; the shell spec 'the list follows without an error' shows the same on the list the commands read. Criterion 5: the spec's last test reaches the editing surface by Tab, opens command search with Alt+Q, types 'accept all', and runs the command with Enter, with no pointer; the other review commands are listed there under their names and synonyms; axe finds no violation with the list open.


**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/review-rules.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `reviewOffers` in packages/ui/src/shell/review-rules.ts offers accept and reject only where `edit` is held, and never with `suggest` alone (criterion 3, a property test over capabilities); `stepSuggestion` moves next and previous with wrap-around, checked by a property test (criterion 1, the step only); `ACCEPT_ORIGIN` and `REJECT_ORIGIN` name the commit origins of 08 §14. Criterion 3 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): no menu or pane shows suggestions yet, and nothing but the test imports `reviewOffers`; the criterion is about what is shown. Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): `bulkPlan` in packages/ui/src/shell/review-bulk-live.ts gives the ids that accept all or reject all applies (matching rows in scopes where the user has `edit`) and the count left pending (criterion 2, the rule; the message is not built), and `applySuggestionChange` removes a resolved row with no error for an unknown id (criterion 4, the rule). Both are tested in packages/ui/test/ux-036-037-review-bulk-live.test.ts (6 tests passed, Node.js, with properties). Neither criterion is ticked, and criterion 2's rule is also a module that only its test imports: the message text and the browser run for a resolve by another person are open.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 1, 3, 4 and 5 ticked. Criterion 2 stays unticked: "accept all" and "reject all" are built for the selection and the document (`review.acceptAll`, `review.rejectAll`, one commit, the skipped count said), and the engine takes a region by identifier and an author filter (FE-047), but the commands have no way to name a region or an author. The review pane (UX-037) now has an author filter whose 'Accept all by <author>' and 'Reject all by <author>' call the core with that author (one commit, counts announced; packages/ui/e2e/ux-037-review-pane.spec.ts), so the selection, the document and an author are covered; no surface names a region (the engine takes one by identifier), and the criterion is left open for that. Choices made here, not in the spec, proposed: (a) the six commands are off the toolbar and found by command search (Alt+Q) and the menus, and have no key of their own (the spec gives none); (b) with a caret, accept and reject take the suggestion the caret is in, with a selection those inside; accept all and reject all take the selection when there is one, else the document; (c) next and previous start from the selection's start and wrap at the ends, and each says its kind, author and words; (d) `review.accept` and the three others are not registered while every listed suggestion is out of the user's reach, and are offered when there are none, so a document without suggestions still shows them, disabled with 'There are no suggestions in this document.'; (e) "how many were left" counts the suggestions the user may not edit (as `bulkPlan` does) and not those outside the chosen range; (f) an author who holds `edit` may resolve her own suggestion, as `reviewOffers` offers (the spec does not say). What the ticks do not cover: the history pane's own display of the origin (HIS), a screen reader run, and a run with the real service over HTTP rather than the mock's authority room. Earlier: Round 1 (lane UX-027-039): criterion 3 is ticked on the offer rule, and it is the rule that the menu will call; the menu is not built. Criterion 1 stays open: the commits and their history entries are FE-047's and the core's, and the keys that call `stepSuggestion` are not bound. Criteria 2, 4 and 5 stay open (the bulk plan and its message, a resolve by another person in a browser, and the keyboard run). The accept and reject transforms for text, formatting and block suggestions, and the single atomic commit for "all", are FE-047's. The review pane and its filter by author are UX-037. The leave-pending behaviour of "accept all" is inferred by analogy with "replace all" (07 section 7). Whether a suggester may withdraw their own suggestion is not specified (07 section 17 leaves block-level P4 rules open). Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): "how many were left" is not defined in the spec. `bulkPlan` counts the matching rows the user may not edit, and not rows outside the match (a reading, open for the designer).

### UX-037 Review pane

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Editor |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §5, §12 · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-036

As an editor, I want a pane that lists the suggestions, so that I can review them by author and in order.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given suggestions, when the pane opens, then they are listed in document order with kind (insertion, deletion, formatting, block change), author, the server time of the commit that introduced them, and an excerpt.
- [x] Given a filter by author, when it is chosen, then only that author's suggestions are listed; assistant authors (UX-046) are included as authors.
- [x] Given a suggestion in the list, when it is chosen, then the document moves to it, and accept and reject are offered on the item (UX-036).
- [x] Given suggestions arrive or are resolved, when the change happens, then the list updates live.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when the list is used, then each item is exposed with kind, author and excerpt.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18, criteria 2 to 5):** The pane is new: packages/ui/src/panes/ReviewPane.svelte, a built-in pane (`review`, packages/ui/src/shell/panes.ts) opened by the new command `view.review`, reading the core's `suggestionItems()` (UX-036; packages/engine/src/suggestion-list.ts) through `CoreApi` and the rows rule `reviewPaneRows` (packages/ui/src/shell/review-pane-rows.ts, now used by a screen). packages/ui/e2e/ux-037-review-pane.spec.ts (9 tests; with the review specs and the neighbouring header and command search specs, 129 passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56453 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-036 ux-037 ux-035 ux-011 header --retries=0`, 2026-11-18, harness core). Criterion 2: the author select lists the authors present; choosing Grace Hopper lists her suggestion only, and choosing the author `assistant:writer` lists that one: an author is an identifier and an assistant is filtered like any other. The bulk buttons follow the filter ('Accept all by Grace Hopper' calls the core with that author, one commit). Criterion 3: 'Show' moves the core's selection to the suggestion's words (the engine scrolls the body to them: packages/engine/src/view/ux-036-reveal.universal.test.ts, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit), the row is marked `aria-current`, and 'Accept' and 'Reject' on the item resolve that one with the count announced; they are absent for a user without `edit` (`canResolve`, `reviewOffers`), who still has 'Show'. In the real element: packages/element/e2e/ux-037-review-pane.spec.ts (1 test; Chromium, Firefox, WebKit and Edge, 4 passed, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57349 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-037-review-pane.spec.ts`): suggestions typed in review mode are listed in document order with kind, author and words, 'Show' selects the words in the core, 'Reject' and 'Accept' on the items remove the words or the mark and the rows. Criterion 4: the pane re-reads the list when the core's `revision` moves. packages/engine/test/ux-036-review.test.ts ('another person resolves a suggestion first', two cores on one room, Node.js): the second core's `revision` moves and its list drops the suggestion when the other person's commit arrives; the shell spec adds a suggestion and removes one in the harness core and the rows follow without a reload; in the element the rows follow the member's own accept and reject. Criterion 5: each row is a focusable group named 'Insertion by Ada Lovelace: words s1' (kind, author, words; 'no text' for a formatting change), Tab goes from the row to Show, Accept and Reject, and Enter on them works; axe finds no violation. Criterion 1 stays unticked, see Notes.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/review-rules.test.ts (Node.js, 2026-11-13): `listSuggestions` in packages/ui/src/shell/review-rules.ts lists only the chosen author's suggestions, an assistant identifier being filtered like any other, in document order; a property test checks the count, the order and that the input is unchanged (criterion 2, the rule only). Criterion 2 was unticked in review (2026-11-13): the review pane and its author filter are not built, and nothing but the test imports `listSuggestions`. Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): packages/ui/test/ux-036-037-review-bulk-live.test.ts (6 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/ux-036-037-review-bulk-live.test.ts`): `applySuggestionChange` in packages/ui/src/shell/review-bulk-live.ts keeps the list in document order, with no duplicate ids, after any sequence of arrivals and resolves, matching a model (criterion 4, the list's update rule only). Criterion 4 was unticked in review (round 6, 2026-11-13): the criterion is about the pane's live list, no pane exists, and nothing but the test imports `applySuggestionChange`.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 2 to 5 ticked. Criterion 1 stays unticked for one part only: the server time of the commit that introduced a suggestion. The core has no record of it: a suggestion mark holds its identifier and author, and the commit that made it is known only to the service (decision 109 of docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md settles the time as the server's time at acceptance of the commit; decision 86 adds the suggestion-to-commit index, HIS-042, that would give it, and no endpoint of the protocol lists suggestions with their commits). The pane shows the time when `ReviewItem.committedAt` is given, and shows none otherwise (tested both ways); the core does not give it yet. Kind, author, excerpt and document order are shown and tested. What the ticks do not cover: (a) an assistant author is listed and filtered as an identifier; how it is named (UX-046) is not built, so it shows the neutral label 'Collaborator' next to its identifier-based filter value; (b) the live update in the real element was run for the member's own accept and reject and, at the core, for another person's commit, not as one browser run with two clients; (c) the pane is opened by the command `view.review` (command search, menus), not by a header button, since 13 section 3 lists the header's buttons as comments, catch-up and history only; (d) a screen reader was not run. Earlier: Round 4 (lane UX-027-039, 2026-11-13): `reviewPaneRows` in packages/ui/src/shell/review-pane-rows.ts gives the pane's rows (document order, the author filter, kind, author, commit time, excerpt or null when empty, and the four-part accessible name), tested in packages/ui/test/ux-037-034-pane-rows.test.ts (Node.js, 6 tests passed, with fast-check properties). No criterion ticked: the rows are not rendered, the positions and commit times come from the core, the list is not wired to a pane, and criterion 3's keys are not bound (decision 10, ADR 0233). Round 1 (lane UX-027-039): criterion 2 is ticked on the filter rule. Criterion 1 stays open: the core's positions and commit times, and the pane's rows (kind, author, excerpt) are not built. Criteria 3 to 5 need the pane and the list's live update. The date shown for a suggestion is the server time of its commit (08 section 14). Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 was ticked on the update rule `applySuggestionChange` and unticked in review (dead module); the pane's rows and their live rendering, and the browser run, are not built.

### UX-038 Require tracked changes for everyone

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | should | Owner |

**Spec:** 13 §5; 07 §5.2; 03 §4 (`settings.trackChanges`) · **Requirements:** R8 · **Depends on:** UX-015, UX-035, PRM-014, CDM-018

As an owner, I want every change in the document tracked until I say otherwise, so that nothing enters the text unreviewed.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a user with `manage`, when they turn "tracked changes for everyone" on, then every user including editors edits as suggestions, and the mode switcher shows editors in review mode with the reason.
- [ ] Given a user without `manage`, when the setting is shown, then the control is not offered, and a direct attempt to change it is rejected by the server.
- [ ] Given the owner turns it off, when connected users receive the change, then editors can edit directly again without a reload.
- [ ] Given an editor made a direct change before the setting reached their client, when the server rejects it, then it is handled as any rejected change (UX-017).
- [ ] Given the setting is on, when anyone opens the review pane, then the setting and who may switch it off are visible.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/ux-038-tracked-for-everyone.test.ts (round 6, lane ui-2, 2026-11-13; 6 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/ux-038-tracked-for-everyone.test.ts`): `trackedModeFor` in packages/ui/src/shell/tracked-for-everyone.ts gives `review` with the reason `trackedForEveryone` for a chosen `edit` while the setting is on, and `edit` again as soon as it is off, with no state to reload (criterion 3, the rule only; a property over toggles). Criterion 3 was unticked in review (round 6, 2026-11-13): the criterion is about connected clients receiving the change, and nothing but the test imports `trackedModeFor` or `trackedControlOffered`. `trackedControlOffered` offers the setting only with `manage` (the rule behind criterion 2's first half). Not claimed: the mode switcher's display (criterion 1), the server's refusal (criterion 2, second half), the rejected change (criterion 4) and the review pane's line (criterion 5).

**Evidence (round 7, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-038-tracked-for-everyone.spec.ts (3 tests, 9 runs passed across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-038-tracked-for-everyone.spec.ts` with `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57270`): with the setting on, the mode button reads "Mode: Reviewing", the reason is shown, and the axe check is clean; with the setting off and on again the mode changes in the same page (a window marker survives). Criterion 1 stays unticked (review, round 7): the browser run uses the fake core's host setter, no user with `manage` turns the setting on through any control, and the production `ShellCore` supplies no `trackedForEveryone`, so the display is proven only against the fake. The server side is covered by packages/authority/test/suggestions.test.ts (PRM-015, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/authority/test/suggestions.test.ts`): an editor's direct change is refused with `permission_denied` and the same change as a suggestion is accepted, and a `manage` holder switches the setting off. Engine side: `document-setting` in `SuggestionModeInfo` (packages/engine/src/api.ts).

**Notes:** Round 15 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): re-checked for the browser run. `ShellCore` in packages/element/src/editor.ts supplies no `mode`, `modes` or `trackedForEveryone`, so the real switcher has no mode to show. The engine already reads `settings.trackChanges` into `suggestion` (`reason: 'document-setting'`, packages/engine/src/suggestion-mode.ts), which is the source for the shell view. Not done here: it needs a shell change in the element and a document fixture with `trackChanges: everyone`, and the turning-on control stays unbuilt. Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 1 was unticked because no one turns the setting on in any test (there is no settings control) and the switcher's reason is shown only against the fake core's host setter, while the production element's `ShellCore` (packages/element/src/editor.ts) supplies neither `trackedForEveryone` nor `mode`, so the real shell never shows editors in review mode with the reason; the authority half (PRM-015) does hold. Round 7 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): `CoreApi.trackedForEveryone` (packages/ui/src/core-api.ts) is the view the switcher reads; the real core does not yet supply it, so the browser run uses the fake core's host setter (`setTrackedForEveryone`). Criterion 2 stays open: no settings control is built, so the first half (the control not offered to a user without `manage`) has no UI; the server half is refused for `defs:settings` in PRM-015 (a user without `structure` is refused; the spec's gap about `manage` versus `structure` remains). Criterion 3 stays open: the in-page change is proven, but delivery to connected clients over the sync channel is not tested (no real channel sets the view). Criterion 4 stays open: the client's handling of a server rejection (UX-017) is not run against a rejected direct change. Criterion 5 stays open: no review pane exists (UX-037). The reason's wording is this lane's (en-GB key `reason.mode.trackedForEveryone`), for the designer to confirm.

**Notes:** `settings` lives in `defs`, whose changes need `structure`, while 07 section 5.2 says `manage` switches it off; no rule P1 to P9 binds that distinction (gap, open questions). Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): the reading that "every user including editors" covers every user, the owner included, is open; `trackedModeFor` applies it to every user, and the spec's default is not changed. Criterion 2's server half and criterion 5 stay open. Criterion 3 was ticked on the rule and unticked in review (dead module, no client receives the setting). Lane w3 (2026-11-17): re-checked; still blocked. No settings control exists to turn the setting on (criteria 1 and 2 first half), the production shell supplies no `trackedForEveryone`, and there is no review pane (UX-037, criterion 5). The `manage` versus `structure` gap in 07 section 5.2 is still open. Nothing new is ticked. Status blocked.

### UX-039 Share button

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §5, §11.1; 05 §8.1 (`de-share-request`); PRODUCT (operating context) · **Requirements:** R6, R15 · **Depends on:** UX-014

As an owner, I want a share button that opens my host application's own sharing dialog, so that people are added in the place that knows them.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the share button, when it is activated, then the editor dispatches `de-share-request` (a composed, bubbling `CustomEvent` carrying identifiers and no content) and shows no sharing dialog of its own.
- [x] Given the host handles the event with its own dialog, when the dialog opens, then the editor's state and focus are preserved, and focus returns to the share button when the host closes it.
- [x] Given region grants and workflow participants, when the button is activated, then it does not open them; they are managed in the editor's permissions pane.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when the button is used, then it has an accessible name and is operable.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/share-and-painter.test.ts (round 5, packet U, 2026-11-13): the share command's run calls only the host's `requestShare` and opens no pane (`openPane` and `toggleOutline` never called); the command is enabled for a viewer, since it changes no content. Run with the fake core, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui`. Not claimed: the `de-share-request` event and the host dialog (the element's, criteria 1 and 2), and the button's accessible name and keyboard use (criterion 4, the component's).

**Evidence (round 9, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/element/e2e/ux-039-share.spec.ts (3 tests, run in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 9 passed in all, with `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56277 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-039-share.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). Criterion 1: a click on the share button raises one `de-share-request`, composed and bubbling, whose detail is `{ docId }` only, and the element holds no `dialog` or `[role="dialog"]`. Criterion 2: a host dialog opened with `showModal` leaves the editor's text unchanged; its Done button calls the element's `focus()`, and the share button is then the focused control in the element's shadow root. Criterion 4: the button is found by role and the name `Share`, and Enter raises the event. WebKit does not focus a button on a mouse click, so the criterion 2 test focuses the button before it activates it.

**Notes:** Round 9 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2 and 4 ticked on the element's browser test (see Evidence). The element did not give focus back before: `DocEditorElement.focus()` now calls the shell's `restoreFocus` first (packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts; `restoreShareFocus` in packages/element/src/editor.ts). Decision, not stated in the spec: focus returns when the host calls `focus()` on the element after its dialog closes; the element cannot see the dialog close, so a host that never calls `focus()` gets no return. A share request keeps its return target until that call, so a later `focus()` for another reason moves focus to the share button once.
