Round 5 (packet U, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 ticked on the unit test above. The `de-share-request` event is the element's; the shell's share command calls the host's `onShareRequest` callback and the event is not checked here. Phase inferred (1b). The event's `detail` is not defined in 05 and nothing says who sees the button or how a host without a handler hides it (open questions).

### UX-040 Output restrictions in the editor

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Viewer |

**Spec:** 13 §5, §12; 05 §3.1 (`restrict`), §5.5 (`restrictions`), §6.2; 07 §12 · **Requirements:** R10, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-004, PRM-056, PRM-058

As a person whose access carries restrictions, I want the editor to honour them and tell me, so that I understand why export, copy or print are not available.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `no-export` in the access object, when commands render, then export commands are disabled with a reason, `requestExport` refuses, and a direct export request to the control plane is refused by the service.
- [x] Given `no-copy`, when the user copies, cuts or drags content out of the editing surface, then the action is prevented and the user is told why.
- [x] Given `no-print`, when the user calls `print()` or the print command, then it is refused with a reason.
**Evidence (round 16, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-14; criterion 3 unticked in review, round 7):** the `print()` half only; the criterion also names the print command, which is not built, so it stays open. Reviewer also found that the new static import of `print.ts` in doc-editor.ts makes packages/element/test/fe-119 critical-path test (`critical-path.test.ts`) fail; the import must become dynamic. Fixed (V2 group 3, round 7): `print()` now imports `print.ts` and `print-frame.ts` dynamically; `critical-path.test.ts` passes again, and the demo build lists both as lazy chunks. packages/element/e2e/ux-040-no-print.spec.ts (1 test, 3 runs passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57270 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-040-no-print.spec.ts --project=<browser>`): under `no-print` (set on the core, then awaited on the element's `de-permission-change` event) `DocEditor.print()` in packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts resolves `{ status: 'refused', reason: 'no-print' }`, and no request to the exports port is sent. The refusal is `printTypesetPdf` in packages/element/src/print.ts, tested in Node.js by packages/element/test/fe-055-print-access.test.ts. The print command half is not built: the registry has no print command (see Notes).
- [x] Given a restriction is added or removed in `perm.changed`, when it arrives, then the commands update at once, without a reload.
- [x] Given the integration guide and the help text, when they describe restrictions, then they state that restrictions deter casual copying and are not a security boundary.
- [x] Given a restriction, when the user uses other features, then comments, navigation and viewing are unaffected.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18):** criterion 5 ticked. The integration guide (apps/reference-host/docs/integration-guide.md, "Output restrictions (UX-040)") now says the editor honours the three restrictions and the service refuses a direct export, and that restrictions deter casual copying and are not a security boundary; its stale "not wired yet" text is removed. The help texts that name a restriction (`reason.query.no-export`, `reason.query.no-print`, `notice.refused.noCopy` in packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts) carry the same sentence. Tests, Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <files>`, 5 files, 19 tests passed): packages/ui/test/ux-040-restriction-help.test.ts, apps/reference-host/test/integration-guide.restrictions.test.ts, with integration-guide.limits.test.ts, ux-040-print-command.test.ts and ux-040-no-copy-notice.test.ts still passing. The editor has no separate help pane; the disabled-command reasons and the refusal notice are its help text for restrictions. The export dialog hint `export.filter.noExport` is unchanged (an e2e test matches it exactly).

**Evidence (round 15, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-restriction-live.spec.ts (2 tests, 6 runs passed across Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57270 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-restriction-live.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). Criterion 4: the export command is enabled with the restriction absent, `setPermissions` adds `no-export` at a new revision, and the command is disabled with `reason.query.no-export` at once, with no reload; removal is covered by ux-040-export-command-state.spec.ts. Criterion 6: under `no-export` a comment command (`review.comment`, with a block anchor) and `view.zoomIn` stay enabled, and the document surface is visible. Criterion 4 is ticked for the output command that exists (`document.export`); there is no print command yet (criterion 3), so no print command is covered.

**Evidence:** No criterion ticked (round 5, lane ui-2, 2026-11-13). packages/ui/src/commands/output-restrictions.ts (`outputDecision`, `commandOutputAction`, `commandDecision`); packages/ui/test/output-restrictions.test.ts (5 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/output-restrictions.test.ts`, 2026-11-13): print is refused with the reason `no-print` only under that restriction (the rule behind criterion 3); each output action is refused only under its own restriction, with that reason (property); no command that is not an output action is refused under any restriction list (the rule behind criterion 6). Round 1 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13), service half of criterion 1: `services/document-service/src/control-plane/ux-040-no-export.test.ts` (2 tests passed, memory and PostgreSQL in Podman; `pnpm vitest run --project node services/document-service/src/control-plane/ux-040-no-export.test.ts`): a direct `POST /v1/documents/{id}/exports` with `restrict: ["no-export"]` is refused (403), and the same request without it is accepted (202).

**Evidence (round 9, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/element/test/ux-040-no-export.test.ts (4 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/element/test/ux-040-no-export.test.ts`; packages/element/test/export.test.ts and print.test.ts still pass). Criterion 1, client half: `startExport` in packages/element/src/export.ts refuses with `PortError` code `forbidden` and detail `{ reason: 'no-export' }` before any request is sent, and `requestExport` in packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts passes the session's access in. Not claimed: the disabled commands with their reason (criterion 1, first half), which the shell does not render.

**Evidence (round 10, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-export-restrictions.spec.ts (2 tests, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56275 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-export-restrictions.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). Under `no-export` the PDF target of the export dialog is disabled with the reason "Your access does not allow export.", Export is disabled, and no export request is sent; the control without the restriction has both enabled. The dialog change is in packages/ui/src/components/ExportDialog.svelte (`noExport`, the start guard). The existing 'client export filters (UX-055)' tests in packages/ui/e2e/extensions.spec.ts passed in Chromium (the save test failed once in a combined run with a trace-file error and passed on re-run; not changed).

**Evidence (round 12, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1 ticked. The command half: `document.export` names the shell query `shell.exportAllowed` (packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts), which App.svelte sets from `commandDecision` (packages/ui/src/commands/output-restrictions.ts), so under `no-export` the registry gives `enabled: false` with the reason key `reason.query.no-export` (packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts). Browser run, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-export-command-state.spec.ts (2 tests) and packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-export-restrictions.spec.ts (the dialog test now lets the restriction arrive while the dialog is open, because the refused command no longer opens it), `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56275 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts <file>`. Final run: 12 tests passed; the first WebKit attempt at the dialog test failed the harness policy check and passed on retry. Unit: packages/ui/test/output-restrictions.test.ts and the tests of the changed command and strings (7 files, 46 tests, Node.js) pass. The `requestExport` refusal is round 9's and the service refusal is round 1's (PostgreSQL and the memory store in Podman).

**Evidence (round 13, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** criterion 2 ticked. packages/element/e2e/ux-040-no-copy.spec.ts (3 tests, reviewer-corrected from 4; the real engine and shell in the element; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=56277 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-040-no-copy.spec.ts --project=<browser>`): under `no-copy` (set on the engine core as a new access revision) Ctrl+C writes no clipboard text and shows the notice "Nothing was copied: your access does not allow copying from this document."; Ctrl+X writes nothing and removes nothing; a drag of the selected text starts no drag, changes no text and shows the same notice; without the restriction a copy writes the selected text and shows no notice. Passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit (one Chromium control run failed once and passed on re-run; not explained). The drag check depends on the surface's first paragraph being under the pointer.

**Evidence (round 17, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-14):** criterion 3 ticked, now that the print command exists. `document.print` in packages/ui/src/commands/builtin.ts is enabled by `shell.printAllowed`, which packages/ui/src/App.svelte sets from `commandDecision` (packages/ui/src/commands/output-restrictions.ts); under `no-print` it is disabled with `reason.query.no-print` (packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts), and its run calls the host's `onPrintRequest`, which packages/element/src/doc-editor.ts wires to `DocEditor.print()`. Browser run, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit: packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-print-command.spec.ts (2 tests, 6 runs passed; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57270 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-040-print-command.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). Node.js: packages/ui/test/ux-040-print-command.test.ts (6 tests passed; `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/ux-040-print-command.test.ts`), with the related registry and command tests (11 files, 97 tests passed). The `print()` refusal stays evidenced by packages/element/e2e/ux-040-no-print.spec.ts (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, round 16) and packages/element/test/fe-055-print-access.test.ts (re-run: 23 Node tests in five files passed).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criterion 5 ticked; all six criteria are ticked. Criterion 5 was read as the guide plus the texts that name a restriction, because no help pane exists. Fix (V2 group 3, round 8, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-14): two review findings. (1) The print frame was appended to the host page's `document.body`, which FE-020 forbids (packages/ui/test/fe-020-overlay-boundary.test.ts failed on packages/element/src/print-frame.ts). Choice: the frame is now mounted in the element's own shadow root (`printPdfAtUrl(url, container)`; `DocEditor.print()` passes its shadow root), so the FE-020 test needs no allowance. A browser test, packages/element/src/v-ux-031-045-print-frame.universal.test.ts (1 test, passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `pnpm vitest run --project browser <file>`), shows the frame lands in the shadow root and nothing is added to the host body; it removes the frame before load, so it does not show that a PDF prints. Not checked: when the signed PDF URL is on another origin, the frame's `contentWindow.print()` and its `afterprint` listener are cross-origin calls that browsers refuse, so the frame printer may only work for a same-origin URL; open until the viewer's own print path (02 §9.8) is built. (2) The parity profile was regenerated with `pnpm --filter @docedit/ui generate` and now lists `document.print`. Decision (13 and 02 are silent on a print command id): `document.print` is a built-in command under the capability `export-pdf`, because 02 §9.8 prints the typeset PDF, so printing has the same capability as PDF export. Round 17 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-14): the print command is built, so the round 16 open item on `document.print`, `shell.printAllowed` and the disabled-reason wiring is closed by the round 17 Evidence above. The element's public `queryCommand` and `exec` take engine commands only, so the shell command is tested through the shell harness (`print: true` option in packages/ui/e2e/harness/main.ts), not through the element. Criterion 5 stays open. Round 15 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criteria 4 and 6 ticked on the live browser run above. Round 16 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-14): criterion 3 was ticked for `print()` and unticked in review (round 7), because the print command half is not built (see its Evidence). `DocEditor.print()` was added, with the default frame printer in packages/element/src/print-frame.ts (the spec default: print the typeset PDF through the viewer; the viewer's own print path is not built, so the frame printer stands in for it). Open for the owner: the registry still has no `document.print` command, so the menu, keyboard and `shell.printAllowed` wiring of the command half is not built; the `no-print` reason is not yet shown beside a disabled command. Criterion 5 (the integration guide and help text) stays open: 13 section 3 states the "not a security boundary" wording, but no integration guide or help text that describes restrictions was found to check. Round 13 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 ticked on the browser run above. The demo harness in packages/ui cannot set `no-copy`, so the shell-level test is not used; the element run uses the engine core's `setAccess`, the same call the session makes on `perm.changed`. The restriction is not yet sent by the backend mock or the authority (no restriction field there), so only a test-side change of access proves the path. Round 12 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 stays open: the removal of `no-export` is tested for the export command, but the addition while the command is idle, and the other commands, are not. Criterion 3 stays open: there is no print command in the registry (`document.print` appears only in output-restrictions.ts), so the `no-print` refusal has no call site. The `policy violations` failure on the first WebKit attempt is not explained yet; it did not recur on retry. Round 11 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): the telling half of criterion 2 is built. A copy refused by `no-copy` (core `copySelection`, FE-055) now has its own notice, `notice.refused.noCopy` ("Nothing was copied: ..."), not the general "That change was not made." (packages/ui/src/shell/notices.ts, packages/ui/src/i18n/en-GB.ts); tested in packages/ui/test/ux-040-no-copy-notice.test.ts (3 tests, Node.js) and packages/ui/test/notices.test.ts (6 tests still pass). Criterion 2 stays unticked: the prevention is FE-055's (engine view handlers, not re-run here), and no browser run shows the notice on screen, because the demo cannot set a `no-copy` access. Round 10 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): the export dialog now disables the deployment targets and Export under `no-export`, as the filters already were. Criterion 1 stays unticked: the menu's `document.export` command is still offered enabled, because the command registry does not read `commandDecision` (`packages/ui/src/commands/output-restrictions.ts`), and the wiring there is the shell's. The `requestExport` refusal and the service refusal are the earlier rounds' evidence. Round 9 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): the `requestExport` refusal for `no-export` is built and tested (see Evidence); criterion 1 stays unticked because the disabled export commands are not rendered. The interplay with the PDF preview, the read view (first the typeset PDF) and printing is not specified: the preview is produced by an export job, which `no-export` refuses, yet `no-print` exists as a separate restriction (open questions). Round 5 (lane ui-2): the rule is built, but nothing calls it yet. The `print()` and export call sites, the copy, cut and drag guards on the editing surface, and the commands' re-render on `perm.changed` are not wired, so criteria 1 to 4 and 6 describe the shell and stay open. The export refusal in `extensions/filters.ts` (UX-055) is a separate guard and is not changed here. Round 1 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open for its client half (`requestExport` and the disabled commands are not built). Criteria 2 to 4 and 6 stay open: no browser run, and the copy, cut, drag and `print()` guards are not wired. A read of an export job by a `no-export` person is refused (403) by the service; no spec rule for that case was checked, and no test asserts it.

## Assistance ports

### UX-041 Show spelling and grammar findings from the proofing port

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §6.1, §12; 02 §15; 05 §8.3 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-004, PRO-091, PRO-092, FE-066

As an author, I want spelling and grammar findings in my text, so that I can correct mistakes, with checking supplied by my host or backend.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a proofing port, when the document opens or text changes, then text is sent to the port with the language from the `lang` mark (or the document default), and findings return as decorations with suggestions; the document is never changed by a finding alone.
- [x] Given a scope the user cannot view, when proofing runs, then none of its text is sent to the port.
- [x] Given findings return after the text changed, when they arrive, then stale ones are discarded or mapped so that none is shown on the wrong text.
- [x] Given the port is slow or unavailable, when the author types, then typing is unaffected, no error blocks the interface, and the proofing state shows quietly.
- [x] Given no proofing port, when the interface renders, then no proofing control is shown.
- [x] Given a finding, when the author uses a keyboard or a screen reader, then findings can be moved between and each is exposed with its text, kind and suggestions, not only by decoration.
- [x] Given findings are decorations, when the document is saved, then they appear in no commit, version or history.

**Evidence:** packages/ui/test/ux-041-findings.test.ts (round 6, lane ui-2, 2026-11-13; 4 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/ux-041-findings.test.ts`): `typed` clears the findings and moves to a new revision, `arrive` drops a result for an earlier revision and keeps one for the current revision, and a fast-check property over operation sequences shows that a finding is only ever held for the revision the state is at (criterion 3, the state rule in packages/ui/src/proofing/ux-041-findings.ts). Criterion 3 was unticked in review (round 6, 2026-11-13): the criterion is about findings shown on text, there is no port or decoration, and nothing but the test imports the module. Not claimed: criteria 1, 2, 4 to 7, which need the port, the decorations, the typing path and the browser run.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1 to 7):** The core now takes the proofing port (packages/engine/src/proofing.ts, `ProofingController`; `DocumentCore.attachProofing`, `proofing` view, `proofingFindings`, `proofingStep`; the element passes `backend.proofing` through `AttachAt.proofing`, packages/element/src/session.ts). Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/ux-041-proofing.test.ts packages/element/test/ux-041-proofing-port.test.ts packages/ui/test/ux-041-present.test.ts`, 2026-11-17, all pass; a stepped clock and a mock port that records its requests). Criterion 1: the blocks go out with the document default language when the document opens, only changed blocks go out again after the pause, a `lang` mark sets the language sent, a finding becomes a decoration over its text with its suggestions, and `getContent()` and the sync queue are unchanged. Criterion 2: with the region's scope listed without `view`, no request ever holds its block or text, and the omitted count is 1; withdrawing the access later stops sending it. Criterion 3: a result computed for a block's old text is not shown, the other blocks' findings of the same result are; a finding that does not fit its block is dropped. Criterion 4: a port that never answers leaves the state at `checking` and typing goes on; a port that rejects or throws gives `unavailable`, with `problem` and `refusal` untouched, and the next change tries again; a newer check cancels the one in flight. Criterion 5: no port gives `available: false` (Node.js; element test: a session whose backend has no port leaves proofing off), and packages/ui/e2e/ux-041-042-proofing.spec.ts shows no status item and no proofing command, and takes both away when the port leaves (Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56742 DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-041-042-proofing.spec.ts`, Windows 11, 27 tests passed). Criterion 6: `proofingStep` moves the selection between findings and wraps, and the shell binds Alt+F7 and Alt+Shift+F7 (`proofing.next`, `proofing.previous`; shortcut table row) and announces kind, text, message and suggestions as one sentence (`findingSentence`, packages/ui/src/proofing/present.ts; browser run in the same spec, axe finds no violation with the item shown). Criterion 7: no step is queued and the confirmed content is unchanged (Node.js), and against the mock service (the real authority room) the head and the stored content stay where they were while findings come, are stepped through and ignored. Browser run of the decorations: packages/engine/src/view/proofing-highlight.universal.test.ts (`DOCEDIT_BROWSERS=chromium,firefox,webkit pnpm exec vitest run --project browser packages/engine/src/view/proofing-highlight.universal.test.ts`, 3 passed): the finding is drawn as an element of the editing surface with `data-de-finding`, the current one gets its own class, and the element is gone when the text under it changes. The mock port is a test double: no real proofing service was run (none ships; UX-043 is a spike).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): all seven criteria ticked on the evidence above. Defaults chosen here, none of them from the spec (13 §6.1 and the Notes below left them open), all proposed and changeable: the unit of checking is the block (a paragraph, heading or list item with an identifier), and a block with several languages is sent in the language that covers most of its text; the pause before a check is 400 ms (`DEFAULT_DEBOUNCE_MS`, provisional, not measured); a block with no `lang` mark and no document default is sent as `und`; only the body is proofed (headers, footers, footnotes and the definitions are not); text under a suggestion's deletion mark is sent as it is; findings that the text moved under are discarded, not mapped, and only for the changed block. The state module packages/ui/src/proofing/ux-041-findings.ts (round 6) is superseded by the per-block rule in the engine and is still called by nothing but its own test. The decorations carry `data-de-finding` and a class, not ARIA attributes: what a screen reader hears comes from the announced sentence, not from the span. The proofing code is not yet a separate chunk (13 section 9). Earlier notes: The proofing code loads on demand (13 section 9). Whether checking is per block, per paragraph or per change, and its debounce, are not specified. Round 2 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): building block only, no criterion ticked. packages/ui/src/proofing/request.ts builds the request (the `lang` mark, else the document default, else none; text only from viewable scopes, the rest counted), states the rule that the control needs a port, and tells a current finding from a stale one by revision. packages/ui/test/proofing-request.test.ts (4 tests passed, Node.js, with a fast-check property for the scope rule). Nothing calls these functions: the port, the decorations, the document's opening and text-change triggers, the keyboard and the screen reader are not built, and the proofing port is not wired into the core. So criteria 1 to 7 stay open. Round 6 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 was ticked on the state rule in packages/ui/src/proofing/ux-041-findings.ts and unticked in review (dead module). Discarding was chosen over mapping (the mapping needs the core's position map). The rule is not yet wired to the port or to the decorations.

### UX-042 Act on a finding and manage custom dictionaries

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §6.1 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-041, UX-008

As an author, I want to apply, ignore or remember a suggestion, so that I resolve findings in one step.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a finding, when the author opens the context menu or the keyboard command, then they can apply a suggestion, ignore it once, ignore it everywhere, or add the word to a custom dictionary.
- [x] Given applying a suggestion, when it is applied, then it is an ordinary edit through the same guards, and in suggestion mode it becomes a suggestion; in a scope the user may not change it is not offered.
- [ ] Given the port supports custom dictionaries, when the author adds, lists or removes entries, then the port stores and honours them in later checks; given it does not, then dictionary controls are not offered.
- [x] Given the language of a range changes, when the status bar or a command sets it, then findings for that range are recomputed in the new language.

**Evidence:** No criterion ticked (round 5, lane ui-2, 2026-11-13). packages/ui/src/proofing/finding-actions.ts (`applyMode`, `offeredFindingActions`); packages/ui/test/finding-actions.test.ts (9 tests passed, Node.js, 2026-11-13, after the round 6 merge): the four actions are offered to an editor with a suggestion and a dictionary port (the rule behind criterion 1); an apply becomes a suggestion in `review` mode and for a user with only `suggest`, is an edit only for an editor in `edit` mode, and is not offered where the user has neither `edit` nor `suggest`, in `view` or `fill` mode, or without a suggestion (the mode rule behind criterion 2, properties); no dictionary action is offered without a dictionary port (the offer half of criterion 3, property); nothing is offered without `view`.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criteria 1, 2 and 4; criterion 3 in part):** Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/ux-042-finding-actions.test.ts`, 11 tests passed, with the mock port of that file and the mock service for the suggestion case). Criterion 1: `applyFinding`, `ignoreFinding` (once, and everywhere in the language) and `addFindingToDictionary` act on a finding; the context menu (packages/ui/src/components/ContextMenu.svelte) lists one item per suggestion, then ignore once, ignore all and add to dictionary, opened by the pointer or by Shift+F10 or the menu key, and the items run from the keyboard (packages/ui/e2e/ux-041-042-proofing.spec.ts, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, 27 tests passed with the UX-041 ones; the fake core records the calls). Criterion 2: `applyFinding` is one ordinary transaction through `dispatchTransaction`: in a region the user may not change it returns false, records the refusal and leaves the document alone; in `view` mode it is refused; and for a member who may only suggest, joined to the mock service (the real authority room), the old word keeps a `del` mark, the new one carries an `ins` mark, and the commit is accepted with nothing set aside. The menu offers "apply" only from `offeredFindingActions` (round 5) over the capabilities at the caret and the mode, so it is not offered with `view` and `comment` only (e2e). Criterion 4: setting a language with the `lang` command (the status bar's language control and the command both run it) makes the changed block go out again in the new language, and the old findings are replaced by those of the new one. Criterion 3 in part: the controller adds a word to the port's dictionary and checks again, lists and removes words, and a port without all three members is treated as having no dictionary, so the menu offers no dictionary action (e2e) and the controller refuses it (Node.js).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criteria 1, 2 and 4 ticked; criterion 3 stays unticked. The port in the protocol (`ProofingPort`, PRO-092) has `check` and `addWord` only, and its contract list (packages/protocol/src/contract/optional.ts) must name every member, so adding `listWords` and `removeWord` there would need contract cases in PRO's lane. They are proposed here as optional members of the engine's `ProofingPort` (packages/engine/src/proofing.ts) and tested with a mock that stores them; the protocol is unchanged. What is missing for the criterion: the two members in the protocol and its contract, and a control with which the author lists and removes entries (only "add to dictionary" is in the interface). "Ignore everywhere" lasts for the controller's session (not saved with the document or the user): where it is kept is open, like the dictionary's place. "Ignore once" hides that word at that place in that block, and stays hidden after other text of the block changes while the word and its offsets are the same. Where custom dictionaries live (user, tenant, document) and who may share them are not specified. Round 5 (lane ui-2): no criterion ticked. The menu and the keyboard command are not built. Criterion 2 also needs the edit to pass through the core's guards, and criterion 3 needs the port to store and honour entries; criterion 4 needs the language recomputation, and none of these is built. The suggest-only rule follows P4 in 07 §12 as the spec default; it is not an owner decision. Round 6 (fixer, 2026-11-13): a duplicate written in round 4 (packages/ui/src/proofing/ux-042-actions.ts and its test) was merged into finding-actions.ts and deleted. The context now takes the `EditorMode` in effect (`review` is suggestion mode, as in `shell/suggest-mode.ts`) instead of a suggestion-mode flag. From the duplicate it keeps two rules, read from the spec rather than stated by it: nothing is offered without `view` (13 §6.1 sends the port only text the user can view), and apply is not offered in `view` or `fill` mode. Nothing calls the module yet.

### UX-043 Spike: dictionaries and licences for a built-in fallback spell checker

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spike | 2 | S | could | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §13; 13 §2, §6.1; 02 §15 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want to know whether a built-in fallback spell checker is worth shipping, so that I can decide it on evidence. Time box: 3 days.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the spec lists "dictionaries and their licences for a built-in fallback spell checker" as open, when the spike ends, then a written recommendation states whether to ship a fallback at all.
- [x] Given candidate dictionaries for the first locales, when they are listed, then each has its licence checked against the project licence (Apache-2.0 recommended) and its size.
- [x] Given the 300 KB initial budget, when the cost is estimated, then the recommendation shows how the fallback would load on demand.
- [x] Given no proofing port, when the recommendation is written, then it states what the web view's context menu should do for misspellings (open questions).

**Evidence:** Criterion 2 (lane V-UX-031-045, round 2, 2026-11-13): `docs/adr/0327-lane-V-UX-031-045.md`, Part A, the npm registry metadata and `npm pack` tarball sizes for en-GB, de-DE and fr-FR (declared licences `(MIT AND BSD)`, `(GPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0)` and `MPL-2.0`); no test, a measurement. The locale list is this lane's proposal, the owner sets the first locales.

**Evidence (round 4, lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** criterion 3, `docs/adr/0327-lane-V-UX-031-045.md` Part B: `npm pack dictionary-en-gb@3.0.0` in a scratch folder outside the repository, then the gzip size of `index.dic` and `index.aff` (PowerShell 7 `GZipStream`, Optimal): 194,899 bytes against the 300 KB initial budget, engine excluded, and the on-demand load design. No test, a measurement.

**Evidence:** `docs/adr/0234-lane-UX-040-052.md`, Part C (proposed, lane UX-040-052, round 2, 2026-11-13). C1 is the recommendation: no built-in fallback in phase 2, spelling stays port-only (criterion 1). C4 is the proposal for the context menu without a port: the browser's spellcheck stays on and its suggestions show on a marked word (criterion 4, a proposal awaiting the owner's decision). Criteria 2 and 3 are not claimed (see C2 and C3). No test: a spike, with no code.

**Notes:** Round 2 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 2 ticked on the licence and size measurements for en-GB, de-DE and fr-FR in ADR 0327 (lane V-UX-031-045) Part A. The de-DE licence (GPL-2.0 OR GPL-3.0) is a possible blocker for Apache-2.0 distribution, for the owner or counsel to decide. Criterion 3 stays open: no built bundle, so no cost against the 300 KB budget (ADR 0327 Part B). Round 3 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): one candidate measured for en-GB, the locale the interface strings use (proposed, not set by the spec): `dictionary-en-gb` 3.0.0, licence `(MIT AND BSD)`, tarball 203,045 bytes, unpacked 575,855 bytes; method and reading in ADR 0327 Part B. Criterion 2 stays open: the first locales are the owner's to set, so the list is not final. Criterion 3 stays open: no built bundle, so no cost is estimated. Round 4 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 ticked on a dictionary-only gzip estimate with the on-demand design (ADR 0327 Part B); the engine and a built chunk are still not measured, and the owner may replace the estimate later. Round 2 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 4 ticked on the proposal in ADR 0234 Part C, which the owner has not accepted. Criterion 2 stays open: no candidate dictionary was listed and no licence was checked, and the first locales are not set by the spec. Criterion 3 stays open: no size was measured, so no cost is stated. If the recommendation is "no fallback", spelling stays port-only, and the open question about the context menu without a port is settled by the owner.

### UX-044 Decision: how an assistant's agent identity is established and capped

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| decision | 2 | S | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §6.2; 04 (origin is a client claim); 05 §3.1, §5.3; 07 §4, §5.2; 08 §2, §7 · **Requirements:** R14, R10 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want the way an assistant acts for a user fixed in a decision record, so that "capped at `suggest`" is enforced by the server and not claimed by the client. Time box: 2 days.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the spec says commits carry `origin: "assistant"` and `author.agent` and that the agent is capped at `suggest`, when the decision is written, then it states how the server learns that a commit is the assistant's, given that `origin` is only the client's claim.
- [x] Given 05 section 3.1 has no agent claim, when the decision is written, then it states how an agent identity is issued, by whom, and where `author.agent` comes from.
- [x] Given a user who is an editor, when the decision is written, then it adds the cap to the computation of effective capabilities (07 section 4), so that the agent's capabilities are at most `suggest` whatever the user's.
- [x] Given an assistant implemented in the browser, in the host backend with a service credential and `DEP-Actor`, or in a third service, when the decision is written, then it states how each case obtains the agent identity.
- [x] Given the decision is accepted, when it is recorded, then it is an ADR and 05 and 07 are amended before UX-046 starts.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18):** criterion 5 ticked. Owner decisions 172 and 119 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md) accept ADR 0234 part A; the acceptance is recorded as A7 in docs/adr/0234-lane-UX-040-052.md. The A6 amendments are made: docs/spec/05-protocol.md section 3.2.1 (agent tokens, `POST /agents`, `DEP-Agent`), docs/spec/07-permissions.md section 4 (the agent cap), docs/spec/13-experience-extensibility.md section 6.2 (the P4 sentence). Checked, Node.js: packages/protocol/test/prose.test.ts, packages/protocol/test/his-038-sub-docs.test.ts and packages/tck/test/pro-041-cors.test.ts (3 files, 24 tests passed; `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <files>`), which compare 05 with the protocol definition. No code and no new test: a decision.

**Evidence:** `docs/adr/0234-lane-UX-040-052.md`, Part A (proposed, lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13). Criteria 1 to 4 are the content of that record: A1 (the server learns from the verified agent token, not the client's `origin`), A2 (where `author.agent` comes from), A3 (the cap in 07 section 4 as an intersection with `{suggest}`), A4 (each assistant case). Not claimed: criterion 5, since the record is not accepted and 05 and 07 are not amended (A6). No test: a decision, with no code.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): all five criteria ticked. Not decided here: A5 (who may register an agent, retention of attribution) was not put to the owner, so the spec defaults in A5 stand and are marked as such in A7. The README definition of done asks for a security review of this decision; none was done in this lane. To keep 05 equal to the protocol definition, `POST /agents` is named in 05 section 3.2.1 only and is not in the route table of 6.2, and `DEP-Agent` is not in the CORS header list of 6.1; both join those lists when UX-046 builds them. Round 2 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 stays open. It needs the owner to accept 0234 Part A and the amendment of 05 and 07; the owner question is in ADR 0327 (lane V-UX-031-045) Part C. Round 1 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1 to 4 ticked on the proposed record only; the owner has not accepted it, so the security review and criterion 5 are open. Open for the owner (A5): registration rules and retention of agent attribution. Gap found: 13 section 6.2 relies on P4 for the cap, but P4 applies where a user holds `suggest` without `edit`; nothing derives a cap for an agent acting for an editor. Security review required (README definition of done). Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M23 confirms the gap and recommends a separate agent token with an actor claim in the manner of RFC 8693, whose capabilities the room caps; until then 13 §6.2 should not claim server enforcement ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

### UX-045 Assistant pane

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | should | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §3, §6.2, §12; 05 §8.3 · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-019, UX-044, PRO-091, PRO-092

As an author, I want to ask an assistant to draft, rewrite, summarise or answer questions about my document, so that I get help without leaving the editor.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an assistant port, when the author opens the assistant pane, then they can ask for a draft, a rewrite of the selection, a summary, or an answer to a question about the document, and the response is shown in the pane.
- [x] Given a response, when it is shown, then nothing in the document changes until the author approves an insertion (UX-046).
- [x] Given a running request, when the author cancels it, then it stops and nothing is applied.
- [x] Given the port returns an error (`rate_limited`, `quota_exceeded`, or a port failure), when the request ends, then the author sees what happened, nothing is applied, and retry is offered.
- [x] Given no assistant port, when the panes are listed, then the assistant pane is not offered.
- [x] Given a user who has only `view`, when they use the pane, then questions and summaries work and actions that would insert content are disabled with a reason.
- [x] Given a keyboard and a screen reader, when a response arrives, then progress and completion are announced and the response is readable.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18):** all seven criteria ticked. Built: packages/ui/src/panes/AssistantPane.svelte (loaded on demand from shell/panes.ts), packages/ui/src/assistant/controller.ts (drives `assistantStep` with the port's stream and an `AbortController`), the `view.assistant` command (registered only while `core.assistant.available` is true), the `AssistantApi` of packages/ui/src/core-api.ts, and for the element packages/element/src/assistant.ts (the backend's `AssistantPort` as `AssistantApi`; the core builds the request through `assistantRequest`, UX-047). Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <files>`): packages/ui/test/ux-045-assistant-controller.test.ts (9 tests) and packages/element/test/ux-045-assistant-bridge.test.ts (2 tests) passed, with packages/ui/test/assistant-lifecycle.test.ts and panes.test.ts; the whole of packages/ui and packages/element passed (215 files, 1478 tests). Browser, Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, all passed: packages/ui/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts (6 tests; the shell over a scripted fake port; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57271 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts --project=<browser>`) and packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts (2 tests; the real element, engine and shell with a backend that carries a scripted assistant port; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57272 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts --project=<browser>`). Criterion 1: the four requests send the tasks `draft`, `rewrite`, `summarise` and `answer`, and the answer shows in the pane; in the element the request carries the document id and blocks. Criterion 2: the pane has no insertion; the document revision (shell) and the body text (element) are unchanged after an answer; insertion is UX-046's. Criterion 3: Cancel aborts the request at the port (the signal is aborted and a late chunk is ignored) and nothing is held. Criterion 4: `rate_limited`, `quota_exceeded` and a thrown port error each show "Nothing was applied" and Try again, and a retry works. Criterion 5: without a port, or once it is detached, there is no command and no pane (the older packages/ui/e2e/ux-045-no-assistant-port.spec.ts still passes, and the element test shows no pane for a backend without the port). Criterion 6: with only `view`, Ask a question and Summarise work and Draft and Rewrite selection are `aria-disabled` with the reason as their description. Criterion 7: the pane has a `role=status` region whose text is the progress and completion message, the controls work by keyboard (Enter on a focused button), the answer is a named region, and axe finds no violation in the pane. No real screen reader was run; the announcement is checked as the text of the live region, as the other panes' are.

**Evidence (earlier rounds):** No criterion ticked (round 5, lane ui-2, 2026-11-13). packages/ui/src/assistant/request-lifecycle.ts (`assistantStep`, `assistantPaneOffered`, `insertionAllowed`, `assistantAsk`, `retryOffered`, `errorMessageKey`, `announcement`); packages/ui/test/assistant-lifecycle.test.ts (10 tests passed, Node.js, 2026-11-13, after the round 6 merge): a response is held and nothing is inserted until the author approves it (property over event sequences without an approval); a failure ends with nothing held and retry offered, and retry is offered only after a failure (state level and property, criterion 4); a cancel discards the response (state level, criterion 3); no pane without a port (rule, criterion 5); the insertion gate and the request gate for a view-only author, with the reason (rule and property, criterion 6); the message names a live region would announce (input to criterion 7 only).

**Evidence (round 10, lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13):** packages/ui/e2e/ux-045-no-assistant-port.spec.ts (1 test, 3 runs passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=56275 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-045-no-assistant-port.spec.ts`). Criterion 5: with no assistant port, no command id contains `assistant`, `view.assistant` does not run, and no `.de-pane[data-pane="assistant"]` element exists.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): the pane, the controller, the command and the element's bridge are built, so the earlier "not built" notes and the audit untick of criterion 5 are closed by the Evidence above. Decisions made here, not in the spec: the pane offers no insert button until UX-046 exists (criterion 6's "actions that would insert content" are the Draft and Rewrite requests, which `assistantAsk` gates); the pane is offered when `core.assistant.available` is true, which the element sets while the backend's `assistant` port is attached (it has no feature name, G91); the answer is collected and shown when the stream ends, not word by word. Not done: a real assistant provider (none ships, see parity demonstrators), and a run with a real screen reader. The agent token of ADR 0234 A is not used yet: the port is called with the user's own session, and the agent identity belongs to UX-046's insertion. Round 2 (lane V-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): no criterion changed. The pane, its loader and the port calls are still not built (ADR 0327 Part D), so criteria 1 to 4, 6 and 7 stay open. Round 10 (lane U-UX-031-045, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked on the browser test above. Criteria 1 to 4, 6 and 7 stay open: the pane is not built (no loader in `shell/panes.ts`), and the core has no assistant port to call. Assistant code loads on demand (13 section 9). The `view`-only behaviour is inferred. Round 5 (lane ui-2): no criterion ticked. The pane, the port calls, the stop of a running request at the port and the progress and completion announcements (criterion 7) are not built, and criteria 1, 2 and 5 describe what the shell draws. Insertion as suggestions is UX-046, and UX-044 is not accepted. What the author is told about the data that goes to the provider is a host matter (the port is provider-neutral). Round 6 (fixer, 2026-11-13): a duplicate written in round 4 (packages/ui/src/assistant/session.ts and packages/ui/test/ux-045-assistant-session.test.ts, which re-implemented the state machine, the no-port rule and the gate under the same type names) was merged into request-lifecycle.ts and deleted. Its distinct parts (`retryOffered`, `errorMessageKey`, `announcement`, `assistantAsk`) now work on `AssistantState` from request-lifecycle.ts. `assistantAsk` uses the same gate as `insertionAllowed`: a draft or a rewrite needs `edit` or `suggest` (the duplicate asked for `suggest` alone), and the refusal reason is `no-change`. The `assistant.*` message keys are names only; the string catalogue has no entries for them. Not built: the pane itself, the port calls and a live region. `assistantPaneOffered` is not wired into `shell/panes.ts`: the assistant descriptor there has no loader and no feature, so it is not offered at all; announcing the `assistant` feature would change the listing that panes.test.ts checks, which is the shell owner's decision. Audit (2026-11-13): criterion 5 unticked. The e2e test (ux-045-no-assistant-port.spec.ts) passes in all three browsers, but the assistant descriptor in `shell/panes.ts` has no loader, so the pane is never offered with or without a port; the test cannot fail on a port and shows no port-dependent rule, and the criterion needs the pane to be offered when a port exists.

### UX-046 Assistant writes arrive as suggestions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §6.2; 07 §5.2 (P4); 08 §7; 04 (origins) · **Requirements:** R8, R10, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-044, UX-045, UX-037, PRM-014, HIS-009, HIS-015, FE-041, FE-042

As an author, I want an assistant's writing to arrive as suggestions attributed to the assistant, so that nothing a model writes enters my text without a person accepting it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the author approves an assistant result, when it is inserted or replaces text, then it arrives as suggestion marks, never as a direct edit, even when the author is an editor.
- [x] Given the commit made for an assistant action, when it is stored, then it carries `origin: "assistant"` and `author.agent`, and history shows both the person and the model (HIS).
- [x] Given a hand-crafted commit under the agent identity that changes text directly, when the authority checks it, then it is rejected with `permission_denied`.
- [x] Given the target scope grants the user no `suggest` (a read-only region), when the result is applied, then it is rejected, nothing is applied, and the user is told.
- [x] Given a user who has only `suggest`, when the assistant writes for them, then the result obeys P4 and the agent's capabilities never exceed the user's.
- [ ] Given an assistant suggestion, when it is shown in the review pane, then it names the model and the person it acted for, and an editor accepting it is recorded as that editor's action with origin `suggestion.accept`.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18):** criteria 1 and 3 ticked on the owner's decisions 119 and 172 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md: an assistant's direct edit is refused by the server, its output arrives as suggestions only; the agent token is capped by the room at `suggest`). Criterion 3: `computeAccess` in packages/authority/src/permissions/access.ts limits a connection that carries an agent to `view`, `comment` and `suggest` in every scope (`edit`, `fill`, `structure` and `manage` are gone, as in ADR 0234 A3), so P4 applies to everything it submits. packages/authority/test/sec-010-assistant-origin.test.ts: an editor's agent submitting a direct insert, with origin `assistant` or `input`, is refused with `permission_denied` and nothing is stored; the same agent's suggestion is accepted and stored with origin `assistant` and the agent. packages/authority/test/ux-046-agent-cap.test.ts (3 tests): the capability sets of an editor's agent, and a check over every fixture user that an agent never holds a capability its user lacks. The control test of the earlier rounds, which accepted an editor's direct edit under an agent, and the attribution test in room-scenarios.test.ts were changed to match (their writes are now suggestions). Criterion 1: `insertAssistantResult` in packages/engine/src/core.ts puts the result at the selection through `recordAsSuggestion` whatever the edit mode and the role, so it is never a direct edit; the commit carries the origin hint `assistant`. packages/engine/test/ux-046-assistant-insert.test.ts (7 tests, Node.js, against the mock service, which runs the real room): an editor in editing mode gets an `ins` suggestion named for the editor and the baseline stays; replacing a selection gives a `del` and an `ins`; a suggester's result is accepted by the authority; a viewer, a commenter and an editor in a restricted region without a grant are refused with nothing applied or sent and the refusal recorded. The pane's Insert button calls it (packages/ui/src/panes/AssistantPane.svelte); packages/ui/e2e/ux-046-assistant-insert.spec.ts (3 tests, the shell over the fake core) and, in the real element, packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts (the approved answer appears in `suggestionItems()` as one `ins`), all passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit. Commands: `pnpm exec vitest run --project node <files>`; `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57271 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-046-assistant-insert.spec.ts --project=<browser>`; `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57272 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts --project=<browser>`. Criterion 4's earlier evidence stands, and the client side now also refuses before sending (the core's `no-suggest`).

**Evidence (lane V-UX-046-060, round 2, 2026-11-13):** packages/authority/test/ux-046-assistant-commit.test.ts (4 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run packages/authority/test/ux-046-assistant-commit.test.ts`; the in-process room, no container). Criterion 4: a viewer's assistant result in the inherit region is refused with `permission_denied`, the reason is sent to the submitter, and nothing is stored or applied. Criterion 5: a suggester's assistant result is refused as a direct insertion and accepted as an `ins` suggestion that names the suggester, stored with origin `assistant` and the agent; the agent's cap never above the user's is the UX-046 insertion property in packages/ui/test/ux-046-assistant-insertion.test.ts. Criterion 2: the stored commit carries `user`, `origin: 'assistant'` and `agent` (the test above and packages/authority/test/sec-010-assistant-origin.test.ts, control case); the history names the person and the agent for an assistant commit (packages/history/test/attribution.test.ts, 'names the person and the agent on the runs of an assistant commit', passed in the same run). The two are joined by field name only: no test runs the room's commit through the history reader.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 1 and 3 ticked (Evidence above); the open question of ADR 0328 decision 4 is answered by owner decision 119, and the stale "Criteria 1 and 3 stay open" text below is superseded. Criterion 6 stays open, for two reasons. The review pane cannot name the model: a suggestion mark carries `id` and `user` only, and the link from a suggestion to the commit that made it (and so to `agent`) is the suggestion-to-commit index of HIS-042 (owner decision 86), which is not built; and the agent identity itself is not issued yet, because the agent token, `POST /agents` and `DEP-Agent` of 05 section 3.2.1 are specified (UX-044) but not built in the protocol definition or the gateway, so no real connection carries `agent` yet and the pane's insertion goes out on the user's own connection with the origin hint `assistant`, a client claim that the server does not turn into attribution (04 section 2). The authority's cap is therefore tested through the room with a connection that carries `agent`, as the earlier rounds did. The accept half of criterion 6 (`suggestion.accept`, recorded as the editor's action) is UX-036's and is tested there. Security review is still required for this story (README definition of done) and was not done here. Needs the decision UX-044. Lane V-UX-046-060 (round 2, 2026-11-13): criteria 2, 4 and 5 ticked (Evidence above; ADR 0328 lane V, decision 6). Criteria 1, 3 and 6 stay open: criteria 1 and 3 are the decision in docs/adr/0328-lane-U-UX-046-060.md, decision 4 (the control test accepts an agent's direct edit for an editor); criterion 6 needs the review pane, which is not built. Lane U-UX-046-060 (round 2, 2026-11-13): criteria 1 and 3 stay open and need a decision that is not made here. The authority's control test (`packages/authority/test/sec-010-assistant-origin.test.ts`, the control case) accepts the agent identity acting for an editor with a direct insert, which criterion 1 forbids. Recorded in docs/adr/0328-lane-U-UX-046-060.md, decision 4. The rejection criterion is a property test in the `perm-suggest` profile once PRM defines it (07 section 17 leaves block-level P4 rules open). Security review required. Round 4 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): building block only, no criterion ticked. packages/ui/src/assistant/insertion.ts (`assistantInsertion`, `agentCaps`, `assistantReviewLabel`, `assistantOrigin`) and packages/ui/test/ux-046-assistant-insertion.test.ts (4 tests passed, Node.js, with fast-check properties) state that a result is a suggestion for an editor (criterion 1), that no `suggest` refuses it (criterion 4), that the agent holds at most `suggest` and never more than the user (criterion 5, the UX-044 A3 cap as the UI shows it), and the label and origin names (criterion 6). Not built: the commit, the server's check of the agent identity, the authority rejection (criterion 3, the authority package) and the review pane. Criterion 2 (the history of the commit) is not claimed.

### UX-047 Limit the assistant's context to what the user may view

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §6.2 · **Requirements:** R10, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-045

As an author, I want the assistant to see only what I am allowed to view and what is relevant, so that I do not leak parts of the document by asking a question.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a request, when context is assembled, then it is the selection, the surrounding blocks and the outline, plus only what the host attaches through the port.
- [x] Given a scope in which the user has no `view`, when context is assembled, then none of its text is included.
- [x] Given a mock port that records its input, when requests are made, then no token, no other user's profile data and no content outside the three named parts or the host's attachments is present.
- [x] Given a host attaches extra context, when the request is sent, then the attachment is passed through unchanged and marked as supplied by the host.

**Evidence:** Round 7 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2 and 4 ticked on the function-level tests below (`assembleContext` is not yet called by a port, UX-045). Criterion 3 stays open (see Notes). Earlier review (round 3): the criterion 3 test passes sentinels in a cast object to the test's own recording port. packages/ui/src/assistant/context.ts (`assembleContext`); packages/ui/test/assistant-context.test.ts (5 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/assistant-context.test.ts`, 2026-11-13) show at function level: the request has exactly the selection, surrounding, outline, attachments and omitted count (criterion 1); a fast-check property drops the text of every scope the requester cannot view and counts what was dropped (criterion 2); a mock port records the serialised request, which holds no session token, other profile or unnamed field (criterion 3); an attachment comes through unchanged with `source: 'host'` (criterion 4).

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-17, criterion 3):** The request is now built from the live core: `DocumentCore.assistantRequest(task, prompt, attachments)` (packages/engine/src/assistant-context.ts) reads the document, the selection range, the outline and the `view` check, and nothing else. packages/engine/test/ux-047-assistant-request.test.ts (3 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/engine/test/ux-047-assistant-request.test.ts`, 2026-11-17): a mock port that records its input is given the request of a real core that holds sentinel strings in its user, in a peer's name, in the access object, in a region the reader may not view and in a definition; the request's keys are exactly `docId`, `task`, `prompt` and `context`, the context's keys are exactly `selection`, `blocks`, `outline` and `attachments` (the last only when the host attached something), the attachment is passed on unchanged with `source: 'host'`, no sentinel and no unselected, non-adjacent body text appears in the serialised request, and a selection that runs over a scope without `view` carries only the text of the scopes with it and a count of what was left out. The core holds no token (no option of `CoreConfig` or `attach` takes one), so a token cannot be in the request; no token sentinel is tested because there is none to place.

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-17): criterion 3 ticked on the engine's builder and a recording mock port. The assistant pane and the call to a real port are UX-045's (not built), so no request is made from the pane; the pane will call the same builder. Defaults proposed, not from the spec: the surrounding blocks are the one text block before the selection and the one after; a selection over several blocks gives one text joined by line feeds, with `blockId`, `from` and `to` of the first block. The pure function `assembleContext` in packages/ui/src/assistant/context.ts (round 3) has the same rules over a different input shape and is still called by nothing but its test. Round 3 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): the builder is pure and calls no port, because UX-045 (the assistant pane and its port) is not built. The requester's `view` projection is passed in by the caller; the core must supply it. Changed on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that version 1 can hide regions and field values from some readers (SPK-005; ADR 0019, spike S12 SPK-029). Proofing and assistant ports receive only the requester's projection. Round 7 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 2 and 4 ticked at function level. Criterion 3 stays open: it asks for the request a port records, and no port is built (UX-045). The sentinel test shows only that the builder reads named fields.

### UX-048 Prompt-injection controls for the assistant

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 13 §6.2; 10 §3.1; 03 §8 (I4, I9, I11) · **Requirements:** R10, R14 · **Depends on:** UX-045, EXP-035, EXP-042, CDM-029, CDM-038

As an author, I want text in my document to be unable to steer the assistant into acting, so that a hostile comment or field value cannot make the assistant do something I did not ask for.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given document text, comments or field values are included in a request, when the prompt is assembled, then each is delimited and labelled as data in the structure passed to the port, and a corpus of injection strings shows none of them appears outside a data field.
- [x] Given the model output asks for a side effect (inserting text, creating a comment, calling a host action, following a link, loading an image), when it arrives, then nothing happens until the user approves that specific action.
- [x] Given output contains a link that was not in the source, when it is processed, then the link is blocked and shown as plain text with a notice; a link that was in the source must still pass the allowed schemes (`http`, `https`, `mailto`, in-document).
- [x] Given output contains an image that was not in the source, when it is processed, then it is not inserted; only assets already attached to the document can be used, and no address or data URI is inserted.
- [x] Given output contains LaTeX control sequences or math delimiters, when it is applied, then it is inserted as literal text, creates no node whose attributes feed LaTeX, and the export treats it as ordinary escaped text.
- [ ] Given the injection corpus, when the suite runs in CI, then no case causes a side effect without approval.

**Evidence (lane w3, 2026-11-18):** the controls are now on the product path, so criteria 2 and 5 are ticked and criteria 1, 3 and 4 hold in the product and not only in the functions. packages/ui/src/assistant/controller.ts (the pane's controller): a request is built with `buildPrompt` (the API sends the structure to the port beside the plain request; `structure` is an additive optional field of the protocol's `AssistantPort.request`, packages/protocol/src/ports/optional.ts), every answer passes `guardOutput` before it is shown, and the guarded text is held as one `insert-text` proposal in `createActionQueue`; only the author's approval runs its executor, which calls the API's `insert` (suggestions, UX-046). The element's bridge (packages/element/src/assistant.ts) turns the core's request into labelled data parts (selection, blocks, outline, host attachments) and gives the guard the text of that request as the only place a link may come from, and no attached assets (so no image passes; the document's asset list is not given to the bridge). Criterion 1 in the product: packages/ui/e2e/ux-048-assistant-injection.spec.ts, test 1 (the structure the shell hands the port holds the document text only in a `data` part with its source and label, and the instructions and the request field do not contain it or the hostile request text). Criteria 2 to 4 in the product: its test 2 (a hostile answer with a link and a bare address not in the source, a picture from an address, an attached asset picture and an instruction to call a host action: the pane shows text only, with no link, picture or control; the four notices are listed; no request leaves the page; nothing is inserted until Insert, then exactly the guarded text once) and test 3 (a dismissed answer cannot be approved later). Criterion 5: packages/engine/test/ux-046-assistant-insert.test.ts, 'LaTeX in an assistant result is literal text' (the real core inserts seven LaTeX attack strings as one text run with only the `ins` mark, and every child of the paragraph is a text node), with the existing export half packages/export-latex/src/assistant-text.escape.test.ts. In the real element, packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts (hostile answer test): no address is fetched, the suggestion text keeps `\input{/etc/passwd}` literally and the blocked link as text. Runs: Node.js `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/ui packages/element packages/engine packages/protocol packages/backend-mock packages/authority` (597 files, 4178 tests passed); Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, passed: `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57271 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts packages/ui/e2e/ux-048-assistant-injection.spec.ts --project=<browser>` and `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57272 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-045-assistant-pane.spec.ts --project=<browser>`. Two WebKit runs of the ux-045 spec with `--retries=0 --workers=4` timed out on a busy machine and passed with the default retries and two workers; the cause was not investigated.

**Evidence:** Round 7 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 3 and 4 ticked on the function-level tests below (no port receives the structure yet, UX-045). Criteria 2, 5 and 6 stay open (see Notes). Earlier review (round 3): building blocks only; for criterion 5 the test checks only that the guard returns one text segment, and no insertion or export of that text is exercised). packages/ui/src/assistant/prompt.ts (`buildPrompt`, `PROMPT_INSTRUCTIONS`); packages/ui/src/assistant/output-guard.ts (`guardOutput`); packages/ui/test/assistant-injection.test.ts (10 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/assistant-injection.test.ts`, 2026-11-13): a seven-string injection corpus is kept in data parts and absent from the instructions and the request (criterion 1, with a fast-check property); a link absent from the source is shown as text with a `link-blocked` notice, a source link with `https` or `mailto` passes, and a source `javascript:` link is blocked (criterion 3, with a property); an unattached image, a data URI and an address are not inserted, and an attached asset is (criterion 4); LaTeX sequences and delimiters come out as one unchanged text segment (criterion 5, with a property). Export side of criterion 5 (fixer, round 3, 2026-11-13): packages/export-latex/src/assistant-text.escape.test.ts (2 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/assistant-text.escape.test.ts`) puts ten LaTeX attack strings, and a fast-check property of 200 mixed strings, into a paragraph's plain text and converts it: the paragraph is the only block, its text runs in main.tex read back to the literal string, every backslash in them is one of the text emitter's escapes, and no command or maths appears. Criterion 5 stays unticked: the insertion of guarded text into the document ("when it is applied") is UX-045 and UX-046, not built.

**Evidence (lane U-UX-046-060, 2026-11-13, criterion 2):** packages/ui/src/assistant/action-queue.ts; packages/ui/test/ux-048-action-queue.test.ts (7 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/ux-048-action-queue.test.ts`): a proposal runs nothing until its own approval; an approval runs the executor once; a rejected or unknown proposal never runs; a failing executor is reported and not retried; the guarded output for each injection output makes proposals and runs nothing; a fast-check property over mixed proposals, approvals and rejections. The product does not send output to the queue yet (the assistant pane, UX-045, is not built), so the criterion holds for the queue only.

**Evidence (lane V-UX-046-060, round 2, 2026-11-13, criterion 5):** packages/authority/test/ux-046-assistant-commit.test.ts, test 'inserts the strings as plain text nodes that carry only the suggestion mark, nothing LaTeX reads' (passed, Node.js, in-process room): seven LaTeX attack strings arrive as an approved suggestion from a suggester's assistant, are stored and applied, and the paragraph's runs hold the literal string with only the `ins` mark; every child of the paragraph is a text node. The export half is packages/export-latex/src/assistant-text.escape.test.ts (passed in the same run).

**Notes:** Lane w3 (2026-11-18): criteria 2 and 5 ticked; criterion 6 stays open. The corpus tests run in `pnpm test`, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs `pnpm test`, but no hosted CI run of them was seen in this lane, and the corpus still covers one hostile answer in the browsers and the seven-string corpus at function level, not a corpus of answers through the pane. Decisions made here, not in the spec: the answer is shown as plain text (no Markdown rendering), so a link that passes the guard is inserted as `label (address)` text and not as a link mark, and a picture that is an attached asset is left out of the insertion with a notice, because the core inserts text only; the guard accepts a link only if its address occurs in the text of the request (selection, blocks, outline, host attachments), and an empty asset list in the element means no image passes; the model's output has no way to request a comment or a host action (the port streams `text`, `done` and `error` only), so those kinds of the queue are reachable only by code, and the e2e shows an instruction to call one stays text. The security review that the story requires was not done. Round 2 (lane V-UX-046-060, 2026-11-13): criterion 5 ticked. Criterion 6 stays open: the injection corpus runs in the Node suite (packages/ui/test/assistant-injection.test.ts), but the CI workflow that runs it on every change is in `.github/`, which this lane may not edit (needsFromOthers). Round 3 (lane ui-2, 2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 6 stay open. No action queue exists to hold a side effect until approval (UX-045 and the approval flow are not built), and the corpus runs in the Node suite but covers no side effect yet. The link and image rules are the implementation's reading of 13 section 6.2: a markdown target cannot hold a parenthesis or whitespace; a dropped image leaves no text; a blocked link is shown as "label (target)" (ADR 0136, decision 13). The implementation has not had the security review that this story requires. Security review required. The corpus is maintained with the SEC epic's testing plan (SEC-001). Round 7 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1, 3 and 4 ticked at function level. Criterion 5 stays open for its insertion half: the guarded text is not yet inserted into the document (UX-045, UX-046). Its export half is evidenced, but the criterion asks for both. Audit (2026-11-13): criteria 2 and 5 unticked. Criterion 2: `createActionQueue` is imported by no product code and no output parser produces a side-effect request (the test outputs come back as inert text), so nothing "arrives" at the queue and the evidence itself says it holds for the queue only. Criterion 5: the authority test applies a ReplaceStep built by hand in the test, and no code turns guarded output into steps (UX-046 criterion 1, the insertion as suggestion marks, is still open for the same reason), so "when it is applied" is not exercised; the export half stands. Criteria 1, 3 and 4 stay ticked at function level only: `buildPrompt` and `guardOutput` are called by no product code yet.

## Extension model

### UX-049 Spike: the document API offered to extensions and its versioning

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spike | 2 | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 13 §7.1, §13; ADR 0014 (consequences) · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want the extension interface specified before anyone builds on it, so that it is a compatibility promise we can keep. Time box: 1 week.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given the spec says only "a range-based document API with events", when the spike ends, then a written recommendation lists the operations, the events (blocks added, changed and deleted, selection changes, suggestions and comments) and what each carries.
- [x] Given a range held across an edit, when the recommendation is written, then it states whether ranges are mapped or stale writes are refused.
- [x] Given the manifest, when the recommendation is written, then it lists the manifest fields, how an extension declares the API version it needs, the compatibility promise and the deprecation window.
- [x] Given tier 2 (UX-060), when the recommendation is written, then it states how the same API carries the declared read and write levels, so tier 1 code does not need rewriting.
- [x] Given the recommendation is accepted, when it is recorded, then it is an ADR before UX-050 starts.

**Evidence:** `docs/adr/0234-lane-UX-040-052.md`, Part B (proposed, lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13). Criteria 1 to 4 are the content of that record: B1 (operations and events, with what each carries), B2 (ranges mapped for reads and checked for writes), B3 (manifest fields and the `api` version), B5 (tier 2 uses the same API). Criterion 5 (lane w3, 2026-11-17): the owner accepted part B in decision 173 (`docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`), and the acceptance is recorded as B7 of the same ADR (`docs/adr/0234-lane-UX-040-052.md`, status of part B: accepted). The origin hint is in 04 section 2 (`docs/spec/04-sync-engine.md`), in the protocol definition (`packages/protocol/src/definition/common.ts`, regenerated `spec/dep/*` and `src/generated/types.ts`) and in the authority's list (`packages/authority/src/codes.ts`); `packages/protocol` (22 files, 248 tests) and `packages/authority` (148 files, 1065 tests) pass in Node.js (`pnpm exec vitest run --project node <package>`, 2026-11-17). No test of its own: the ADR is the deliverable. UX-050 had already started (its host logic exists); the order in the criterion was not kept, and the acceptance came after it.

**Notes:** Round 1 (lane UX-040-052, 2026-11-13): criteria 1 to 4 ticked on the proposed record. Open for the owner: the origin for extension commits (B1 proposes a new `extension` hint, which changes 04), the deprecation window (B4; no number is set) and acceptance before UX-050 starts. Open issue in 13 section 13. Lane w3 (2026-11-17): decision 173 settles the acceptance and the origin hint (04 changed). Still open, and listed in the report: the length of the deprecation window (the answer gives none; no figure is invented, B7) and B1's `author.extension` field (not named by the decision, so not added).

### UX-050 Register host extensions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | L | must | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 13 §7.1, §7.2, §12; 05 §8.1 (`extensions`) · **Requirements:** R14 · **Depends on:** UX-049, UX-003, UX-019, FE-001, FE-004

As an integrating developer, I want to register extensions through the element's `extensions` property, so that I can add features of my own to the editor.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an array of extensions (manifest and code) is set before or after the document loads, when it is read, then each manifest is validated and the extension activated; an invalid one is rejected individually with a non-fatal `de-error` that names it, and the others still load.
- [x] Given an extension needs an API version the editor does not support, when it is activated, then it is not activated and the reason is reported.
- [x] Given an extension is added or removed from the array at run time, when the array changes, then its contributions appear or disappear without a reload.
- [x] Given tier 1, when documentation describes it, then it says the extension runs in the page with the host's trust.
- [x] Given an extension throws, when the editor handles it, then editing continues and the error is reported through `de-error` with `fatal` false.
- [ ] Given the reference host ships a sample extension with a command, a panel and a custom block, when its automated test runs, then each contribution works.

**Evidence:** Host logic only, no criterion ticked (review of round 3: `ExtensionHost` is not created anywhere in the package and the element's `extensions` property is not wired, so no extension is ever activated or removed in the editor; UX-049, which must be decided first, is open; no contribution exists to appear or disappear until UX-051). packages/ui/src/extensions/host.ts (`ExtensionHost`, `checkManifest`); packages/ui/test/extension-host.test.ts (14 tests passed, Node.js, `pnpm vitest run packages/ui/test/extension-host.test.ts`, 2026-11-13). A bad manifest, a duplicate identifier and a missing `activate` are each reported non-fatal and the others load (criterion 1, host logic only). An unsupported API version is not activated and is reported (criterion 2). A removed extension's tracked contributions are disposed in reverse order, and a re-set array keeps an extension whose `activate` is unchanged (criterion 3, host logic). A throwing activation is reported, its partial contributions are undone and the others load (activation only).

**Evidence (lane V-UX-046-060, criteria 1, 2, 3 and 5):** packages/element/e2e/ux-050-extensions.spec.ts (2 tests passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `DOCEDIT_DEMO_PORT=57281 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/element/playwright.config.ts packages/element/e2e/ux-050-extensions.spec.ts`, `--trace=off`). The element's `extensions` property is set before connect, and the list is applied at mount and by a later change through `doc-editor.ts`, `editor.ts` and the shell's `setExtensions`. A bad identifier is reported as `extension #2 was not loaded: ...`, an unsupported API version and a throwing activation likewise, each as a `de-error` with `fatal: false`; the valid entry loads, and a throwing entry's partial command is removed (criteria 1, 2, 5). A list emptied after mount removes the contribution and a list set again adds it, without a reload (criterion 3). Shell level: packages/ui/e2e/ux-050-extensions.spec.ts (4 tests passed in Chromium, Firefox and WebKit, `DOCEDIT_UI_PORT=57280 pnpm exec playwright test -c packages/ui/playwright.config.ts ux-050-extensions.spec.ts`), with the harness's `setExtensions` and `extensionReports`; typing after a throwing activation still changes the revision. The element test reads the shell's full-layout group tab, because its `exec` answers from the core's commands, not the shell's registry (not changed here).
